# The toolchain, dependency and modernization programme — plan of record (Parts I–III)

**rev 1.1, 2026-10-20** — consolidates plan-rev3.md rev 3.18 (Parts I–II) and modernization-plan.md rev 2.7 (Part III), both frozen at those revs; written at `5aa0f260` on `chore/toolchain-and-deps` (rev 1.0), where no version has moved (what has landed is §0.2 and §3.1) and the cut is at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z (D1 (a)); rev 1.1, at `96de1139`, records the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z) on the two facts rev 1.0 left unsettled — G-base step 8 takes serde-saphyr 2.0, and a before-the-merge sweep commit's freshness gate uses its own instant minus 7 days, read by the lead as binding every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) — and the two tool commits of 2026-10-20, `2c913f99` (the E6 comparators) and `96de1139` (G-base step 8), neither moving a version in the workspace's locks. Nothing is built by this consolidation: every status word below (landed, scheduled, deferred, dropped, superseded, design, taken; not built) is copied from the two sources, never upgraded — save where rev 1.1 names the 2026-10-20 commit that changed it.

**How to read this file.** §0 says where the programme stands and what runs first at the cut; §1 holds the rules every run and every commit follows, with the named gates; §2 is the one table of decisions in force; §3 is the sequence — every entry, landed (§3.1), from the cut through P38 (§3.2) and after P38 (§3.3), each in one template (*Does*, *Gate*, *Needs*, *Place*, *Record*); §4 is verification, §5 the owner's own actions, §6 the files that change, §7 the upkeep after the wave. Every entry and row ends with a provenance pointer — "← plan-rev3 §x.y <id>" or "← modernization-plan §x.y <id>" — into the two frozen files, whose section and line citations stay valid as history; Appendix A says where each of their sections went. A bare "§n" in this file is this file's section; a section of another document is always named with its document (plan-rev3, modernization-plan, `tasks/pending-decisions.md`, …). "Step N" is a step of the merged order of record, modernization-plan §6.3's steps 1–20 (§3.2, §3.3). Where the sources leave a fact unsettled, the text says *Not established in the sources* and names what is missing and who settles it; it is a question for the next reader, not an answer. Inside a provenance pointer (←), a § after a document's name continues that document's citation until another document is named.

## 1. Rules of the programme

### 1.1 No compromise on migrations (owner, 2026-10-12)

- **The rule**, in the owner's words: "don't compromise when migrating to new apis and new functionalities for both rust and tauri and react" (`CLAUDE.md:52` as the plans cite it; `865a2376`). As `CLAUDE.md`'s *Core Principles* state it (overriding *Minimal Impact* for this work): when the programme moves to a new Rust, Tauri or React API or feature, it moves fully — no hold kept for convenience, no "not now" where the new form is available, no shim, partial adoption or older API form beside the new one. A real blocker (a dependency pinning an old major, the seven-day freshness rule DU-23) is named with its way out, never accepted silently. Safety rules and settled product decisions are not compromises and stay. ← `CLAUDE.md` *Core Principles*; plan-rev3 revision log rev 3.9; modernization-plan §5 *Rev 2.0* preamble
- **Its application** is the re-plan of 2026-10-12 (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-12-no-compromise-replan.md`; plan-rev3 rev 3.9, modernization-plan rev 2.0): every decision whose taken answer was a compromise was re-answered (§2 marks each "rev 2.0" / "re-answered"); where a settled product decision conflicts and no form keeps it, the entry is an owner question (all five taken 2026-10-17, §2). ← modernization-plan §5 *Rev 2.0* preamble
- **The scope for Part III** (owner, 2026-10-11): *Simplicity First* is removed for this work; *Minimal Impact* means "touch what the modernization needs and nothing unrelated". Every item is **work** (a commit with sites, effort, risk and a gate; default "do it"), a **DU** (only what still adds an enforcement point, widens a component's job, reverses a row of an accepted design, or changes user-visible behaviour or an on-disk or wire format — the shipped code being the "no change" answer), or **not to do** (the settled rules: memory search stays lexical; the approval hidden-character escape set and its tables; `test-home-guard`; the schema line; the MCP tool-governance axes — modernization-plan §3.5). Volume alone is not a DU trigger. ← modernization-plan *Conventions* (head of file)

### 1.2 Freshness — DU-23 and its gate

- **The instant.** The cut is an instant, written in UTC with the owner's zone (PDT, UTC−7) beside it, never a date. Every version-moving commit takes the newest release **eligible at the cut instant** — crates.io `created_at` or npm `time` at or before the instant minus 7 days — checked by the freshness gate and measured on the day. At D1 (a): instant 2026-10-21T19:53Z, max date **2026-10-14T19:53:00Z** for every gate through P38; every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) runs on its own clock (*The JS gate* and *Before the merge*, below). ← plan-rev3 §4.3 DU-23 rev 3.13 and rev 3.15 paragraphs; the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z); the lead's reading of it, 2026-10-20
- **A toolchain's clock** is the later of its stable channel manifest's last-modified time (`static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-<version>.toml`) and its GitHub release's `published_at`, eligible 7 days after that — the rule for every toolchain move from now on (1.100.0, due 2026-11-12, among them). The freshness gate does not read it, so P03 and P03b record that check by hand in their `Verified:` lines. ← plan-rev3 §4.2 DU-2 rev 3.15 bullet; §5.2 *freshness gate*
- **The cargo gate:** `lockdates.py --gate --max-date <the instant minus 7 days, UTC>` (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/lockdates.py`) exits non-zero for any `(name, version)` the commit adds or moves relative to its parent's lock whose crates.io `created_at` is later than the max date — unless the entry is a recorded DU-23 (c) waiver — fails on a failed lookup, and prints the `--precise` sequence, dependents first. It runs in G-base step 2 and in every commit that moves the lock: P31a, P04, P06–P19b, P25–P30, P41 and later. Built in `dd677009`. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *freshness gate*
- **The JS gate:** `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge = 604800` binds every install but counts from the install, not from the instant; so after every JS lock write — P31a's JS half, P31, P32, P33, and M66, M67 and M69 — `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date <the max date>` runs over every `name@version` of that `bun.lock`, transitive and platform packages included, and fails on any entry published after the max date; its result goes into the commit's `Verified:` line (C5 (a): the cut instant governs every `bun.lock` entry). The max date is 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z through P38; for M66, M67 and M69, which land after P38 and the merge, it is the commit's own instant minus 7 days, UTC, as for every lock-moving commit after P38 (the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19: its "don't need to mark a fixed date" read as covering every lock-moving commit after P38, not the sweep alone). An install may also run under a `minimumReleaseAge` of (install time − the max date) in seconds so that it takes what the check will pass. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *freshness gate* (rev 3.15, rev 3.16); modernization-plan §6 *Freshness and new majors*; the lead's reading, 2026-10-20
- **Waivers** (DU-23 (c)) are to become one machine-read record by widening the built `--waivers FILE` — still one `name@version  reason` a line at `5aa0f260` (`lockdates.py` `read_waivers`, `:230-248`; rev 3.14 cited `:52-54`, `:207-225`) — to four fields: an id, a `package@version` or RUSTSEC id, its reason and the commit that recorded it (A-11, an amendment of the built gate mode, still to make; neither source gives it an entry or a place, and B-23 needs it, §3.2). The amendment changes `read_waivers` and its docstring, the driver's usage text (`gbase.sh:82-83` at `5aa0f260`), the self-test's `x-waived` case and its waivers file (`:1481`, `:1510-1514`; rev 3.14 cited `:79-80`, `:1441`, `:1470-1474`) and the gbase README's `--waivers` lines, where the file's name and format are recorded. A-11 also adds a test of the partial-output path (gate output that ends before its verdict) on a recorded registry fixture, never the network, beside the driver's `--self-test`; the lookup-failure path already has one, the self-test's `x-nolookup` case (`gbase.sh:1516-1520` at `5aa0f260`; rev 3.14 cited `:1476-1480`). P34's advisory ignores are mirrored into the record (B-22 (b)). None is recorded: D2's waiver never triggers. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *freshness gate* (rev 3.14 A-11, rev 3.16); §0 rev 3.15 paragraph
- **A floor is the version taken:** a caret floor a commit writes is the version the lock holds when it lands (e.g. P41 `rustls = { version = "0.23.46", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }`, P10 `sha2 = "0.11.1"`, P12 `keyring-core = "1.0.1"`, P16 `zip = { version = "9.0.3", default-features = false, features = ["deflate"] }` never below 9.0.1, P27a `opener = "0.9"`). ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 5 (C3); modernization-plan §6 *Freshness and new majors*
- **New majors:** a version-moving commit names each new major version of a transitive crate it pulls in and the dependency it comes through (G-07). A new major found at the cut is recorded in DU-23's table and its commit and researched before it lands, or its blocker named with its way out — serde-saphyr 2.0.0 (D4, P30), jsonschema 0.59.0 (eligible from 2026-10-23T20:30:14Z: M82, landing after P38 on its own clock, takes it after its research, and 0.58.6 only with the blocker named and its way out — the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19; rev 1.0's dated record: M82 takes 0.58.6, 0.59.0 the sweep's) and oxlint 1.88.0 (M66 names 1.87.0). ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 5; §5.2; modernization-plan §6 *Freshness and new majors*
- **Before the merge:** a freshness sweep — each release that became eligible after the cut instant gets its own commit with its gate, or a named blocker. **Its clock** (**Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z)**; FRESHNESS-SWEEP): no fixed date — a sweep commit's freshness gate takes as its max date the commit's own instant minus 7 days, UTC (P03b's per-commit clock): `lockdates.py --gate --max-date <the commit's instant − 7 days, UTC>` for a cargo move, `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date <the same>` for a JS move, the result in the commit's `Verified:` line. The same clock binds every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) — Part III's three by the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (*The JS gate*, above); every gate through P38 keeps 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z, and P03b keeps its own clock. ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 5 (MF-2 4); the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (`tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0, rev 3.34)
- **Dependency source at the locked version** (MF-1): a `[D]` citation names the version read; each commit citing a Tauri crate source (M63, M72–M78) re-reads it at the version the lock holds when it lands (tauri-2.12.4, tauri-build-2.7.3, tauri-codegen-2.7.3, tauri-utils-2.10.2) and corrects a moved line in its own commit — M72's and M77's first, since 2.12.4 fixes a panic in the IPC and asset protocol handlers on an empty URI path. ← modernization-plan §6 *Dependency source at the locked version*

### 1.3 macOS first (DU-33)

The owner, 2026-10-12: "no need to test for linux or windows, focus on mac first" — taken as (a): every Linux and Windows verification is deferred, local and CI-only alike — the open local runs, P01a/P40 (the wave's temporary Windows `cargo check`), Q08's Windows and Linux legs and DU-31's native-smoke on those systems — while CI's existing jobs (ubuntu as CI's host; the windows-latest Pester job on every pull request and every push to `main`), P01b and the release scripts' Linux and Windows halves stay as they are. It binds Part III too: M17's `LockFileEx` leg and M18's Windows store difference wait with it, and CI's ubuntu rust job is the one non-macOS build a gate reads (M39, M65). Reopening it (A-23) was answered no on 2026-10-17. ← plan-rev3 §4.4 DU-33; modernization-plan §6 *DU-33*; `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.3 (*DU-33 (a) reopened*)

### 1.4 Safety rules of every run

- **Test hygiene** (rev 2.1 §5.2, standing): a sandboxed `HOME`/`OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE`/`OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR`, `RUSTUP_HOME`/`CARGO_HOME` at their real values, `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never`, output to files, orphaned `sleep 3600` children killed; the seven-item isolation rule of `plan.md` rev 2.1 §5.2 for every manual daemon, CLI or app run. Every test that writes, edits, lists or runs a command does so in a temporary project under its crate's home-store guard; no test reads `~/.openalpaca`. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 lead-in; modernization-plan §6 *Conventions*
- **A sandboxed `HOME` does not keep a test off the machine's secrets:** a test reaches credential discovery only through `DiscoveryHost` (`9ec986ec`), whose `system()` panics in a unit-test build; a new path that shells out to `security`, the keyring or a credential file takes its host the same way. Residue: the guard holds in `openalpaca_llm`'s own unit tests only — a daemon test that booted `initialize_services` on a fresh store would run discovery against the machine (none does). ← plan-rev3 §5.2 (rev 3.12)
- **The scratch root is canonical from the moment it is made:** `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")`, once; every expected path is built from `$T` (on macOS `/tmp` and `/var/folders/…` lead into `/private/`). ← plan-rev3 §5.2 (rev 3.1)
- **Onboarding first on a fresh store:** E4, E5 and plan-rev3 §7.5 begin with one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents and a check that `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone; a refusal made during onboarding is not an attempt. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 (rev 3.1)
- **Every live daemon runs sandboxed from its first launch** — `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb`, `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` under a temp root, a scratch `HOME`. Launchers: **(i)** by hand, `sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <binary>`, stderr to a file; **(ii)** through the CLI, `OPENALPACA_DAEMON_BIN` naming a wrapper that does `exec sandbox-exec -f <profile> <real openalpacad> "$@"`; **(iii)** the app's executable itself under the profile, started directly, never through `open`/Finder. Launcher (iii) cannot carry a GUI check (WebKit's Networking process blocks the webview's loopback connects with `EPERM`, 8 of 8 launches); it is kept for the spawn and the cold daemon-ready figure only; a webview check uses rev 3.8's launcher — the daemon under (i), the app unsandboxed with the same store variables. **The proof, before the first model turn:** from a shell under the same `sandbox-exec`, `127.0.0.1:<port>/v1/health` succeeds and a public host fails; for (iii) also from a `setsid`-detached child; a first chat turn shows a `web_fetch` to a public host refused in the daemon's log. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *Every live daemon runs sandboxed*
- **Every in-app Start is confined** (MF-4): an in-app Stop then Start spawns `<exe dir>/openalpacad` under `setsid` with no profile. So: **(1)** a debug bundle (G-bundle leg A, before Q04): remove the staged sidecar from a copy and put a wrapper named `openalpacad` first on `PATH` — `exec sandbox-exec -f scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb <real daemon> "$@"`; **(2)** from Q04 on and for E5's release bundle: G-ident on the untouched bundle first, then, in a copy used for the GUI legs only, replace `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` with the wrapper beside `openalpacad.real` and hash `.real` — or Start by hand under launcher (i); **(3)** after every in-app Start, prove confinement — `sandbox_check(network-outbound)` on the new pid, a refused public request in its log, a `ps` check that the running pid is the confined one. Before M77 the Markdown-link checks point at loopback URLs. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *Every in-app Start is confined*
- **Credential discovery and the CLI backends are off, and the log shows it.** Before the first launch of every live daemon — every launcher, G-bundle's leg B, `tauri dev` and E5 — the store's `config/llm.toml` carries:

  ```toml
  [credential_discovery]
  claude_code = false
  codex = false

  [cli_backends.claude_code]
  enabled = false

  [cli_backends.codex]
  enabled = false
  ```

  **Pass condition:** the run's `daemon.log` (or launcher (i)'s captured stderr) has no `TokenManager initialized` line (`services/llm.rs:187`) and no `Auto-detected … CLI` or `Registered … CLI backend` line (`router_builder.rs:333`, `:339`, `:350`, `:356`). Residue (F-06): every test router also registers the real CLIs when they are on `PATH`; CLI-backend detection gets a host as discovery did — recorded, not built. Until B-18 (discovery seeded off) lands, this pre-seed stays. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *Credential discovery and the CLI backends are off*
- **Never start a daemon against a copy of the owner's store** (E8): `file_assets.in_project = 1` rows point at real project files; a copied store neither copies nor isolates them. ← plan-rev3 §5.2
- **The owner's own development runs use a temp store until E8 has passed:** a bumped build starts only with `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` and `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR` under a temp root, the pre-seed and the sandbox. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 (rev 3.13, SF-1)
- **Synthetic registered projects for every runtime check** that touches files, Open, Reveal, `send`, a missing or re-pointed project, or a delete — a scratch git repository registered through the test daemon's own API; the CLI runs from a scratch directory outside any repository, or with `--global`. ← plan-rev3 §5.2
- **One canonical daemon build, hashed before Tauri copies it:** `cargo build --release --locked -p openalpacad`, its `shasum -a 256` recorded **before** `tauri build`, the bundled copy and `libexec/openalpacad` compared to it (G-ident). ← plan-rev3 §5.2
- **Cleanup waits for the detached daemon:** stop it, wait for process exit **and** lock release (`daemon_lifecycle`), then delete `$T` and the throwaway app data. ← plan-rev3 §5.2
- **Mind the `managers::mcp` tests' orphans:** after a daemon test run, list `ps -o pid,ppid,lstart,command -A | grep 'sleep 3600'` and kill only those with ppid 1 started inside the run. Since `c7aebb98` the stubs lead their own process groups and record a `sleep 30` their `Drop` kills; `CLAUDE.md`'s gate checks that `git status` is unchanged and no `sleep 30` is left after the workspace run. ← plan-rev3 §5.2; plan-rev3 revision log rev 3.17; `CLAUDE.md` *Gotchas*
- **`CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside every `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, from M63 on** (MF-5): M63 adds `[build] build-dir = "{cargo-cache-home}/build/{workspace-path-hash}"` to the root `.cargo/config.toml`; from then every export or scratch build sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` under the same scratch root (for the shell always the same path, `target/gui`), or strips `build-dir` from the export's config — else it writes a `<cargo home>/build/<path-hash>` nothing removes. ← plan-rev3 §5.2; modernization-plan §6 *Build-dir beside target-dir*
- **Build hygiene** (`CLAUDE.md`): one invocation set (`--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`); only the pinned toolchain builds into the repository's `target/`; `cargo clean` past an owner-chosen threshold, never by a hook, cron or agent rule unasked; the shell in `target/gui`; agent sessions with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`. ← `CLAUDE.md` *Build hygiene*; modernization-plan §5.1 DU-M1…M4

### 1.5 Conventions for every commit

- **Rev 2.1's** (standing): conventional prefixes; a `Verified:` line naming the gate run; the attribution lines; one concern per commit; docs that cite a version or a changed line change in the commit that changes it; the lock produced by cargo in sequence. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 lead-in; modernization-plan §6 *Conventions*
- **Part III's three:** never a blanket `cargo clippy --fix`; **a `Display` string is behaviour** — every `thiserror` commit writes a golden test of each variant's text against the manual `impl` *before* the derive and keeps it; **Part III's source commits land after P38** (exceptions: M04 and M07, which are P36; the three target-directory commits; M63, placed right after Q11). ← modernization-plan §6 *Conventions*
- **One place per commit:** the merged order (§3) gives every commit one place; an entry's "after X" line is its constraint, not a second place (SF-16). ← modernization-plan §6 (rev 2.2)
- **Gate texts name the pinned toolchain** (A-14, SF-8): every gate run invokes a plain `cargo`, which `rust-toolchain.toml` resolves — 1.99.0 from P03, 1.99.1 from P03b — never a `cargo +1.9x.y` literal; a script reads the channel from the tree's own `rust-toolchain.toml`. A `cargo +1.99.0` still written in an entry or gate reads as the pinned toolchain; the literal is not rewritten, and run literally after P03b it would be a second toolchain. **The one exception is the G-base driver**, which runs `cargo "+$TOOLCHAIN"` (`TOOLCHAIN="1.99.0"`, `--toolchain`), writing the channel into its export itself; a run after P03b passes `--toolchain 1.99.1`. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 (rev 3.14, rev 3.15); modernization-plan §6 *The pinned toolchain*
- **Gate counts:** a test-count gate reads "the parent's count, 0 failed". ← modernization-plan §6 (D-14)
- **Status words:** **Required** (lands under every answer), **Recommended** (lands under the answers taken), **Optional** (only M53 may be dropped), **Conditional** (waits for an owner question — none left in Part III since 2026-10-17), **DU-bound** (lands only under the named answer). ← modernization-plan §6 *Conventions*, *Status words*
- **Memory code** (D-13): a commit whose sites reach `memory_files`, `memory_index`, `memory::service` or `memory::learning` — M05, M06, M08, M21–M24, M29, M41, M45–M53 and M64 among them — follows modernization-plan §3.5 item 1's read-first rule and names the reading in its `Verified:` line. ← modernization-plan §6 *Memory code*
- **Shell commits:** M63 and M71–M79 run G-GUI beside G-bundle; G-bundle's leg A under the corrected launcher (MF-4); G-bundle on every shell and CSP commit. ← modernization-plan §6 *Shell commits*, *Conventions*
- **Backing out, in general:** revert in reverse order; to revert a middle commit restore its manifest line and `cargo update -p <crate> --precise <old>`; formats marked **[rollback M]** are safe to roll back. ← plan-rev3 §5.2 *Backing out*

### 1.6 What every no-version entry means, and where it goes

Applies to plan-rev3 §5.4's and §5.5's entries (§3.1, §3.2, §3.3).

- **No version moves.** No `(name, version)` enters or leaves the lock, so the freshness gate finds nothing, DU-23 permits the commit at any time, and the cut waits for none of them. A test-only dev-dependency on a crate the workspace already locks, or a feature on one, changes a manifest and the lock's dependency lists but no version; the body names it.
- **The gate's named test fails first** — written before the fix and shown to fail on the entry's parent (or with the fix reverted), and the `Verified:` line says so. Beside it: G-CI at the parent's count with 0 failed; G-lint with no fmt hunk added and no new clippy warning (0 from P36 on); `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` clean, regenerated in the same commit when a route, CLI verb, config key (§5.5's entries) or public type changes, with `API_MAP.md` and `CLAUDE.md`'s tables; a GUI change runs the four bun gates.
- **The body** names the audit ids it applies, the ZCode location it learned from (`ZCode:<path>:<line>`; a design written fresh, never a code port), and what a person or a model now meets — for a §5.5 entry, the row it builds and the reference it learned from (Claude Code, ZCode or the lead).
- **Tests** follow plan-rev3 §5.2 and `CLAUDE.md`'s *Gotchas*: the crate's home-store guard, `GuardedProject` for files and commands, `KillOnFail` for any spawned process, no test that reads `~/.openalpaca` or the keychain (`DiscoveryHost`).
- **Placement (a) — before the commit that rewrites its code** (by §5 item 10's rewrite where that commit is pre-written in prep); an entry not ready by the cut lands in the wave before the named commit, never as a reason to delay the cut (moot since all nineteen landed).
- **Placement (b) — not in G-base's way.** An entry landing before G-base's run of record must not rewrite a region `step-4-secrets.patch`, `step-6-rmcp.patch` or `step-8-config.patch` edits; one that only shifts lines re-runs the driver's `--dry-run` green; no entry appends to `middleware/skill/tests.rs` before the run. N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3 and N-MCP-4 rewrite patched regions, so they land after the run of record and before P19.
- **Placement (c) — after what it needs:** N-MCP-5 and N-PLUG-5 need P19; N-GUI-1 needs Q07; an entry that touches nothing a later commit rewrites lands after P38, in the merged order.
- **Placement (d) — against Part III** (A-34, A-35, A-38): N-SKILL-1 before M07; N-SKILL-2 before P30 and M36; N-PLUG-1 before M58 (a); N-PLUG-6 before any plugin-surface refactor; N-LEAD-1's `JoinSet` is M40's sibling; N-LEAD-2's permit wait takes M60's `run_until_cancelled` form.

← plan-rev3 §5.4 *What every entry means*, *Placement*; §5.5's lead-in

### 1.7 The named gates

Each is cited by name in every entry; the commands are as the sources write them (a `cargo +1.99.0` reads as the pinned toolchain, §1.5).

| Gate | What it is |
|---|---|
| **G-check** | `cargo +1.99.0 check --locked --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` (`+1.93.0` before P03; the pinned toolchain, `rust-toolchain.toml` — 1.99.0 from P03, 1.99.1 from P03b). `--locked` dropped for the steps that regenerate the lock and restored afterwards. Expect 0 errors; the only warnings through the wave are the `fetch_update` deprecation (from P03) and the `proc-macro-error2` future-incompat note. |
| **G-CI** | What CI runs, locally and on the PR: `cargo build --locked`, `cargo test --locked` (`--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`), `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`; `bun run check`, `test`, `format:check`, `build`; Pester. Locally also `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`. Pass = 0 failed and a clean `--check`; the anchor is the parent's count, 0 failed (the tip's last: 5,283 / 0 / 17 at `38649f7e`). The `managers::mcp` flake counts as the known flake only when the module passes on a rerun. |
| **G-GUI** | By hand (CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri` until Q08): `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui --locked` and `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`, with a `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` of its own, after `bun run build`. Where the real sidecar exists create nothing; in a fresh export create an empty placeholder sidecar and delete it afterwards. Anchors (on the 2.12.3 lock): check 0 warnings, clippy **0** unique triples including `openalpaca_storage`, fmt 0 hunks; both commands run `--locked`, so the lock stays unchanged. Needed after P31a, P04, P06 (a trigger only until Q06 lands), P08, P09 and P10 (once after P10 or at the end of Phase E covers both), Q04–Q07, Q11–Q14, Q17, M72–M79 and at the end; not after P12. From M63 on paired with `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR`. |
| **G-lint** | Clippy unique `(file, line, lint)` triples from `cargo clippy … --message-format=json` (level `warning`, primary span under `crates/` or `apps/`) and `Diff in` hunks from `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, from colour-free logs. Anchors: 78 (1.93.0) → 101 (1.99.0, after P03) → 100 (after P11) → 0 (after P36); **no commit adds a fmt hunk** — compared hunk by hunk against its parent — until M64 formats the tree (0 from then on, gated by M65's `cargo fmt --all -- --check`). P19 formats the lines its rmcp hunks add and P30 its pin test's line. |
| **G-ident** | For any packaged or bundled build (E5, E5b) and E7's pre-wave binaries: `shasum -a 256` of the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` == the hash recorded before `tauri build` of the canonical daemon-only `--release --locked` build == `libexec/openalpacad` (Q02's `.sha256`); and version markers derived from the chosen lock — after the wave `rmcp-<the 3.5.x P19 takes>` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, `3.53.2` ≥ 1; before it `rmcp-0.16.0` ≥ 1 and `3.51.1` ≥ 1 (`LC_ALL=C grep -c -a -F <marker> <binary>`). |
| **G-base** | §4's driver run on the tip at the cut instant (plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a, 6b), compared with `recut-lock-99-final.lock`: equal, or every difference listed — `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.0 → 3.5.1 (DU-25 (v4)), `serde-saphyr`/`granit-parser` 1.3.0 → 2.0.0 at step 8 (`[D4(a)]`; `96de1139`) and each release dated after 2026-10-10 that is **eligible at the cut instant**; a younger one fails the freshness gate. Step 1b checks every Tauri crate exactly and against the instant, the driver starts no earlier than the instant, and the suite verdict fails a crashed test binary — without these the run does not count. |
| **G-bundle** | The one gate that exercises the CSP, the capability, the webview's handlers and the shell's sidecar spawn before E5. Build: `bun run build`, then `bunx tauri build --debug --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` for leg A, and a **release** bundle for leg B (built by `package-macos.sh`'s sequence by hand, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` not exported). **Leg A — the webview** (daemon under launcher (i), the app unsandboxed): no CSP or Trusted Types violation and no "IPC custom protocol failed"; connect, log tail, stop and start (under the confined in-app Start, §1.4), Reveal and the folder picker answer; an ungranted `invoke` refused once an app manifest exists; no duplicate window chrome. **Leg B — the spawn** (launcher (iii), no daemon running): the shell locates and starts its staged daemon, proved from outside (`/v1/health` on `discovery.json`'s port, `ps -o pid,ppid,pgid,command`), and with the staged sidecar removed the refusal sentence; a symlinked-ancestor case expects the staged daemon found and started (D7 (a)). Attached to P31a, Q04, Q07, Q11–Q14, Q17, Q18 and M63, M71–M79, each naming the leg it needs. Every `bunx tauri build` sets `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR`, `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH`, `npm_config_cache` and `npm_config_logs_dir` under the scratch root, and `CI` is `true` or unset (tauri-cli 2.12.3 refuses `CI=1`), and ends with a `find <home dirs> -newermt <start>` written for bash (`tasks/lessons.md:125-129`) — the named cache and log directories only, never `~/.openalpaca`, whose being left alone is proved from the run's own logged environment and commands (`tasks/lessons.md:131-135`) (G-09). A launch leaves WebKit temp directories under `/var/folders/<user>/{T,C}/` named for the throwaway identifier and for the shell's ad-hoc signing hash (`com.apple.WebKit.*+openalpaca_gui-<hash>`, shared with the owner's own builds of the same tree) — accepted, not removed by an agent with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui`; an isolated store, onboarding first. The Tauri CLI's own JS version check runs `npm list`, which exits 1 under Bun's layout and checks nothing, so the pair check stays the only guard. |

← plan-rev3 §5.2 *Named gates*

---

## 2. Decisions in force

Every decision the programme carries, once, with the answer taken. **Taken is not built**: where an answer changes behaviour, the shipped code stays the "no change" answer until the entry in *Applied by* lands. Each decision was taken on 2026-10-11 (DU-1…DU-32, DU-M/D/R/W), 2026-10-12 (DU-33 and the no-compromise re-answers, marked "rev 3.9" in Part I and "rev 2.0" in Part III), 2026-10-17 (D1–D3 in chat; every other row by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (PDT; 2026-10-18T01:56Z): "for the decisions all use recommend by Zcode or Recommend options") or 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z) by the owner's answer "all use you opinion for the open rows, use the best choice, prefer using claude code's design, use Zcode design when claude code's design is not available", read as a rule — Claude Code's design where one exists, else ZCode's, else the lead's recommendation (marked **2026-10-19**). The two facts this plan's rev 1.0 left unsettled were answered by the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z), "those things are single issue, you can directly mark the try to use serde-saphyr 2.x and don't need to mark a fixed date, now begin implementation, with fable do the orchestration and opus do the implementation" — G-base step 8 on serde-saphyr 2.x (the DU-4 / D4 row) and no fixed date for a sweep commit's freshness gate, read by the lead on 2026-10-20 as covering every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) (the DU-23 row), each marked **Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z)**. The full history — options, ZCode's evidence, the *decided by* attributions — stays in `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 9) and the frozen plans; `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0 records each as an *Owner decisions taken* row.

Abbreviations in the *Source* column: **PR3** = `tasks/deps-upgrade/plan-rev3.md` (rev 3.18); **MOD** = `tasks/deps-upgrade/modernization-plan.md` (rev 2.7); **PD** = `tasks/pending-decisions.md` (rev 9); **AFP** = `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0 (rev 3.33). *Applied by* names an entry of §3 (its place in brackets where the merged order gives one), "owner action §5 item n" (n is plan-rev3 §5.1's item number, kept), "landed" with its commit, or "no change".

## 3. The sequence

Every entry of Parts I–III, once, in the order of record: **§3.1** what has landed, in landing order; **§3.2** the cut through P38 — G-base's run of record, then Part I in plan-rev3 §5.3's phase order with the Part II and Part III commits and plan-rev3 §5.4's three MCP fixes placed inside it (merged-order step 2); **§3.3** everything after P38, in modernization-plan §6.3's merged order (steps 2a–20), then the taken work that order does not yet place. "Step N" below is always that merged order's step. Each entry follows one template:

- **Heading.** `#### <ID> — <commit subject> · <Required/Recommended/Conditional/…> · <status>`, the subject in backticks.
- **Does.** What the commit does — exact versions, keys, figures. **Gate.** The named test(s) and commands, verbatim. **Needs.** Prerequisite ids and decisions. **Place.** Its position — its merged-order step. **Record.** ← its source; for a landed entry its commit and one-line deviation or residue.

A dropped, superseded or deferred entry keeps its heading, with the status its source gives it. Grouped Part III series (M19–M38, M45–M52, M53 (a–g), M55 (a–d), M58 (a–j)) are written once as a group, followed by one heading per member that names what the member alone carries and points at the group for the rest.

### 3.1 Landed

Every entry that has landed on `chore/toolchain-and-deps` (cut from `main` at `f253cc26`, 2026-10-11), in the order it landed — the order of its first commit in `git log --reverse f253cc26..96de1139`. A tool or a commit amended later keeps one entry, its later commits listed in its *Record* in order. Status words are the sources'; a figure belongs to the record it is quoted from (the G-base driver's `--dry-run` anchor count reads 37, 43 and 23 at three revisions because the driver and the tip changed). Nothing here moved a `(name, version)` in `Cargo.lock` or `bun.lock`; the branch is local and unpushed as the sources last record it (plan-rev3 rev 3.17: "local, not pushed"), so no GitHub job has run on any of it (P01's *Record*; §5's draft-PR action). Prep commits pinned at `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12` (P10, P12, P15–P18, P17a, P19, P19b, P41, P36 = M04 + M07, M08) are **not** landed — they land at the cut by the rewrite of §3.2.

**Reconciliation — every commit on the branch.** The 84 commits `f253cc26..96de1139` (81 at `5aa0f260`, rev 1.0: 47 and 34): 49 belong to the entries below — `2c913f99` and `96de1139` amend the E6 tools and the G-base driver — and 35 are records (plans, this plan's `7fc775bc` among them, reports, lessons, `CLAUDE.md`, `tasks/pending-decisions.md`, `tasks/api-fix-plan.md`, and `1e633bd7`, a header comment in `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` — the 2026-10-17 review's C-03, per `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25) and are no entry. The gate at the tip `38649f7e` is §0's; each landing's own gates — Phase 0's at `1379b184` (rev 3.6) among them — are in the group notes below.

**Build hygiene (2026-10-11; Part III's Phase 0, before any build — the owner's rule that "nothing builds or tests until the cache behaviour is changed", modernization-plan rev 1.2)**

#### M01 — `docs(claude): build hygiene — one invocation set, one toolchain in target/, cargo clean at a threshold` · Required · landed
- **Does.** Four lines in CLAUDE.md *Build & Development* (after the command block, before the `Toolchain:` line): (1) build, test and clippy with `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui`; `-p <crate>` only to run a filtered test — each distinct package/feature selection costs 0.4–1.7 GiB of `target/` per lock state; (2) no toolchain but the pin builds into the repository's `target/`; a session that tries another toolchain uses a worktree with its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; (3) `cargo clean` when `du -sh target` passes a threshold the owner picks; never a hook, cron or agent rule that deletes `target/` unasked; (4) `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` runs with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` — `target/gui` is a second full tower inside `target/` (≥ 2.73 GiB cold for the workspace alone [M: `attr-C-sizes.tsv`]), so the threshold of line (3) counts it. The numbers from modernization-plan §1 in the commit body.
- **Gate.** `git diff --stat` shows CLAUDE.md only.
- **Needs.** DU-M1 (taken (b) on 2026-10-11, so this sentence and M02's `default-members` change are one commit).
- **Place.** modernization-plan §6.3 step 1 (Part III's Phase 0: "may land before, during or after the wave").
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 Phase 0 M01; rev log 1.2. Landed in `f54dfac1` **together with M02** (2026-10-11). Deviation: DU-M4's one-time `cargo clean` was moot — the owner had already run it. Its action half — DU-M4 (a)'s one `cargo clean` right after P03 lands locally, and the second right after P03b (taken by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17), each with `du -sh target` before and after in the commit's `Verified:` line — is scheduled, §3.2. The cold-rebuild figure M01 first quoted ("144 s, 118 s with incremental off") was modernization-plan §1.3's sum of three commands; plan-rev3 rev 3.8 (Q00) replaced it with the single build's figure: the CI set builds clean in 39.8 s (incremental off) / 44.2 s (on).

#### M02 — `build: default-members = every workspace member but the Tauri shell` · DU-bound (DU-M1 b) · landed
- **Does.** `Cargo.toml:15-26`: the comment rewritten (a bare `cargo build`/`test`/`clippy` now selects what `--workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` selects; the shell builds when named), `apps/openalpacad` and `apps/openalpaca` added to the array.
- **Gate.** `cargo metadata --format-version 1 | jq '.workspace_default_members | length'` = 10; a cold `cargo build` into a fresh `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (a worktree) produces one tower — expect ≈ 2.73 GiB / 7,975 paths and ≈ 52 s on this machine [M: `attr-C-sizes.tsv`]; G-CI unchanged (CI passes the explicit flags).
- **Needs.** DU-M1 (b).
- **Place.** modernization-plan §6.3 step 1.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 Phase 0 M02; rev log 1.2; §8 row `Cargo.toml:15-26`. Landed in `f54dfac1` with M01 (the commit's subject is `build: default-members = every member but the Tauri shell; one invocation set`): `cargo metadata` lists 10 default members, no build run (the owner's rule above).

#### M03 — `chore(claude): CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 for agent sessions` · DU-bound (DU-M2 a-i) · landed
- **Does.** `.claude/settings.json` = `{"env": {"CARGO_INCREMENTAL": "0"}}`; `.gitignore`: line 139 (`.claude/`) removed, line 140 (`.claude/*`) kept, `!.claude/settings.json` added after it (a file under an excluded directory cannot be re-included, so the directory line must go).

**Phase 0 — every commit that moves no version (`3bf78e54..1641ced0`, 2026-10-11; plan-rev3 rev 3.5, modernization-plan rev 1.3)**

*Group note.* Each commit reviewed (three reviewers: 31 findings, 1 high, 11 medium) and its findings applied or ruled on before landing; no version moved; the application's behaviour unchanged (P24 and Q01 extract `cors_layer()` and `health_json()` for their tests and nothing else). The rulings, each the recommendation under the owner's 2026-10-11 delegation, are cited in the entries as "rev 3.5's ruling N". Evidence: `tasks/deps-upgrade/evidence-2026-10-11-p0.tar.gz`. **Phase 0's build gates were run at rev 3.6** (three scratch worktrees at `1379b184`, each gate re-checked by an independent verifier against its logs; evidence `tasks/deps-upgrade/evidence-2026-10-12-gates.tar.gz`): the CI set — `cargo build --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` clean; the suite 5,095 passed / 0 failed / 17 ignored (the new ignored test is P14's nested-array PDF, by design until P15); clippy 78 distinct workspace warnings (104 generated, 26 duplicates); `gen_api_docs.py --check` clean; rustfmt drift 1,585 hunks in 303 files (informational); the four bun gates green (121 files, 1,551 tests); the shell's `check` and `clippy` (`target/gui`, an empty placeholder sidecar) 0 warnings. Owner-side and open at rev 3.5: the push and the three CI jobs, a `package-macos.sh` run in a worktree (Q03's gate — run at rev 3.6 with the owner's authorisation), one Linux packaging run, Pester on Windows (CI), the G-base run, E6 on the real files with the DU-10 count (the consent point first), Q00 (taken at rev 3.8). ← plan-rev3 rev log 3.5, 3.6.

- **Does.** Removes the `src-tauri` `Cargo.lock` cargo never reads (still tracked when written: 5,146 lines, tauri 2.9.5; `cargo update` had left it untouched).

Landed `e182f2ab` (rev 3.5) with the gate's first half only — `bunx tauri info` was not run (it may query registries); run at rev 3.6: it reads `tauri 2.11.5` from the root lock with no `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` — established (its "@tauri-apps/api not installed!" is the CLI's npm fallback misreading Bun's isolated layout, not an absence).

#### G-base driver — `chore(deps): the G-base driver — the re-cut's eight steps, fail-on-first-error, with its patches and lock snapshots` · a deliverable before P01 (§5 item 6a; amended by 6b and later) · landed, amended
- **Does.** `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/` — `gbase.sh` with its patches, locks and README (the subject above is the commit's; §5 item 6a names none). From one canonical scratch root `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")` it exports the base (`git archive <base> | tar -x -C "$T/src"`) and runs **the eight-step sequence, not one diff plus one `cargo update`**: for each step the manifest edits as anchored `perl` substitutions (each anchor asserted to match exactly once), that step's source hunks, `cargo +1.99.0 update` where the step refreshes the lock plus the step's `--precise` list, `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui`, and a lock snapshot compared with its `recut-lock-0N-*.lock`; then the full suite, `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, the four bun gates with the chosen Bun on the chosen JS set, and G-GUI (the empty placeholder sidecar, `dist/` from `bun run build`). It exits non-zero at the first failed command (`set -euo pipefail`, every cargo exit code tested, nothing recorded-and-continued), writes every log under `$T/logs/`, and prints the lock differences it found with the dates of the releases behind them. **As amended by 6b (`95c2aea1`):** the pair is step `1b` (P31a's Rust half: the manifest edits, the targeted update, `--precise 2.12.3` — `TAURI_PRECISE="2.12.4"` since rev 3.15) compared with a derived shipped reference; step 2 is a plain `cargo update`; steps 3–7 compare with the un-held snapshots and no hold filter; step 6 takes rmcp with no `--precise`, its one expected difference the exact move `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.0 → 3.5.1 (`lockdiff.py`'s `name:OLD->NEW` spec); a DU-26 pair check after steps 1b and 2 and against `package.json` in the final phase; `lockdiff.py` exits 2 on anything but a finished comparison; `--with-stepbacks` is refused (DU-23 (b) has no measured pair reference). **As re-anchored at rev 3.15 (D1 (a); C5):** `NOT_BEFORE="2026-10-21T19:53Z"`, `TAURI_PRECISE="2.12.4"`, `DIALOG="2.8.3"`, `JS_API="2.12.4"`, `JS_CLI="2.12.3"`; step 1b's reference re-derived for the 2026-10-14 family and renamed `locks/d1-lock-01b-pair.lock`; seven `JS_PINS` rows — react and react-dom 19.3.1, @tanstack/react-query 5.104.2, dompurify 3.4.17, marked 18.1.1, @testing-library/user-event 14.6.8, vitest 5.0.4; a `--bun-lock` mode in `lockdates.py` for §1.2's `bun.lock` check; the bun gates' scratch `HOME` and `CI=true`. **As amended on 2026-10-20 for D4 (a) (`96de1139`):** step 8 writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` (`gbase.sh:814` at `96de1139`) and `step-8-config.patch` carries P30's 2.0 options; step 8's comparison expects D4 (a)'s two moves beside rmcp's (`SAPHYR_EXPECT`, `:688`).
- **Gate.** At `507f9e8a` (rev 3.5's ruling 7): `--self-test` (8/0), `--dry-run` with the final phase on the Phase 0 tip `1641ced0` (37 anchors, 3 patches, the base lock the measured head's `(name, version)` set) and `--edits-only --steps 8` (`diff -r` against the whole diff: identical). At `95c2aea1`: `bash -n`, `py_compile`, `--self-test` 16/0 with four cases mutation-tested, `--dry-run` with the final phase on the tip (43 anchors, 3 patches), `--edits-only` against an independent control by `diff -r`. At `e4cfd37c`: self-test 17/0 with a new case. At the nineteen pre-cut entries (rev 3.17): each re-ran `--dry-run` green (23 anchors, the three patches applying). At `96de1139` (2026-10-20): `bash -n`; `--dry-run --steps 8` on `7fc775bc` and on `2c913f99`, 23 anchors and 3 patches, exit 0, and with the final phase 43 and 3, exit 0; `--edits-only --steps 8` exit 0; on an export with step 8's edits, cargo 1.93.0 `check -p openalpaca_core --all-targets` 0 errors and 0 warnings, the `deprecated` warning gone; `cargo update -p serde-saphyr` from the 1.3 lock moving exactly serde-saphyr and granit-parser 1.3.0 → 2.0.0, both published 2026-10-13 and eligible; `--self-test` 42 passed, 0 failed on cargo 1.99.0. The verification of `dd677009` and `833bade4` is in `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/README.md`'s status line and revision-log rows of 2026-10-17.
- **Needs.** Nothing to land. Its **run of record** needs the cut instant (D1 (a): at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z) and each of 6b's requirements (1)–(5) recorded as built in the gbase README — §3.2.
- **Place.** Phase 0 (§5 item 6a: "a deliverable before P01"); its run of record is the first thing at the cut, before P42 → P03 (§3.2).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a, 6b; §5.2 (the freshness gate); §8 (the rev 3.15 driver row); rev log 3.5, 3.10, 3.11, 3.12. Commits, in order:

#### Q02 — `build(gui): prepare-sidecar.ts builds through cargo and stages cargo's own artifact; --reuse for development; sha256 and provenance recorded; a GUI-only target dir` · Recommended (DU-30 a) — before P04 under (w-i) · landed
- **Does.** `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts` rewritten as plan-rev3 §7.2's G2 (build provenance, DU-30 (a)): cargo decides freshness and names the artifact (`--message-format=json-render-diagnostics`, the `compiler-artifact` message for `openalpacad`'s bin target); `.sha256` + `.provenance.json` before `tauri build`; `target/gui` by default; the triple from `--target` when given, else `rustc -vV`. `package.json`'s `prepare:sidecar:dev|release` scripts; `src-tauri/.gitignore` gains the `.sha256`/`.provenance.json`. As built (rev 3.5's ruling 1): `--reuse` takes no path (it keeps what this script staged, checked against its `.sha256`); the `.sha256` and `.provenance.json` are written for every profile, not only release; the dev switch is `OPENALPACA_SIDECAR_REUSE=1`, read by the script; `target/gui` is a separate commit, `src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml`, the release scripts reading the bundle from `target/gui/release/bundle/`.
- **Gate.** plan-rev3 §7.2's G2 gate (in a temporary worktree, never over the main checkout's staged binary): a one-line source edit in `apps/openalpacad/src/args.rs` changes the staged hash; `touch` of the staged file does not skip cargo and the staged hash equals cargo's artifact; `--release` and debug stage different hashes; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/x` stages `/tmp/x/<profile>/openalpacad`; `--target <host>` stages `…/<triple>/<profile>/openalpacad`; after `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` in the default target dir the staged file is unchanged and the next prepare still resolves cargo's artifact; bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/`.
- **Needs.** DU-30 (a).
- **Place.** plan-rev3 §7.3; before P04 under (w-i).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q02, §7.2 G2; rev log 3.5, 3.6. Landed `bc0e6963` and, for the target dir, `c5922704` (rev 3.5). The G2 gate run at rev 3.6: every item passes but `tauri dev` (not run) and the triple assertion (documented, not asserted — the script cannot see tauri's later `--target`); a provenance digest is comparable only at the same target dir and `--target` choice; `--reuse` and `--dry-run` still run `rustc -vV` for the triple. **Its one defect, fixed in `f1107db1`** (`fix(gui): stamp the staged sidecar so the shell's copy follows every stage; correct the target-dir docs`, rev 3.6): the stage kept cargo's artifact mtime (Bun's `copyFileSync` clones on APFS), and tauri-build re-copies the sidecar only when the staged file is newer than its build script's last run, so a dev stage after a release stage left the shell's `target/gui/debug/openalpacad` on the release daemon — gone with the stage stamped at copy time. Kept as a hand rule: a root `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` without `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui` puts the staged bytes over cargo's dev daemon in `target/`. The triple half was closed by Q15 (below). Back-out: revert; E5 falls back to the hand sequence.

#### Q15 — `build(gui): the sidecar hook reads TAURI_ENV_TARGET_TRIPLE and TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` · Recommended (rev 3.9) · landed
- **Does.** `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts` reads the triple Tauri's CLI hands its hooks and `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` for the profile; one hook script for dev and build (`tauri.conf.json:7-9` no longer hard-codes the release profile); explicit flags kept for the release scripts. Closes rev 3.6's "the triple … documented, not asserted".
- **Gate.** The G2 gate re-run; `tauri build --debug` stages a debug daemon; `--target <host>` asserts equality.
- **Needs.** None recorded (it amends the script Q02 wrote).
- **Place.** Any time (no version), before E5.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q15; rev log 3.10. Landed `fe9ea06f` (rev 3.10), after review. As built: a run is Tauri's hook only when `TAURI_ENV_TARGET_TRIPLE` and `TAURI_ENV_PLATFORM_VERSION` are both set (a partial set is refused); `rustc -vV` runs only to compare with the host, and **a non-host triple is passed to cargo as `--target`** (built, not refused); a `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` other than `true` is refused; **`--reuse` refuses a stage whose provenance profile or triple differs from this run's**; `package.json`'s `prepare:sidecar:dev` is `prepare:sidecar` (`:release` kept for by-hand runs); the Linux and Windows scripts' comment on the hook corrected (comments only, DU-33). Gate run: the G2 items, a real `tauri build --debug` (bundled == staged `.sha256` == cargo's debug artifact), a release hook build on the previous tree, the dry-run matrix. Not run: a real cross build (no target installed), `tauri dev`, the owner's packaging run. Residue: a `TAURI_ENV_DEBUG` leaked into a real `tauri build` cannot be told apart from the environment — the release scripts' by-hand first call, the bundle-vs-recorded-sha check and the provenance's `profile_from` are the protections.

P19's prep `88394660` is rewritten over it at the cut.

#### N-MCP-2 — `fix(mcp): a streamed MCP body is bounded by idleness, never by a total deadline; a refused redirect is a typed, non-retriable failure` · Required · landed
- **Does.** The streamable-HTTP client (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/http.rs:96-98`, built with `.timeout(self.request_timeout)`, 30 s — a total deadline that cut every SSE tool answer, the legacy GET stream, P19b's `subscriptions/listen` and M15's progress window at 30 s) gets a `connect_timeout` and an idle `read_timeout` only, as the LLM clients from `providers::build_http_client` do; the per-request deadline stays `McpClient`'s. A redirect the client refuses becomes its own non-retriable `McpError` carrying the target origin; bring-up reads `ConfigInvalid` ("server redirected to <origin>; set `url` to the canonical endpoint") and a call never enters the reconnect ladder. **D6 (a) with its third form (2) rides here:** the redirect policy refuses a hop to another origin (scheme, host and port) for every server, and any hop at all for a server with `HttpAuth` or `extra_headers` configured; an unauthenticated server follows same-origin hops up to reqwest's ten. `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s MCP paragraph names both changes (a server whose URL relies on a redirect — http→https included, or any redirect when credentials are configured — stops connecting until its `url` is the canonical endpoint). The tests' loopback HTTP stub adds `axum` (already locked) as a dev-dependency of `openalpaca_mcp` and tokio's `net` feature there.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: an SSE stub that streams one tool answer for 45 s completes; a stub that holds a GET stream open past 30 s keeps it open; a loopback redirect to a second port yields the typed error, the row reads `ConfigInvalid` naming the origin, and the reconnect counter does not move; the second port's handler sees no request carrying the `HttpAuth` header or an `extra_headers` entry; a credentialed server redirected to another path on its own origin is refused the same way; an unauthenticated server redirected on its own origin connects. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp`. The driver's `--dry-run` on the tree with this commit (the `error.rs` edit sits below `step-6-rmcp.patch`'s two `error.rs` hunks, `@@ -117`, `@@ -132`).
- **Needs.** D6 (a), third form (2) (taken by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17).
- **Place.** Before the cut: `http.rs` is in no G-base patch and no prep delta; P19's HTTP round trip (A-04) and P19b's listen stream (A-05) need it first. modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-MCP-2; §5.3 Phase G (the paragraph after P23, SF-6). Landed `48f2b9a6` (rev 3.17), `McpError::RedirectRefused { origin }`. Deviations: the idle bound is the longer of five minutes and the configured request timeout (so a silent GET or listen stream is not cut every 30 s); the gate's 45 s and 30 s cases run at a test-only 500 ms bound; `futures-util` joins the dev-dependencies; a mid-session refusal leaves the row as it is; the streamed-answer test builds its parameters from the wire form, so that G-base's step 6 compiles it against rmcp 3.5. Residue: `ConfigInvalid`'s shared sentence ("could not be parsed … Repair") reads wrong for a refused redirect and needs a plan entry; an in-session re-handshake waits up to the idle bound (300 s or more), not 30 s, until N-MCP-1 gives it one deadline (§3.2); the connect bound is checked on the builder only.

#### N-LEAD-1 — `fix(core): a lead's run cancels the subagents it leaves running` · Required · landed
- **Does.** A lead that exited on MaxRounds, CostExceeded or the 600 s wait cap left its detached subagents (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:651`, `:661`) calling the model outside every cap, writing project files and raising approval cards, out of `/cancel`'s reach (`run_lead_agent` cancelled nothing, `runner/lead_agent/mod.rs:725-764`; the dispatcher's `remove_cancellation_token`, `orchestrator/dispatcher/lead_agent.rs:1068`, deletes without cancelling). Now: when the loop returns, the run's subagents are cancelled before `lead_agent.rs:1068` (the token first — R45's process-group kill rides on it); a bounded wait for their spans to close as Cancelled ("the run ended before this subagent finished"); the handles held in a `JoinSet`, so a dropped run future aborts them (M40's reason); `docs/agent-loop.md`'s lead contract says so.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a lead that exits on MaxRounds while a subagent's stub model call is pending — the subagent's span closes as Cancelled within the bound, its stub sees no further call, and the completion record lists it; a subagent running a `sleep` command under `KillOnFail` is killed with its group when the lead exits; dropping the run future aborts the subagents. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::lead_agent orchestrator::dispatcher`.
- **Needs.** None. B-15 taken as (1): no completion guard, this entry's cancel only.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`lead_agent/tools.rs` is in P36's prep); first in the lead set, because N-LEAD-2 bounds a queued subagent by this cancel. modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-1; B table B-15. Landed `f6c11897` (rev 3.17). Deviation: the cancel is a per-run subagents token, a child of the run's token — not `ctx.cancel_task` nor per-entry tracker tokens, so the run itself does not read as cancelled and a lane tells a `/cancel` from the run's end — then a 5 s bounded wait, an abort of the `JoinSet`, and one `SubagentLane` guard closing each lane once; the "completion record", defined nowhere, is read as the run's completion report and its summary, which end with a runtime line naming the stopped lanes. Residue: a task stuck past the abort's 1 s grace closes its lane when the runtime drops it; a plugin agent aborted mid-RPC is sent no `executor.stop` (Z4-12); B-13 will need the per-entry token this commit did not add — B-13 is now schedulable after P38 (§3.3). B-15 (1) is this commit (`f6c11897`'s body); its guard (2) stays conditional.

#### N-LEAD-2 — `fix(core): a subagent spawn queues under the lead's bound` · Required · landed
- **Does.** `spawn_subagent` waited up to 30 s for a semaphore permit before it registered, then failed (`tools.rs:235-249`), while the stock limit is 6 (`config/daemon.toml:10`) and `spawn_subagents_batch` accepts 8 — so entries 7 and 8 each blocked 30 s and failed, against the prompt's "Spawning is always immediate … queued" (`runner/lead_agent/prompt.rs:130-132`). Now: the run is registered as Queued and its run id returned at once; the permit is acquired inside the spawned task with `child_token.run_until_cancelled(sem.acquire_owned())` (M60's form), and the run is set Running when it arrives. The cap does not change.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a batch of 8 under a limit of 6 returns 8 run ids at once, two of them Queued, each Running when a slot frees, with no 30 s wait in the call; a queued subagent cancelled by N-LEAD-1's run end never starts. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent`.
- **Needs.** N-LEAD-1.
- **Place.** Before the cut, after N-LEAD-1; M60 later moves the remaining waits to the same form. modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-2. Landed `7461ab0b` (rev 3.17). No deviation; the reply says whether the subagent starts now or is queued, and a queued lane reads `running` in the timeline (the span schema has no queued state). Residue (for the owner): with the 30 s wait gone nothing bounds the subagents one run may queue; the router's default per-key concurrency (5) is below the stock bound (6), so on a one-key install the sixth subagent reads `running` while its model call waits in the router; a comment in `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/routing/router/capacity.rs` is stale. *Rev 3.18:* the bound is **N-LEAD-6** and the per-key default and the comment **N-LLM-1**, both taken 2026-10-19 (§3.3; ← plan-rev3 §5.5).

#### N-LEAD-3 — `fix(core): each subagent result reaches the lead once` · Required · landed
- **Does.** `summary()` listed every subagent the run ever spawned on every call and sized each answer by `answer_budget(inline_bytes, map.len())` (`runner/lead_agent/tracker.rs:47-52`, `:191-233`), so a second wait re-delivered batch 1 at a shrinking budget. Now: a reported flag per entry; `wait_for_subagents` returns head and tail only for entries not yet reported, shares the byte budget among those, and adds one line naming the run ids already reported; `check_subagent_status` keeps whole re-reads; the tool's description changes; the body names the Stage 0 F2 sizing it refines.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: after two batches of 8, the second wait returns only batch 2's answers, at the 8-entry budget, and one line naming batch 1's ids; a later `check_subagent_status` on a batch-1 id returns its whole answer. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::lead_agent`.
- **Needs.** N-LEAD-1 (any order within the lead set after it).
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tracker.rs`, and `tools.rs`, which P36 edits). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-3. Landed `db7e3825` (rev 3.17). Beyond the letter: `check_subagent_status` also marks an answer reported, but only one that reached the lead whole (within the inline bound, as a stub the run's log lets `read_result` page, or a failure's head and tail) — a review catch; the line of earlier ids is bounded to 512 bytes; the lead prompt's `<tools>` line and its golden change with it.

#### N-LEAD-4 — `fix(core): a resumed run rebuilds its subagents from the session log` · Required · landed
- **Does.** On resume the tracker was built fresh, so `check_subagent_status` answered "Unknown subagent_run_id" (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:1155`) and `wait_for_subagents` "No subagents have been spawned yet." (`tracker.rs:193-195`); span rows keep only 200 characters of an answer. Now: each subagent's whole final answer is spilled under the session's `sessions/<id>/results/`; on resume the tracker is rebuilt from `SubagentOpen` and `SubagentClose` — a closed span becomes Completed or Failed with its kept answer, an open span Failed, "interrupted by the daemon's stop"; the resume note names those subagents.
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a run with one finished and one running subagent is stopped and resumed; then `check_subagent_status` returns the finished one's whole answer and the running one as Failed (interrupted), `wait_for_subagents` reports both, and the resume note names them. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::lead_agent session_log`.
- **Needs.** N-LEAD-1.
- **Place.** Before the cut (P36 edits `lead_agent/tools.rs` and `session_log/record.rs`). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-4. Landed `b417eea1` (rev 3.17). Deviation: the entry's premise — "Resume replays the lead's own rounds" — was false: `rebuild` filtered by task id only, so a subagent's rounds, results and compactions replayed as the lead's; fixed here — a record is the lead's only under the lead's span (`runner::span::lead_span_id`, `lead::<task id>`; a record with no span stays the lead's). Each subagent's whole answer is spilled beside its `subagent_close` record (`session_log/record.rs` untouched); an open lane is not closed in the log at resume; the resume `<system_note>` holds the daemon's words only — a fixed word per lane, each run id escaped, at most twenty named — after a review caught a plugin's uncapped failure text written into it.

#### N-LEAD-5 — `fix(core): a subagent's failure names its cause and whether a retry can help` · Required · landed
- **Does.** A failed subagent read `Agent '<id>' failed: <LoopFinishReason Debug>.` (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:952-959`) while the prompt told the lead to retry failures. Now the failure is rendered by a lead-facing reason function sharing `LoopResult::no_answer_line`'s wording (`runner/agentic_loop/config.rs:366`), and says when a retry cannot help: the cost cap, an auth failure, no routable model.

- **Place.** Before the cut (P36 edits `tools.rs`). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-LEAD-5. Landed `6423a084` (rev 3.17). Beyond the letter, so that the auth case is reachable, it touches `openalpaca_llm`: `LlmRouterError::AllKeysRefused` (every attempt refused credentials; its message names Settings → Models & keys and `openalpaca llm keys add`; not retried; the fallback ladder still walks), the cost line reads the spend the cap was checked against (`LoopResult::cap_spend`), and the lead prompt's step 5 retries unless the failure says a retry will not help. Residue: under `PrimaryFallback` an auth error puts no key into cooldown, so the fallback key may never be tried before `AllKeysRefused` (as before); a plugin agent's failure text and the dispatcher's `Lead agent failed: {:?}` are unchanged.

#### N-TOOL-1 — `fix(core): the shell's stated timeout and hint come from the enforced one; chaining wording fixed; the main loop reads max_tool_runtime_secs` · Required · landed
- **Does.** Before: the main loop's tool timeout was `LoopConfig::default()`'s 60 s and `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs` was not read on that path; `shell_execute` told the model "Timeout: 300 seconds"; its description allowed chaining while its parameter said "single command only, no chaining"; the recovery hint said "increase timeout", a parameter that does not exist. Now: the stated timeout and the hint are generated from the timeout the sandbox enforces for the calling loop; the description and the parameter say what the sanitizer admits; the main loop reads `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs`. R45's kill at the timeout is unchanged.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: the main loop's `shell_execute` definition states the timeout the sandbox will enforce (60 s by default), and a changed `max_tool_runtime_secs` changes both the enforced cut and the stated figure; a timed-out command's hint names no `timeout` parameter; the description and the parameter agree on chaining. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- tools::builtins runner::agentic_loop orchestrator::query_handler`.
- **Needs.** None (B-11's cap value was to be chosen after it).
- **Place.** Before the cut (`simple_query_handler.rs` is in P36's prep). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-TOOL-1. Landed `f35eb584` (rev 3.17): every loop writes the figure its policy enforces into `shell_execute`'s definition (`builtins::state_enforced_timeout`, capped at the shell's own 300 s); the main loop reads `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs` per turn — 60 s before and after; the description and the parameter say what `check_string_safety` admits (`;`, `&&`, `||`, pipes and redirections; no command substitution or line break). Noticed, not changed: the lead's and the subagents' cut is their template's `timeout_seconds`, else 60 s, and the skill tiers' `LoopConfig::default()`'s 60 s — B-11's, *rev 3.18:* reopened and taken 2026-10-19 as **N-TOOL-1b** (§3.3; ← plan-rev3 §5.5). One `pub(crate) use` line shifts `step-6-rmcp.patch`'s `tools/builtins/mod.rs` hunk by one line.

#### N-TOOL-4 — `fix(llm,core): tool errors carry is_error; hints move into the tools` · Required · landed
- **Does.** The Anthropic `tool_result` was sent without `is_error` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/providers/anthropic/request.rs:153-161`); success was inferred from a `[tool_error]` text prefix (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1183`) and hints chosen by tool name and English substrings (`tool_helpers.rs:67-90`). - **Place.** Before the cut (`anthropic/request.rs` is in P36's prep), after N-TOOL-1. modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-TOOL-4. Landed `593e3e96` (rev 3.17): `ChatMessage` gains `is_error` (omitted when false) and the Anthropic `tool_result` carries it, a replay restoring the flag from the record's `ok`. Deviations: the `[tool_error]` mark stays on every wire's text, Anthropic's included, so the gate's "a failure from the flag when the text lacks the mark" cannot arise and the tests pin the inverse; a command cut at the sandbox's limit is hinted by the sandbox (`tools::platform::COMMAND_TIMED_OUT_HINT`, `ToolRegistry::is_command_tool`); `docs/api` is unchanged.

#### N-TOOL-7 — `fix(core): a command's spill stub previews head and tail` · Required · landed
- **Does.** `spill_stub` carried the first 2 KB and a `result_ref` (`session_log/record.rs:185-190`). For `shell_execute` and command tools the stub now previews head and tail within the Err path's budget, so a long build's failing tail is in the stub. `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §5.4's stub text (*Spill, don't truncate*) and its pinned tests change in the same commit, with that plan's rev line and log, and so does CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* bullet on oversized tool results.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a spilled `shell_execute` result whose last line is unique has that line in its stub; a spilled result of a tool that is not a command keeps today's head-only stub; the pinned stub-text test is updated to the new form. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- session_log runner::agentic_loop`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`session_log/record.rs` is in P36's prep). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-TOOL-7. Landed `f6cf46c0` (rev 3.17): a command's spilled result gets `session_log::command_spill_stub` (head and tail at `tool_result_inline_bytes`, the `Err` budget); its record carries `stub: {form, budget}`, so a replay rebuilds it from `results/` after checking the record's bytes and SHA-256, and a record without the marker stays head-only; `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §5.4's stub text moved with it, to that plan's rev 3.31. Residue, older than this entry: an over-threshold `Err` replays as the head-only stub although the model saw its head and tail inline.

#### N-TOOL-2 — `fix(core): validate and gate a call before its confirmation card` · Required · landed
- **Does.** The sandbox raised the card after `confirmation_preview` (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:353-372`) while the extension gate and `validate_tool_arguments` ran only at dispatch, so a person could approve a call refused after the click. Now a `ToolRegistry` precheck — `validate_tool_arguments` and a non-holding extension-ledger check — runs in the sandbox before the card; dispatch keeps its guarded check and the drain guard. The refusals are the same, only earlier.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a call with an argument the schema refuses raises no card and returns the validation error; a call to a disabled extension's tool raises no card and returns the withheld-capability refusal; a valid call still raises its card. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- security::sandbox tools::registry`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tools/registry/mod.rs` is in P36's prep). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-TOOL-2. Landed `c4dee7e5` (rev 3.17): `ToolRegistry::precheck` and dispatch share one private `admit` (the lookup, the extension gate, the schema check), and the precheck is the first statement of the sandbox's confirmation block — before the approval-cache, auto-approve, unattended and no-broker branches — so an unattended run's invalid call now hears its arguments' problem rather than the missing approver; the tests are new modules, so neither G-base-patched test file is touched.

#### N-TOOL-5 — `fix(llm,core): malformed tool arguments are logged and every issue reported` · Required · landed
- **Does.** Two sites silently replaced unparsable arguments with `{}` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/streaming.rs:93-94`, `providers/openai/response.rs:63`), and `validate_tool_arguments` reported only the first problem and rejected a stringified object. Now: a warning once, with the tool name and the byte length, never the content; a top-level string that parses to an object is accepted; every issue is reported in one result, in OpenAlpaca's own wording (the contract follows ZCode:docs/model-tool-call-validation.md:89-172; its wording is not ported).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a streamed tool call whose arguments are a JSON string holding `{"path":"a"}` is accepted with `path = "a"`; arguments that do not parse produce one warning naming the tool and the length, and no call with `{}`; a call with two schema violations reports both — the stream cases following CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* rule for OpenAI-compatible streams. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_llm --features openai`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib tools::registry`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`tools/registry/mod.rs` is in P36's prep). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-TOOL-5. Landed `ab7a2e65` (rev 3.17): one reader in `openalpaca_llm` serves both sites and the streaming frame decoder; unparsable arguments travel as a stand-in object the registry refuses with the reason, `null` is no arguments, and a JSON string holding an object is decoded at the two provider sites only — a review caught the first form decoding it in the registry after the sanitizer had passed the outer string; the registry decodes nothing. Residue: `crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/provider_bridge.rs:224-226`, in the unwired provider bridge, still turns unparsable arguments into `{}`; `tracing-test` joins `openalpaca_llm`'s dev-dependencies, no version moving. Clippy triples 80 → 79 from this commit on.

- **Does.** `<available_skills>` had a 500-character budget and `break`s at the first line that did not fit (`orchestrator/query_handler/mod.rs:255-278`); its header said the *user* invokes skills; a skill with no slash was listed by a display name `invoke_skill` cannot resolve; the lead's prompt passed `skills_catalog: None`. Now one renderer serves the main loop and the lead, built from the availability-filtered `catalog_summary`: each line gives the id, the `/slash` and the description cut with an ellipsis; over the budget it falls back to names only; the budget lives in `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets]`; the wording names `invoke_skill`; the lead's prompt goldens move.

Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- orchestrator::query_handler runner::lead_agent compose`.
- **Needs.** None.

- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-SKILL-1. Landed `5c9cdbf8` (rev 3.17): one renderer, `render_available_skills`: lines `- <id> (/<slash>): <description>`, the description cut at 100 characters (this commit's choice), the whole list names only past `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets] skills_catalog_budget` (default 500, clamped 100..=20000); the lead's header leaves out the sentence that the user can type a `/slash` (a review fix). Residue (for the owner): the four stock skills use just under 400 of the 500 characters, so a fifth of about 110 tips the list to names only. *Rev 3.18:* taken 2026-10-19 as **N-SKILL-5** (§3.3; ← plan-rev3 §5.5).

#### N-SKILL-2 — `feat(skills): skill diagnostics on the API, CLI and Settings; the level-1 scan bounded` · Required · landed
- **Does.** A SKILL.md that cannot be read or parsed, or conflicts on its slash, was skipped with a log warning only. Now the catalog keeps the last scan's diagnostics `{code, severity, path, skill_id?, message}` with the codes `read_failed`, `missing_frontmatter`, `invalid_yaml`, `missing_field`, `id_mismatch`, `deprecated_field`, `slash_conflict`, `unknown_key` and `too_large`, served on a new `GET /v1/skills/diagnostics` (`GET /v1/skills` stays a bare array, api-fix-plan §7); `openalpaca skills list|info` and a count in Settings → Tools show them; `docs/api`, `API_MAP.md` and CLAUDE.md's route and code-navigation rows change; the boot scan requires a regular file (an owner's own link is still followed) and caps the level-1 read with `too_large`. - **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-SKILL-2. Landed `8c63804f` (rev 3.17): the six new `SkillParseError` variants sit inside `middleware/skill/mod.rs:21-42`, shifting step 8's hunks by 39 lines; the `unknown_key` capture is a serde `flatten` of top-level keys typed with no YAML crate (the first cut's `serde_yaml::Mapping` stopped step 8's check with 5 errors, a review catch), and step 8 simulated on the tree passes under serde-saphyr 1.3.0; the level-1 cap is 256 KiB, this commit's choice. The integration review's fix, folded in: the docs said a broken hot edit leaves the previous version in use, which was false — every invocation re-reads the file — so the level-2 `load_full` reads through the same guarded reader on the blocking pool (`load_full_off_runtime`), no level-2 content is cached (the lead's ruling). Residue: nested unknown keys are not reported, plugin skills' slashes are not compared, the shipped-skill count is pinned at 4 in a test, and for D4 a null top-level key fails under serde-saphyr.

- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-PLUG-2.
