#### N-PLUG-4 — `fix(plugins): an update rolls back a failed swap and recovers at boot` · Required · landed
- **Does.** `update_from_path` trashed the old tree then committed the staged one with no rollback (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:2096-2099`), so a failed commit left the plugin absent and its last good tree only in `.trash/`. Now: the trashed tree is restored when the commit fails, before the error returns; a one-line marker, `.staging/<id>.swap`, is written before the trash move and removed after the commit; a boot reconcile pass that finds the marker restores the trashed tree and sweeps `.staging/` copies older than the process. D1 and the extension design's §1.3 rule 3 (nothing the owner dropped in is deleted) are kept.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a commit step forced to fail leaves the old tree in place and returns the error; a marker left as by a crash between the trash move and the commit is recovered at the next manager start. Command: `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`manager.rs` is in P36's prep). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-PLUG-4. Landed `07f0c90c` (rev 3.17): a failed swap puts the incumbent back with the consent it ran under (`PermissionGate::put_back_consent`); a crash between the two moves is recovered at the next start, the tree only, a decision a capability change dropped before the crash staying dropped; an uninstall or an orphan row's removal drops the swap marker; the boot sweep takes only `.staging/` copies of other processes (a pid rule, not an age rule), recovery never deleting the trashed tree (rule 3).

#### N-PLUG-6 — `fix(plugins,core): honour register_template's answer; unload removes only what the plugin owns` · Required · landed
- **Does.** `register_template` refuses a duplicate agent id by returning `false` and the plugin manager ignored the answer (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:1432-1434`); at T2 entries were removed by id whoever owned them, with a fallback scan by display name — so a plugin answering `agent/info` with `writing_agent` deleted the shipped template at its next disable. Now the return value is honoured, and at T2 an entry is removed only when its source is this plugin (`SkillSource::Plugin { plugin_id }`, or the template's source), never through the name-scan fallback.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a plugin answering `agent/info` with a shipped template's id leaves that template in place after the plugin's disable; a plugin's unload never removes a file skill through the display-name scan. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- agent orchestrator::skill`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`manager.rs` and `skill/catalog/mod.rs` are in P36's prep); before any plugin-surface refactor (A-34). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-PLUG-6. Landed `1a5512dc` (rev 3.17): `publish` records an agent id only when `register_template` registered it, and T2 removes a template or a skill only when its source is this plugin (`remove_plugin_template`, `remove_plugin_entry`, never the display-name scan), a tombstone only for what it removed; the echo fixture answers `agent/info` from `agent-info.json`. Not here: B-05's skip rule — a plugin skill inserted over a file skill's key, and `apps/openalpacad/src/hot_reload.rs:606`'s removal of a template by id — B-05's commit, now schedulable after this anchor (§3.3).

#### N-DAEMON-1 — `fix(daemon): each shutdown stage within the remaining window; discovery removed on forced exit; no follow-up autostart during shutdown` · Required · landed
- **Does.** The connector and wake stages were not bounded by the shutdown window and ran before `remove_discovery`; the force-exit watchdog's `process::exit(1)` left `discovery.json` behind; autostart at a run's end ignored shutdown. Now: the connector and wake stages are bounded by what remains of `FORCE_EXIT_GRACE`; the watchdog's exit path removes `discovery.json` first; autostart is skipped once the daemon's cancel token is set (`AppState.cancel_token` threaded into the dispatcher). CLAUDE.md's daemon-lifecycle residue line is updated.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a connector whose shutdown never returns does not hold the shutdown past the window (the `graceful_shutdown_tests` pattern, with no store); the watchdog's exit path removes the discovery file it is given; a run that ends after the cancel token is set starts no follow-up. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpacad --bin openalpacad -- graceful_shutdown followup`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib orchestrator::dispatcher`.
- **Needs.** None. B-14 (taken: a follow-up a crash left `running` settled `cancelled` with a visible notice, at most once) is a commit after this one.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`main.rs` is in P41's prep `96a1dc30`). modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-DAEMON-1. Landed `08831341` (rev 3.17): `shutdown::within` bounds the connector and wake stages at `shutdown::stage_deadline`, `TAIL_RESERVE` (1 s) before the force-exit instant rather than at it; the watchdog removes `discovery.json` before `process::exit`; `Orchestrator::set_shutdown_token` stops the autostart, the follow-up staying `queued`. Residue: `main.rs`'s `set_shutdown_token` call and the wake stage's `within` call are not pinned by a test (no test boots `async_main`); B-14 can now be scheduled after this anchor (§3.3).

#### N-STORE-1 — `fix(store,daemon,cli): Open copies and editor buffers live under state/, not $TMPDIR` · Required · landed
- **Does.** Every Open of an upload copied it to `$TMPDIR/openalpaca-open/<id>-<name>` and nothing removed it; `openalpaca memory edit`, `memory add` and `persona edit user` used `$TMPDIR` buffers; neither a project's Delete data nor the factory reset could reach them. Now Open copies are staged under `state/cache/open/<file_id>/` (0700, the original filename kept), swept at boot and removed in the project purge by file id; the CLI's editor buffers sit in a 0700 directory under `state/`; CLAUDE.md's data-directory paragraph names both, and the residue in `tasks/storage-design/01-current-layout.md:105` is closed there. Applies D1.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: an Open of an upload under a guarded home store stages the copy under `<store>/state/cache/open/<file_id>/` and leaves a scratch `TMPDIR` empty; the project purge removes the staged copy; a boot sweep removes a stale one; the CLI's edit buffer is created under `state/` at mode 0700. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpacad -- routes::files`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage project_life`; `cargo test -p openalpaca`.
- **Needs.** None.
- **Place.** Before the cut (`files_types.rs` is in P17's prep `75358018`); last of the twenty. modernization-plan §6.3 step 1a.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-STORE-1. Landed `38649f7e` (rev 3.17): the CLI's editor buffers under `state/editor/` (0700, the name this commit chose), which the factory reset leaves and its preview lists; the gate's empty-`TMPDIR` check looks for this file's copy rather than re-pointing `TMPDIR`, which is process-wide; a `$TMPDIR/openalpaca-open/` an earlier build left is not swept. Residue: `state/` and `state/cache/` themselves are followed; only `open/` and below are no-follow. `38649f7e` is the tip whose gate is §0's.

### 3.2 The cut through P38

This section is the sequence from the cut to P38: G-base's run of record, the rewrite of the pre-written prep commits, and every scheduled entry of Part I in plan-rev3 §5.3's phase order, with the Part II and Part III commits and plan-rev3 §5.4's three MCP fixes that the order of record places inside that span. **The order of record is `modernization-plan.md` §6.3 step 2** ("Part I's own commits keep plan-rev3 §5.3's phase order; only the places of Part II's and Part III's commits in it are shown"); plan-rev3 §7.7 keeps no second copy, and `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 copies it. Landed entries inside the span (P05, P14, P24) are §3.1's and appear here as one-line pointers so the sequence reads whole. Nothing is built by this document; every status word is copied from the sources. ← plan-rev3 §5.3, §7.7; modernization-plan §6.3 step 2

**What every entry below means** (plan-rev3 §5.3's preamble (1)–(8), condensed; §1 holds the conventions and the named gates in full).
- **(1) A version is a dated record, (8) except the targets written for the cut.** A version named in an entry is a record of what was measured or eligible when it was written, never a target — except the versions plan-rev3 rev 3.15 wrote into P03, P04, P10, P12, P16, P27a, P30, P31a, P31, P32, P33, P41 and P42, which are the newest releases eligible at 2026-10-21T19:53Z as `tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md` measured them on 2026-10-17 (its §2 and §6): targets, because no release published after the max date (2026-10-14T19:53:00Z) can become eligible at that instant. A yank before the cut can still change one, which the cut-day yank scan and the freshness gate catch; the step-backs a commit takes are those the gate prints on the day. P30's serde-saphyr 2.0.0 joins them (D4 (a)). P03b's 1.99.1 is not one of them (its entry). The report's figures were measured on the prep tip with the prep's code commits, so they are each commit's expectation, not its result.
- **(2) The freshness gate on every lock a commit moves.** P31a, P04, P06–P19b, P25–P30, P41 and any later one run §1.2's freshness gate (`lockdates.py --gate --max-date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z`, built `dd677009`; every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) takes its own instant minus 7 days, §1.2, FRESHNESS-SWEEP) against the parent's lock, record the result in the `Verified:` line, and list in the body each new major version of a transitive crate and the dependency it comes through (G-07). Each JS lock write — P31a's JS half, P31, P32, P33 — is followed by `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z` over every entry, transitive and platform packages included (C5 (a): the cut instant governs every `bun.lock` entry).
- **(3) A caret floor written into a manifest is the version the lock takes**, never a newer one the prep commits wrote; a crate the lock holds at more than one version is spelled `name@locked` in every `cargo update -p` and `cargo tree -i`.
- **(4) `cargo +1.99.0` in a gate means the pinned toolchain** (`rust-toolchain.toml`): 1.99.0 from P03, 1.99.1 from P03b (D3 (a)); the same reading covers `modernization-plan.md`'s gate literals, so P03b rewrites none of them (§1).
- **(5) A gate that reads CI** ("on GitHub", "the PR's ubuntu job", "CI's gui job") needs the draft PR (an owner action, §5; plan-rev3 §5.1 item 9), and its run URL goes into Chunk 25; cargo-audit, cargo-deny, zizmor and shellcheck are §5 item 13's pinned versions in a scratch root.
- **(6) A pre-written prep commit lands by the rewrite procedure below**, not by a rebase; the entry records where the prep body and the entry disagreed (A1-05); the prep's figures were measured on a newer lock than the cut admits and are expectations to re-measure.
- **(7) A commit's G-CI anchor is its parent's count with 0 failed.** At the cut the parent is the tip: its last recorded gate is 5,283 / 0 / 17 at `38649f7e`, clippy 79 triples, fmt 1,579 hunks in 303 files (← plan-rev3 revision log, rev 3.17 row).
- **plan-rev3 §5.4's entries** (N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3, N-MCP-4 here; B-23 is written in the same form) move no version, so the freshness gate finds nothing; the gate's named test is written before the fix and shown to fail on the parent (or with the fix reverted), and the `Verified:` line says so; beside it G-CI at the parent's count with 0 failed, G-lint with no fmt hunk added and no new clippy warning (0 from P36 on), and `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` clean, regenerated in the same commit with `API_MAP.md` and CLAUDE.md's tables when a route, CLI verb or public type changes; the body names the audit ids, the ZCode location learned from, and what a person or a model now meets; tests use the crate's home-store guard, `GuardedProject`, `KillOnFail`, and never the developer's `~/.openalpaca` or keychain (`DiscoveryHost`). ← plan-rev3 §5.4 *What every entry means*

**Owner actions that gate entries here** are cited by plan-rev3 §5.1's item numbers (§5 holds them with their status): item 3 — the DU-10 count and the E6 no-print checks, at the consent point before P04, P12, P29 and P30; item 4 — the whole-store backup immediately before the first start of a bumped build on the real store (until E8 has passed, the owner's own development runs use a temp store); item 9 — push the branch and open the draft PR before P42/P03, and read CI a second time after P12, P13 and P08, before P15; item 12 — the Telegram and Discord test bots before P28 and P41; item 13 — cargo-audit, cargo-deny, zizmor and shellcheck pinned in a scratch root, before the cut; item 14 — the pdf-extract issue before P15; item 15 — the rmcp issue, before P19 if it can; item 16 — the MIT LICENSE, before any beta distribution (P34 does not wait for it). ← plan-rev3 §5.1

**The order at a glance** (← modernization-plan §6.3 step 2; plan-rev3 §5.3, §5.4's placement table, §7.7):

1. **G-base's run of record**, at or after the cut instant 2026-10-21T19:53Z, on the tip, before any version moves.
2. **P42** (Bun 1.4.3) → **P03** (Rust 1.99.0) → DU-M4 (a)'s one `cargo clean` (an action, not a commit) → **P31a** (the Tauri pair) → **Q11** (Part II, its one place) → **M63** (Part III) → **P04** (the lock refresh).
3. Phase C: P05 — landed `afb1da60`, §3.1.
4. Phase D: **P06**, **P07**, **P08**.

**The cut and its first gate**

#### G-base — `(no commit) the run of record at the cut instant` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** Runs the G-base driver, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh` (landed `507f9e8a` with Phase 0; amended without the hold `95c2aea1`; fixes `74b86219`, `f7207917`, `e4cfd37c`; gate mode `dd677009`; the amendment and re-anchoring of 2026-10-17 recorded in its README), on the exact branch point — the commit the wave's first commit will sit on — before P42. From one canonical scratch root `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")` it exports the base (`git archive <base> | tar -x -C "$T/src"`) and runs, in order, the toolchain step, the Tauri pair's Rust half (step 1b, P31a) and the re-cut's steps 2–8 of 2026-10-10 — each step its anchored manifest edits (each anchor asserted to match exactly once), its source hunks, its cargo commands, DU-23's freshness gate on every version the step adds or moves, a `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` into `$T/target` and a lock snapshot compared with the shipped one — then the final phase: the full suite, `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, the four bun gates with the chosen Bun on the chosen JS set (P31a's three `@tauri-apps/*` pins and the P31–P33 pins), and G-GUI (an empty placeholder sidecar, `dist/` from `bun run build`). It refuses to start before the cut instant, exits non-zero at the first failed command (`set -euo pipefail`, nothing recorded-and-continued), writes every log under `$T/logs/`, and prints the lock differences with the dates of the releases behind them. The steps (← the gbase README's *The steps* table):

  | Step | Plan | Manifest edits | Source hunks | Cargo and checks; the snapshot compared with |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 toolchain | P03 | `rust-toolchain.toml` `channel` → the toolchain; `Cargo.toml` `rust-version` → its major.minor | — | `check --locked …`; the lock byte-identical to the base's; `recut-lock-01-toolchain.lock` |
  | 1b the Tauri pair | P31a (Rust half) | `Cargo.toml` `tauri-plugin-dialog = "2"` → `tauri = "~2.12"` / `tauri-build = "~2.7"` / `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=2.8.3"` (`[workspace.dependencies]`); `src-tauri/Cargo.toml` `tauri-build.workspace = true`, `tauri.workspace = true` | — | `update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog`, then `update -p tauri --precise 2.12.4`; the freshness gate; all ten Tauri crates exactly, each eligible; the pair check; `check`; `d1-lock-01b-pair.lock` |
  | 2 refresh | P04 | — | — | `update` (a plain refresh), then the freshness gate; the Tauri set again and the pair check; `check`; `recut-lock-02-update.lock` |
  | 3 storage | P06–P08 | `rusqlite 0.40.2`, `lru 0.18`, `sysinfo 0.39.6` | — | `check`; the freshness gate; `recut-lock-03-storage.lock` |
  | 4 secrets | P09–P12 | `base64 0.23.1`, `sha2 0.11`, `aes-gcm 0.11`, `keyring 4.2` (`v1`) | `step-4-secrets.patch` | `check`; the freshness gate; `recut-lock-04-secrets.lock` |
  | 5 documents | P15, P16, P18 | `infer 0.22`, `pdf-extract 0.12`, `zip 8.6` (deflate only), `calamine 0.36` | — | `check`; the freshness gate; `recut-lock-05-documents.lock` |
  | 6 MCP client | P19 | `crates/openalpaca_mcp/Cargo.toml` `rmcp 3.5.0` (the measured diff's floor) | `step-6-rmcp.patch` | `update -p rmcp`, no `--precise` (DU-25 (v4)); the freshness gate; one `rmcp` and one `rmcp-macros`, the same 3.5.x (3.5.1 at the cut); `check`; `recut-lock-06-rmcp.lock` [DU-25(v4)] |
  | 7 web + CLI | P25–P27 | `tower-http 0.7.1`, `tokio-tungstenite 0.30.0`, `rustyline 18` | — | `check`; the freshness gate; `recut-lock-07-web.lock` [DU-25(v4)] |
  | 8 config | P29, P30 | `toml 1.1`; `serde_yaml` → `serde-saphyr 2.0` (D4 (a), `96de1139`) | `step-8-config.patch` (P30's 2.0 options) | `check`; the freshness gate; `recut-lock-99-final.lock` [DU-25(v4)] [D4(a)], the wave's final lock |

  The driver's edits are the measured diff's final state, not each P commit's text: P10's helper, P12's keyring-core (the driver writes `keyring 4.2`), P16's zip 9 (the driver writes `zip 8.6`), P19's `Auto` lifecycle, process-group wrapper and `3.5.1` floor (the driver writes `3.5.0`) are measured by their own commits' gates. **Step 8's serde-saphyr — Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z)**, the owner: "those things are single issue, you can directly mark the try to use serde-saphyr 2.x and don't need to mark a fixed date, now begin implementation, with fable do the orchestration and opus do the implementation" — step 8 moves to serde-saphyr 2.x: `serde-saphyr = "2.0"`, the caret's floor 2.0.0 the version taken at the cut (§1.2, "a floor is the version taken"), as P30 writes it. (Rev 1.0 left this unsettled: the driver then wrote `"1.3"`, `gbase.sh:806` at `5aa0f260`, and neither plan-rev3 nor the README named a driver change for D4.) Built as the driver amendment `96de1139` (2026-10-20; a tool change, no version moved): step 8 writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` (`gbase.sh:814` at `96de1139`; the gbase README's step table, `:79`, and its rule, `:81`, which now names step 8's serde-saphyr, P30's under D4 (a), beside P10, P12, P16 and P19); `step-8-config.patch`, regenerated on an export of `7fc775bc`, carries P30's 2.0 options — `non_finite_float_policy: serde_saphyr::NonFiniteFloatPolicy::AsString`, `emit_comments: false` (C1) and the corrected doc comment (C10) — but **not P30's C1 pin test**, which P30's own commit adds, so the run of record's suite does not exercise `emit_comments`. **Expected lock differences:** at steps 6, 7 and 8 exactly `rmcp: 3.5.0 -> 3.5.1` and `rmcp-macros: 3.5.0 -> 3.5.1` (`lockdiff.py`'s `name:OLD->NEW` spec, so a later 3.5.x stays unexpected); at step 8 also exactly `serde-saphyr: 1.3.0 -> 2.0.0` and `granit-parser: 1.3.0 -> 2.0.0` [D4(a)] (`SAPHYR_EXPECT`, `gbase.sh:688` at `96de1139`, passed beside rmcp's, `:823`), since the shipped `recut-lock-99-final.lock` (measured at `7295f91b` on 2026-10-10) predates D4 (a) and holds 1.3.0 — both 2.0.0 releases published 2026-10-13, eligible; `--strict`'s usage text and the RESULT line name both classes; a release dated after 2026-10-10 is an expected difference only when it is eligible at the cut instant — a younger one fails the freshness gate. *A dated record* (2026-10-20, a finding independent of D4 (a); §3.2 preamble (1); the gbase README's 2026-10-20 row): annotate-snippets 0.12.17, serde-saphyr's dependency under 1.3 or 2.0, was published 2026-10-19T20:30Z, after the max date, so step 8's freshness gate is expected to name `cargo update -p annotate-snippets@0.12.17 --precise 0.12.16`; under `--apply-step-backs`, which the run of record passes, step 8 takes 0.12.16, the reference's version — a step-back the run records in `logs/step-backs.txt`, not a lock difference. **The values at the instant** (rev 3.15, C2; the gbase README records them as the driver's defaults, "the run of record passes no `--tauri`, `--dialog`, `--js-api`, `--js-cli`, `--tauri-crate` or `--js-pin`"): `NOT_BEFORE="2026-10-21T19:53Z"`, `TAURI_PRECISE="2.12.4"`, `DIALOG="2.8.3"`, `JS_API="2.12.4"`, `JS_CLI="2.12.3"`; the eight internals tauri-build, tauri-codegen and tauri-macros 2.7.3, tauri-runtime and tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.2, tauri-utils 2.10.2, tauri-plugin 2.7.2, tauri-plugin-fs 2.7.1; seven `JS_PINS` rows — react and react-dom 19.3.1, @tanstack/react-query 5.104.2, dompurify 3.4.17, marked 18.1.1, @testing-library/user-event 14.6.8, vitest 5.0.4; step 1b's reference `locks/d1-lock-01b-pair.lock`. (Plan-rev3 rev 3.15: until the README records them so, the run passes them as flags — `--not-before 2026-10-21T19:53Z --tauri 2.12.4 --dialog 2.8.3 --js-api 2.12.4 --js-cli 2.12.3`, the eight `--tauri-crate` and seven `--js-pin` values above.) The driver's toolchain is its parameter `TOOLCHAIN="1.99.0"` (`gbase.sh:26` at `6d3e47fc`) — the one exception to "no `cargo +1.9x.y` literal", since the driver writes the channel into the export at step 1; its run of record precedes P03b, and a later run passes `--toolchain 1.99.1`.
- **Gate.** The command line (← the gbase README, *How to run on the cut day*):

  ```sh
  cd <the repository>
  G=tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase
  CUT=2026-10-21T19:53Z    # the owner's D1 (a): the run starts at or after it (12:53 PDT); the driver's default
  TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --self-test --toolchain 1.99.0        # the failure modes still caught
  TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --base <branch point> --bun <bun 1.4.3> --min-passed <floor> \
      --not-before "$CUT" --dry-run                                               # pre-flight + the plan, exit 0
  TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --base <branch point> --bun <bun 1.4.3> --min-passed <floor> \
      --not-before "$CUT" --apply-step-backs --dates --archive-to tasks/deps-upgrade   # the run (≈ 30-60 min cold)
  ```

  Pass = exit 0: every step and gate passed, every lock difference printed and explained (above). **The run counts as the gate only with plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6b's five requirements built:** (1) step 1b checks every Tauri crate exactly — tauri, -build, -codegen, -macros, -runtime, -runtime-wry, -utils, -plugin, -plugin-fs, -plugin-dialog — each `created_at` at or before the cut instant minus 7 days; (2) the Tauri pair and the JS pins are parameters checked against registry dates, not literals; (3) step 2 and every later lock run the freshness gate (`lockdates.py --gate`); (4) the driver refuses to start before the instant (`--not-before <UTC>`); (5) the suite verdict fails a crashed test binary — the set of binaries named in `error: test failed, to rerun pass <spec>` must equal the set holding the expected pins, an empty pin set requires `LAST_RC == 0`, any `(signal:` line fails, the passed count is held to a floor from a measured reference (a 1.93.0 run of the same export), and `--self-test` has a SIGABRT case. Each counts as built when the gbase README, as committed, records it so; its revision-log rows of 2026-10-17 record MF-1's driver half, MF-2 items 1 and 4, MF-5's driver half and MF-6 (the amendment plan-rev3 names for (1)–(5)), and `--self-test` at 42 cases; its 2026-10-20 row records step 8's D4 (a) amendment (`96de1139`), `--self-test` still 42 of 42 on 1.99.0. P14's seven pins: expected to fail on the branch point only; on a tip carrying all of P15–P18, 0 failed with no pin, and a tree carrying some of them is refused (`expected_pins`). The suite figure measured on the cut-day lock is 5,126 passed; P30's pin test adds one at P30's own commit (expected, not measured) — not in the run of record, whose step-8 patch carries no test. cargo-nextest is recorded as the later structural fix (A-45), not scheduled. *Back-out:* none (a gate run, no commit; G-base is the wave's first gate, plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6); a base that already carries a patched file changed in a patch's hunk fails the pre-flight with the patch's name, and the remedy is to re-split from a diff against that base (`split-recut-diff.py`), not to force the patch.
- **Needs.** The cut instant (D1 (a)); `--min-passed`'s floor, measured first: export the base, make the export a git repository (`git init`) as the driver does — without it `memory/scope_context.rs:174`'s precondition and `request_workspace_threading` fail and the floor comes out two too low — run its own suite under its own toolchain (`rust-toolchain.toml`'s) with the same isolation, and pass its passed count less P14's seven pins at the branch point (on a tip carrying P15–P18, its passed count) (← the gbase README, *The final phase*, "The floor"); rustup with 1.99.0 installed (`cargo +1.99.0 --version` answers; the driver installs nothing); Bun 1.4.3, used ahead of P42's commit (the root `package.json` still says `bun@1.4.2` when it runs); network for `cargo update`, `bun install` and the registry reads; Python 3.9+, perl, git, tar; `TMPDIR` on a scratch volume with room for two target directories. Start at the instant or soon after: a JS install before 2026-10-22T15:05:34Z is expected to write the report's lock again (barring a yank), a later one to be refused naming undici 8.12.2. `--dry-run` was green on the tip `38649f7e` (23 anchors, the three patches applying), and on `7fc775bc` and `2c913f99` with `96de1139`'s driver (`--steps 8`: 23 anchors, 3 patches; with the final phase 43 and 3), and `--edits-only` of all eight steps with `locks/recut-lock-99-final.lock` then `cargo +1.99.0 check --offline --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` gave 0 errors (§4's cut-day compile probe).
- **Place.** First at the cut: after the nineteen pre-cut fixes (`48f2b9a6^..38649f7e`, landed) and before P42 (plan-rev3 §7.7, rev 3.17: "what remains of the order starts at the cut: G-base's run of record, then step 2's P42 → P03"). N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3 and N-MCP-4 wait for it (they rewrite regions `step-6-rmcp.patch` edits). Re-run whenever the base moves; a later run passes `--toolchain 1.99.1`.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a and 6b, §5.2 (G-base, the freshness gate, the toolchain convention), §5.4 rule (b); the gbase README (*How to run on the cut day*, *The steps*, revision log); `96de1139` (2026-10-20: step 8 on serde-saphyr 2.0, D4 (a), after the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z), `tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0, rev 3.34). The result — exit code, the RESULT line, `logs/step-backs.txt`, the lock differences with their dates, the counts — is recorded in `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 (§5.1 item 7) and answers plan-rev3 Appendix B #46 (whether the pair commit followed by a plain `cargo update` resolves to `recut-lock-02-update.lock`); the logs, lock-difference and dates files are archived as `tasks/deps-upgrade/evidence-<date>-gbase.tar.gz` (git-ignored) and indexed in `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md` (A1-17).

#### Prep rewrite — `(procedure) land the prep commits by rewriting them, not by rebasing them` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12` (base `6992a402`, tip `7e702582`; a local ref, never pushed) is not a series that rebases into the P commits: its base is a scratch tree ("not for landing") that already carries every version move (`git diff d7c6bbc6 6992a402`: 30 files, +1,713/−1,288), and its commits are deltas over it — `b899046c` ("sha2 0.11") touches no sha2 manifest line, `77d106ec` (P15) and `154b679d` (P18) touch no manifest, and `5c265232`, `44ec0b03` and `88394660` move only zip 8.6 → 9.0.2, keyring 4.2 `v1` → keyring-core and rmcp 3.5.0 → 3.5.1. Each P commit is therefore its G-base step's anchored edits and patch hunks plus the prep delta, rewritten onto the real tip, passed through the freshness gate and re-gated. **The pure deltas:** P10 `b899046c`, P12 `44ec0b03`, P15 `77d106ec`, P16 `5c265232`, P18 `154b679d`, P19 `88394660`, P19b `e87c038d`, P41 `96a1dc30`. **Dropped:** `c7e03960`, which is `9ec986ec` (identical by range-diff). **Moving no version, rewritten onto the tip and re-gated the same way:** P17 `75358018`, P17a `ea8f0886`, P36 `ac6db26c` and M08 `7e702582`. **The re-pins at the cut** (C3; §5 item 5): each floor the version the lock takes — P41 `rustls = { version = "0.23.46", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }` (prep `"0.23"`, `refs/prep/deps-wave-2026-10-12:Cargo.toml:148`); P10 `sha2 = "0.11.1"` (prep `"0.11"`, `:156`); P12 `keyring-core = "1.0.1"` (prep `"1.0"`, `:170`); P16 `zip = { version = "9.0.3", default-features = false, features = ["deflate"] }` with `cargo update -p zip@<locked> --precise 9.0.3`, never below 9.0.1 (prep `"9.0.2"`, `:164`; C7); and P27a `opener = "0.9"`, which the prep tip lacks (it writes `opener = "0.8"`, `:175`). **Where the rewrites meet the landed pre-cut fixes** (`8b6ea6a7..38649f7e`, each prep commit's files intersected with the range's): P36 `ac6db26c` over N-LEAD-1…N-LEAD-5 (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs`), N-TOOL-1 (`simple_query_handler.rs`), N-TOOL-2, -4 and -5 (`tools/registry/mod.rs`; N-TOOL-4 also `providers/anthropic/request.rs`), N-TOOL-7 (`session_log/record.rs`), N-SKILL-1, N-SKILL-2 and N-PLUG-6 (`orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs`), N-PLUG-2, -4 and -6 (`openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs`) and N-MCP-2 (`openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs`), and also N-STORE-1 (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory/tests.rs`), N-LEAD-3, N-LEAD-5 and N-SKILL-1 (`compose/tests.rs`), N-LEAD-5 and N-TOOL-4 (`openalpaca_llm/src/routing/router/tests.rs`), N-SKILL-1 and N-SKILL-2 (`orchestrator/tests.rs`), N-SKILL-2 (`orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs`), N-PLUG-2 (`apps/openalpacad/src/routes/extensions.rs`), N-LEAD-1 (`dispatcher/outcome.rs`) and N-TOOL-7 (`session_log/mod.rs`); P17 `75358018` over N-STORE-1 (`routes/files_types.rs`, `routes/files.rs`) and N-TOOL-4 (`security/sanitizer/mod.rs`); P41 `96a1dc30` over N-DAEMON-1 and N-STORE-1 (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs`) and over N-MCP-2's and N-TOOL-5's dependency-list lines in `Cargo.lock`; P19 `88394660` over `c7aebb98` and N-MCP-2 (`managers/mcp/tests.rs`, `client.rs`, `openalpaca_mcp/src/error.rs`, and `openalpaca_mcp/Cargo.toml`, whose `axum` and tokio `net` dev-dependency lines it finds in place, `futures-util` beside them), N-PLUG-2 (`routes/extensions/tests.rs`) and N-MCP-2's and N-TOOL-5's dependency-list lines in `Cargo.lock`; P19b `e87c038d` over N-MCP-2's `client.rs`; P19, P19b and P41 also over many of these commits' edits to `CLAUDE.md`, and P19 and P19b over their edits to `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` (P19 also `docs/CLI_Manual.md`); **Phase A–B: Bun, the toolchain, the Tauri pair, and the refresh**

#### P42 — `build: pin Bun 1.4.3` · Required (DU-13 (a)) · scheduled
- **Does.** Root `package.json` `"packageManager": "bun@1.4.3"` (eligible 2026-10-17T05:24:54Z; at D1 (a)'s instant eligible and the newest 1.4.x; every JS gate of the compatibility report ran under `1.4.3+c6da4a4d3`, its §2.1). `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain line names it (`:179` at `6d3e47fc`), and so do `README.md:29` ("Bun 1.4.2 (the version the root `package.json` pins)") and `README.md:211` ("Frontend: Bun 1.4.2"); §6's row says so. Also (G-06): `docs/Installation_Manual.md`'s *Build Machine Requirements* names the pinned version, and each of the three package scripts (which today check only that `bun` is present — `scripts/release/package-macos.sh:94-95`, and `:34` in `read_target_directory`) compares `bun --version` with the root `packageManager` and stops on a mismatch, naming both. The 1.4.2 side install of plan-rev3 §5.1 item 2 is dropped once this lands (SF-29); the owner's local Bun is already 1.4.3, so the pin and the local install agree; P31a's incremental install and P31's from-scratch v2 lock are written by it.
- **Gate.** The four bun gates under 1.4.3 on the frozen lock (green on `9ce2cb2d` under the owner's 1.4.3); CI's gui job logs `bun install v1.4.3` (on the draft PR, item 9); `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge` honoured (M44); each package script refuses a Bun other than the pin, shown once with a shim named `bun` that prints another version first on `PATH` (no second Bun is installed). The report's CI-shaped run under 1.4.3 — the committed bunfig, `bun install --frozen-lockfile` from `apps/openalpaca-gui`, the four gates — passed on the cut-day v2 lock and left it byte-identical (its §3.3). Whether Dependabot's bundled Bun 1.4.2 accepts `bun@1.4.3` and the v2 lock is judged after the merge (P35). *Back-out:* revert (1.4.2 returns).
- **Needs.** DU-13 (a); G-base's run of record; §5 item 9's draft PR (before P42/P03) for the CI half.
- **Place.** At the cut, the first commit after G-base's run of record, before P03 (merged-order step 2).

#### P12 — `build(deps): keyring-core 1.0 with the native stores (Secret Service over zbus on Linux)` · Required, gated by DU-10 · scheduled
- **Does.** `Cargo.toml:155` at `865a2376`: `keyring` → **`keyring-core = "1.0.1"`** (C3; 1.0.1 changes tests and CI only; the prep writes `"1.0"` and is re-pinned when it lands); the target-specific stores keyring 4's `v1` feature selects — `apple-native-keyring-store` 1.0.2 (`keychain`, macOS), `windows-native-keyring-store` 1.1 (Windows), `zbus-secret-service-keyring-store` 1.0.1 (each the newest) — so item addressing is unchanged (service `OpenAlpaca`, account the ref, no modifiers); `keyring_core::Entry` and `Error::NoEntry` at `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/secret_store/mod.rs`. **As the prep delta `44ec0b03` built it (A1-05):** (1) the zbus store carries `features = ["crypto-rust"]` (keyring 4's `v1` sets it; secret-service 5.2.0 refuses to compile without a crypto feature), each store's version in `[workspace.dependencies]` and `openalpaca_llm` taking each under its own target table; (2) its cfg is v1's — every unix but macOS, iOS and Android; (3) `KeyringSecretStore` is constructed at `apps/openalpacad/src/services/llm.rs:25` and `:68` and `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ai_config_helpers.rs:38`; (4) the store is a `static NATIVE_STORE: LazyLock<keyring_core::Result<Arc<CredentialStore>>>` inside `keys/secret_store/mod.rs`, made on the process's first get/set/delete and reached through `KeyringSecretStore::native()` — keyring-core's process-wide default store is never set or read, so no lookup can answer `NoDefaultStore`; a store that could not be made (headless Linux, no Secret Service) fails each call with its own reason while `CachingSecretStore::prefetch` answers false and the daemon falls back to the in-memory store; its four tests point a `KeyringSecretStore` at keyring-core's in-memory mock store. Lock: `keyring` leaves; `keyring-core`, the three store crates, `secret-service 5.2.0`, the `zbus 5.19.0` stack (Linux-only compile); `dbus-secret-service`, `security-framework 2.11.1`, `core-foundation 0.9.4` leave. **This is the commit that first brings zbus into the lock** (the lock resolves every target, so on macOS too): its freshness gate refuses zbus_names 4.3.5 (2026-10-16T14:49:32Z), zvariant 5.15.1 (14:49:21Z) and zvariant_derive 5.15.1 (14:49:09Z) and steps them back, dependents first, to 4.3.4, 5.15.0 and 5.15.0; a release published before the cut is stepped back the same way. Why keyring-core: keyring 4's `v1` is "exactly the same API as the original v1" — the older form on the new major.
- **Gate.** G-check (macOS); the PR's ubuntu job (the first Linux compile of zbus; read at the second CI read, before P15); DU-10 — a count of 0, or a disposable-keychain round trip; a count of 0 waives the round trip only when it is 0 on both the `llm.toml` count and a count over `plugins/.config/*.toml` and `plugins/.permissions.toml` (SF-14); a unit test that the first lookup in a fresh process never answers `NoDefaultStore`; the absence check as an inventory (`cargo tree -i keyring` exits 101 once the package is gone): `cargo tree --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --target all -e normal,build --prefix none | grep -c '^keyring v'` prints 0, and `grep -c '^name = "keyring"$' Cargo.lock` prints 0. **No G-GUI** (keyring never reaches the shell). The freshness gate. *Back-out:* revert — keyring-core writes through the same three stores with the same item addressing, so keyring 3 reading those items follows from the same source reading as for v4 and is not measured [NE]; DU-10's round trip, when it runs, measures it.

The prep's `zip = { version = "9.0.2", … }` is not copied. `calamine = "0.36"`, which keeps its own `zip 8.6.0` (calamine 0.36.1, 2026-07-27, the newest, requires `zip ^8.6`; the 8.x line has nothing after 8.6.0). `apps/openalpacad/src/extraction.rs:198` → `let Ok(name) = entry.name() else { continue }; let name = name.into_owned();` (`name()` returns `ZipResult<Cow<'_, str>>` in 9.x). #### N-MCP-1 — `fix(mcp): a call that may have reached the server is never sent again` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `McpClient::call_tool` (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:530-584`) loops: on any `is_retriable()` error (TransportClosed, Transport, Timeout, ServerInternal; `error.rs:83-91`) it reconnects and sends again with a fresh `request_timeout`; `reconnect` kills the stdio child (`:483-484`); and a good handshake resets the attempt counter (`:325`, `:510`, `:564`) — so a server that handshakes and then fails every call loops until the sandbox cut (60 s for agents, 300 s for the lead), and a non-idempotent tool runs twice after a timeout. Now: before sending, if the service is absent or `is_transport_closed()` (rmcp-0.16.0 `src/service.rs:422`; rmcp-3.5.1 `:1053`), reconnect once and send (the transparent respawn of a dead child, the extension design's §3.6 item 1); **once the request may have left, it is never sent again** — Timeout, ServerInternal, Transport and a mid-call TransportClosed go back to the model, and the next call respawns; one deadline spans the pre-send reconnect and the call; consecutive failed handshakes count toward `ReconnectExhausted` — a handshake alone does not reset the count; `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §3.6 item 1's text and its revision log are amended in the same commit. Re-sending when `idempotent_hint` is true is left out (it would be presented with T35 if ever wanted). ZCode: one deadline per call, a reconnect-once before the call, retry only on a definite "not connected" (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:778, :828-830, :916-928). **The ledger half** (T6(a)'s sub-choice, taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T00:26Z): **the ledger learns at the child's exit**; not built; scheduled here): when a stdio server's child exits, its ledger row reads `Failed` at once, its detail the exit code or signal, and `extension_state_changed` is emitted (the supervisor's existing emission, `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:720` at `2e02f684`); recovery is T6(a)'s taken form, unchanged — the reconnect-once before the next call returns the row to `Enabled`; an HTTP server's death is learned at the next call or the idle bound. It is a third option, neither of the two the row posed; `tasks/extension-enable-design.md` §3.6 and §13 Q8 record it (that file's rev 19), and this commit's amendment of §3.6 item 1 says the same. Under DU-25 (m-c), P19's rewrite then pins this entry's one reconnect to the era the load negotiated.
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a stub that counts `tools/call` and lets the first time out sees one call, and the model receives the timeout; a stub that dies after the call is sent sees one send, and the next call respawns it; a server that handshakes but fails every call reaches `ReconnectExhausted` after `max_reconnect_attempts` handshakes, not at the sandbox cut; a dead stdio child is respawned once before the send, and the call succeeds; a stdio stub that exits with a known code between calls leaves its row `Failed` at once, the detail naming that code, with one `extension_state_changed` emitted before any call is made; one killed by a signal names the signal; the next call's one reconnect returns the row to `Enabled`. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::tools`. Plus §1.6's common gate. *Back-out:* revert.
- **Needs.** G-base's run of record; P18; T6(a) (1) and its sub-choice (taken 2026-10-19); N-MCP-2 (landed `48f2b9a6`, whose residue — an in-session re-handshake waits up to the idle bound, not 30 s — this entry's one deadline closes).
- **Place.** After G-base's run of record, before P19 — at Phase G's head, after P18 (merged-order step 2). The fallback, if P19's rewrite over it proves costly, is folding it into M15 (after P38); it is never dropped.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 *The MCP client* N-MCP-1 (Z5-02; amends the extension design's §3.6 item 1; rev 3.18 ledger half); modernization-plan §6.3 step 2 (rev 2.7).

#### N-MCP-3 — `fix(mcp): a call does not hold the service lock across its RPC` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `call_tool`, `list_tools`, `list_tools_once` and `ping` hold the `service` mutex across the whole RPC (`client.rs:367`, `:387`, `:503`, `:556`), and the lock is taken inside the timeout wrapper (`:553-560`): a queued call spends its timeout waiting for the lock and fails unsent, and parallel subagents gain nothing. Now: clone the `Peer` under the guard and drop the guard before awaiting (rmcp 0.16 already has `RunningService::peer`, `src/service.rs:445`, and `Peer::call_tool` / `list_all_tools`, `src/service/client.rs:362`, `:375`); for calls, the lock is taken outside the timeout wrapper or not at all. Before landing: confirm that T3's registry-level drain bounds a call running on a cloned peer, and amend the extension design's §3.2 T4 and disable-latency text (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md:2195-2197`).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: two concurrent calls to a stub that holds the first for 2 s both finish in about 2 s; a disable during an in-flight call on a cloned peer waits for that call within the drain bound. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::extensions`. Plus §1.6's common gate. *Back-out:* revert.
- **Needs.** N-MCP-1 (with or after it).
- **Place.** With or after N-MCP-1, in the same slot and for the same reason: after G-base's run of record, before P19.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-MCP-3 (Z5-04; amends the extension design's §3.2 T4 text).

#### N-MCP-4 — `fix(core): remote MCP tool names mapped to a provider-safe alphabet; collisions within one server warned` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** The remote half of a tool name is used raw in `format!("{server_name}__{remote_name}")` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:33-34`); the registry refuses only empty names, names over 256 bytes and names containing NUL (`tools/registry/mod.rs:548-566`), and both providers send the name verbatim. Now: first read each provider's documented tool-name rule and record it in the body; map the remote half to `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` and cap the combined length, keeping `remote_name` raw for the call; detect collisions within one server's mapped list **before** registration — keep the first and warn, naming both originals (the case-13 skip ignores incumbents of the same extension, `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:1245`, so today the second tool would overwrite the first). A template or skill that names a remapped tool by its raw name stops matching: the body names that, and `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` says the mapped name is the capability. ZCode sanitises names (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/mcp/name.ts:14-18).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: `read.file` registers under the mapped name and calls the server with `read.file`; `read.file` beside `read_file` keeps the first and warns naming both. Commands: `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib tools::mcp`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp`. Plus §1.6's common gate. *Back-out:* revert; a server's tools return to their raw names.
- **Needs.** G-base's run of record.
- **Place.** After G-base's run of record, before P19: `bridge.rs` is a `step-6-rmcp.patch` file (hunks `@@ -7`, `@@ -87`, `@@ -106`), and P19's `ContentBlock` hunks rewrite it.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 N-MCP-4 (Z5-05).

A child leaves the daemon's process group (a terminal Ctrl+C no longer reaches it directly; the daemon's shutdown kills it by group), and the watchdog's `process::exit(1)` leaves group leaders alive — the same window as R45's. **Residue, recorded:** the signal follows the reap (the zero-window form needs `waitid(WNOWAIT)`, which nix does not offer on macOS).   - **The body names:** RUSTSEC-2026-0189 (rmcp 0.16.0, server-transport DNS rebinding) cleared (re-read from the cut-day run); the lifecycle as `Auto` with its costs — a server that ignores unknown methods waits out `DEFAULT_AUTO_DISCOVER_TIMEOUT` (10 s) per (re)connect, so a server configured with `connect_timeout_secs` of 10 or less that ignores `server/discover` never connects (the default is 30; measured 10.02 s in prep), and 401/403, 5xx, a transport failure or a malformed 2xx fail the connect with no fallback — which the prep's edits to the Daemon and CLI manuals carry; the process-group wrapper; the rmcp issue for a configurable auto-discover timeout (rmcp-3.5.1 `src/service/client.rs:657`), §5 item 15, or that it is not filed yet; the run's escaped `setsid` pid (below).

#### P19b — `feat(mcp): a 2026-07-28 session subscribes to its server's list changes` · Required (D5 (a)) · scheduled
- **Does.** A server that speaks `2026-07-28` sends `notifications/tools/list_changed` (and the prompt and resource siblings) only on a `subscriptions/listen` stream, and rmcp's client opens none by itself; with P19's `Auto` alone a modern server is never heard to add or drop a tool — the hole the extension design's §3.7 closed (X-35). `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs`: `serve` opens the stream when the negotiated version has no `initialize` — a `ChangeListener` asks for the list changes the server declares (`listChanged: true` on tools, prompts, resources), and a spawned task forwards each onto the client's change channel as `ServerChange`, where the supervisor's §3.7 refresh already reads them. The connect does not wait on the acknowledgment. A refusal, a partial acknowledgment, a failed stream or one the server ends while the connection lives is a `warn` naming the server; the stream ending with its connection is a `debug`. The task holds a weak sender, so the supervisor's receiver still ends at `disconnect`. A legacy session is unchanged. **D5 (a)'s three conditions and A1-12, written in:** on the `listen` acknowledgment the task sends one `ServerChange` — one `tools/list` refresh — through the existing channel (closing the window between `serve` returning and the acknowledgment); after an unexpected end of the stream while the connection lives the listener reopens the subscription once, and a second end in the same connection keeps the warning; a list change that arrives mid-turn reaches the next turn and leaves the running turn's tool surface alone — if that does not hold on the tree, this commit makes it hold; N-MCP-2 lands first (landed `48f2b9a6`). The body names D5, the three conditions and A1-12; CLAUDE.md's MCP bullet and `docs/Daemon_Manual.md` say a modern server's list changes are heard.
- **Gate.** P19's checks and test filters on the commit; an in-process `2026-07-28` stub — **the gate of record**: the change reaches the channel and the channel ends with the session; the filter asks for exactly what is declared; the mutation without the `listen` call fails; the legacy stubs see no stray `listen`. Additions, each failing on the parent: a stub that announces a change before acknowledging sees it arrive; over HTTP, a listen stream that outlives 30 s keeps delivering; a stub that ends the stream once sees a later change arrive, and one that ends it twice sees the warning and no third subscription; a list change made during a turn's tool call leaves that turn's tool set as it was and shows in the next turn's. Measured in prep (`scratchpad/prep/logs/p19/c2-*`): mcp 39 + 6 passed; daemon `managers::mcp routes::tools routes::extensions` 105 passed; core `--lib` 2,539 passed, 2 failed (P14's pins, as at the parent); clippy and fmt at the parent's counts; `gen_api_docs --check` clean — expectations. **A real `2026-07-28` server** in P19's manual round trip, if one exists by then — a list change made on the server and seen in `GET /v1/tools` without a reload — is an end-to-end step (§4, E5 additions), recorded as not run otherwise. *Back-out:* revert (one commit); P19 stands without it, and a modern server's list changes go unheard again — record that residue in design §18 if it is ever reverted.
- **Needs.** P19; D5 (a); N-MCP-2 (landed); the prep rewrite (`e87c038d` over P19's `88394660`, over N-MCP-2's `client.rs`).
- **Place.** Phase G, right after P19.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.3 Phase G P19b (rev 3.12–3.16; SF-3, SF-12, A1-12, A-05); §7.7. Residue that remains: Appendix B #42's per-server discover cost is P19's, unchanged.

#### P41 — `fix(connectors): one rustls crypto provider — aws-lc-rs installed at daemon start, ring off our manifest` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** As the prep `96a1dc30` built it (a pure delta; rev 3.9's own contents are superseded, SF-11): `[workspace.dependencies]` gains **`rustls = { version = "0.23.46", default-features = false, features = ["aws_lc_rs"] }`** (C3: the floor the version P04's refresh takes; the prep's `"0.23"` admits every rustls release RUSTSEC-2026-0349 affects, and is re-pinned when it lands), and no manifest of ours names `ring`; openalpacad gains `rustls.workspace = true` and a new `apps/openalpacad/src/tls.rs` whose `install_crypto_provider()` installs `rustls::crypto::aws_lc_rs::default_provider()` as the process default — `main` calls it right after the logger, after the command line (W1) and before the store, the runtime and anything that builds a TLS client; an `Err` from `install_default()` means a provider is already installed, which rustls keeps, so the daemon logs a warning naming it (read from `CryptoProvider::get_default()`) and carries on. The two ring installs in `crates/openalpaca_connectors/src/discord/connector.rs` (`:285-286`, `:882-883` at `3598a6fb`) go — `run_loop`'s with a doc line saying the host installs the provider, the test's replaced by an aws-lc-rs install — and the connectors' `rustls` moves from an optional dependency under the `discord` feature (`Cargo.toml:28`, `:67`) to a dev-dependency on the workspace spec. **What it fixes besides the second provider:** `connector_bridge::discord_http()` builds a twilight client for a stored `discord.token` whether or not the connector runs, and `sendable_channels()` is asked on the social fast path and by `send`, so on a daemon whose Discord connector had not started that call panicked with rustls's "Could not automatically determine the process-level CryptoProvider"; and once the connector had started, every reqwest client built afterwards ran on ring (reqwest 0.12 takes the process-wide default provider first, so after P28 Telegram's connections run on the aws-lc-rs provider this commit installs while ring is compiled on teloxide's path). The provider is aws-lc-rs 1.18.2 over aws-lc-sys 0.46.0 (P04, C9). Tests: `tls::tests::the_process_default_is_aws_lc_rs` and `connector_bridge::tests::a_stored_discord_token_offers_discord_before_the_connector_runs`. **A-09 (2), taken:** a test in `cargo test -p openalpaca_connectors --features discord` points twilight's HTTP client and its gateway at a loopback stub, sends one message and reads one `READY`, with dev-dependencies on crates the workspace already locks (an HTTP and WebSocket stub on `axum` with its `ws` feature, or `hyper` with `tokio-tungstenite`) — new `[dev-dependencies]` edges, moving no version. The body names the blocker that keeps `ring` in the lock after P28 — teloxide 0.17's rustls path through reqwest 0.12's `__rustls-ring` — and its way out: teloxide 0.18 (its master on reqwest 0.13) or an upstream teloxide feature on reqwest's `*-no-provider` (the owner's to post).

`useless_format` ×2; `question_mark`, `manual_contains`, `redundant_closure`, `needless_borrow`, `needless_borrows_for_generic_args`, `for_kv_map`, `redundant_guards`, `collapsible_match`), the 17 `collapsible_if` sites as let chains, the 10 allows each with a one-line reason (`event_bridge.rs:37`'s `created_by: _` kept — R79 — under `#[allow(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern)]`, and under `#[expect(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern, reason = "R79")]` once M39 lands; `result_large_err` ×6 as `routes/`'s 14 existing allows, or `client.rs:663`'s boxed; `too_many_arguments` ×2; `large_enum_variant` at `compose/mod.rs:112`), and the 8 remaining judgement items (`field_reassign_with_default` ×4, `derivable_impls` ×2 only where the manual `Default` is field-for-field the derive, `items_after_test_module`, `new_without_default`) each decided with a sentence; and **M04**: `crates/openalpaca_core/src/session_log/mod.rs` (`:519`; `:514-528` in M04's citation) — `fetch_update` replaced by the **infallible** `Atomic::update` (1.95): `let prev = self.next_spill.update(Ordering::Relaxed, Ordering::Relaxed, |current| current.max(floor) + 1); let number = prev.max(floor);`, both `Ordering::Relaxed` kept, the sequence-number contract above the call holding. **Never a blanket `clippy --fix`.** The prep commit is rewritten onto the tip over the nineteen pre-cut fixes (its catalog hunks re-derived over N-SKILL-1, etc. — the rewrite entry's list). M08 (let-else at the 55 `manual_let_else` sites) is its sibling and lands after P38 (§3.3, step 6).
- **Gate.** Clippy unique count **100 → 0** (measured in prep: nothing fires); 0 rustc warnings and 0 `deprecated`, the `live-eval` and shell selections included — `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`, `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --all-targets --locked`, and, after `bun run build`, with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui`, `cargo clippy -p openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`, each 0 `deprecated` (M04); `cargo test -p openalpaca_core session_log`; no fmt hunk added, compared hunk by hunk against the parent (G-lint; the count fell 1,586 → 1,584 in prep); G-CI at the parent's count, 0 failed. #### FRESHNESS-SWEEP — `(one commit per release) each release eligible after the cut instant` · Required (DU-23; MF-2 4) · scheduled
- **Does.** Each release that becomes eligible after the cut instant gets its own commit with its gate, or is named with its blocker and its way out. The releases the cut refuses, named by the source: react-query 5.104.3, jsdom 30.1.3, vitest 5.0.5, Rust 1.99.1 (that one is P03b, its own entry) and the transitive rest of DU-23's rev 3.15 table (§2's DU-23 row; plan-rev3 §4.3). A new *major* found this way is recorded in DU-23's table and in its commit and researched before it lands, or its blocker named. The sweep covers what the cut refused through P38: jsonschema 0.59.0 is no longer the sweep's — M82, landing after P38 on its own clock, takes it after its research, 0.58.6 only with a named blocker (the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19; rev 1.0's dated record: published 2026-10-16T20:30:14Z, too young at the cut, M82 takes 0.58.6) — and oxlint 1.88.0 (eligible; M66 names 1.87.0) is Part III's.
- **Gate.** Each commit's own: the gate of the entry it extends (P31 for react-query and jsdom, P32 for vitest), with the freshness check recorded in its `Verified:` line. **Its max date — Taken 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z)**, the owner: "those things are single issue, you can directly mark the try to use serde-saphyr 2.x and don't need to mark a fixed date, now begin implementation, with fable do the orchestration and opus do the implementation" — no fixed date: a sweep commit's freshness gate uses the commit's own instant minus 7 days, UTC — P03b's per-commit clock, the one candidate rev 1.0 named (a release's publication time plus 7 days at or before the commit) — `lockdates.py --gate --max-date <the commit's instant − 7 days, UTC>` for a cargo move and `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date <the same>` for a JS move, the result in the commit's `Verified:` line. The wave-wide max date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z stays for every gate through P38 (§1.2); every lock-moving commit after P38 (the sweep; Part III's M66, M67, M69) uses its own clock — Part III's three by the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19. (Rev 1.0 left it unsettled: plan-rev3 §5.2 fixes 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z "for every gate of the wave", which no sweep release can meet, and §5.1 item 5 says only that each gets "its own commit with its gate".) E3's matrix lists each sweep commit or named blocker, so the matrix is not frozen at the cut (§4).
- **Needs.** The release's eligibility (published time + 7 days).
- **Place.** Before the merge and before E1–E8, which run after the sweep at merged-order step 19 (← plan-rev3 §6 preamble, *Order* (SF-16)); P03b, the sweep's toolchain case, lands before P38 (§3.2's order, item 13).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 5 (MF-2 4), §6 (E3); §5.3 preamble (1); the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (`tasks/api-fix-plan.md` §0, rev 3.34).

### 3.3 After P38

The order of record after P38 is modernization-plan §6.3's merged list, steps 2a–20 (plan-rev3 §7.7 names that list the order of record and keeps no second copy; `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 carries it). Each step below gives its entries in modernization-plan §6.3's order. Part I's own commits, steps 1–2, are §3.1 and §3.2. ← modernization-plan §6.3

**Conventions that bind every entry here** (the rules themselves are §1; restated only where an entry's gate depends on them):
- **One place per commit** (SF-16). modernization-plan §6.3 gives each commit one place; an entry's "after X" is its constraint, not a second place. Where an entry's own place line in its source disagrees with modernization-plan §6.3, this section writes modernization-plan §6.3's place and tags it. ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **The pinned toolchain** (SF-8; D3 (a)). Every gate runs `cargo` under the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins: 1.99.0 from P03, then 1.99.1 from P03b. A `cargo +1.99.0` in a Part III entry or gate means the pinned toolchain; the literal is not rewritten, and run literally after P03b it would be a second toolchain (M01's line (2)). ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **Gate counts** (D-14). A test-count gate reads "the parent's count, 0 failed"; a figure from a past run is a record, not the gate. ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **Status words.** **Required** lands under every answer; **Recommended** in Part III lands under the answers taken; only **Optional** (M53) may be dropped; **DU-bound** lands only under the named answer. Since modernization-plan rev 2.5 no Part III commit is Conditional: the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 took the questions M70, M83, M84 and M85 waited on, and each is DU-bound under the answer taken. ← modernization-plan §6 conventions, rev 2.2 (D-10)
- **Part III's three conventions** beside plan-rev3 §5.2's: never a blanket `cargo clippy --fix` (P36's rule); a `Display` string is behaviour — every `thiserror` commit writes a golden test of each variant's text against the manual `impl` *before* the derive and keeps it; Part III's source commits land after P38 (exceptions: M04 and M07, which are P36; the three target-directory commits; M63). ← modernization-plan §6 conventions
- **G-bundle** is in every shell and CSP commit's gate; **G-GUI runs beside G-bundle** for M63 and M71–M79 (C-10), leg A under plan-rev3's corrected launcher (MF-4). ← modernization-plan §6 conventions, rev 2.2
- **Memory code** (D-13). The read-first rule (modernization-plan §3.5 item 1) binds every commit whose sites reach `memory_files`, `memory_index`, `memory::service` or `memory::learning` — M05, M06, M08, M21–M24, M29 (`MemoryError`, `memory/service/error.rs`), M41, M45–M53 and M64 among them — and its `Verified:` line names the reading. ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **Build-dir beside target-dir** (MF-5). From M63 on, every build that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` also sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` — the same path or a sibling under the same scratch root, for the shell always `target/gui`. ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **Freshness** (MF-2; C5 (a)). A commit that adds or moves a `Cargo.lock` or `bun.lock` entry takes the newest release eligible under DU-23, checked by `lockdates.py --gate` for cargo and `bunfig.toml` for JS, and its body lists the new majors it pulls in and from where; each JS lock write here (M66, M67, M69) also runs C5's check — `lockdates.py --bun-lock <the new bun.lock> --max-date <the commit's instant − 7 days, UTC>` over every name@version of the new `bun.lock` — and records it in its `Verified:` line. **The max date after P38** (the lead, 2026-10-20, under the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19: its "don't need to mark a fixed date" read as covering every lock-moving commit after P38, not the sweep alone): a lock-moving commit here takes its own instant minus 7 days, by `lockdates.py --gate` for cargo and `--bun-lock` for JS, so "the newest release eligible when the commit lands" reads literally — the newest eligible at the commit's own instant minus 7 days. (Rev 1.0, from the source: `lockdates.py --pins npm` with max date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z, "the newest release eligible when the commit lands" read as the newest eligible at the cut instant, a release eligible later being plan-rev3 §5.1 item 5's before-the-merge sweep's.) A version an entry names stays a dated record (§3.2 preamble (1)). ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions; the lead's reading, 2026-10-20
- **Dependency source at the locked version** (MF-1). Each commit citing a Tauri crate source (M63, M72–M78) re-reads what it cites at the version the lock holds when it lands — tauri-2.12.4, tauri-build-2.7.3, tauri-codegen-2.7.3, tauri-utils-2.10.2 — and corrects a moved line in its own commit; M72's and M77's citations are the first to re-read (2.12.4 fixes a panic in the IPC and asset protocol handlers on an empty URI path). ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **DU-33** (macOS first) binds Part III: M17's `LockFileEx` leg and M18's Windows store difference wait with it, and CI's ubuntu rust job is the one non-macOS build a gate here reads (M39, M65). ← modernization-plan §6 rev 2.2 conventions
- **plan-rev3 §5.4 and §5.5 entries** (the N-, SA- and B-22 entries below): no `(name, version)` enters or leaves either lock; the gate's named test is written first and shown to fail on the entry's parent (or with the fix reverted), and the `Verified:` line says so; beside it G-CI at the parent's count with 0 failed, G-lint with no fmt hunk added and no clippy warning (0 from P36 on), and `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` clean — regenerated in the same commit when a route, CLI verb, config key or public type changes, with `API_MAP.md` and CLAUDE.md's tables; a GUI change runs the four bun gates; the body names the audit ids or row it builds, the reference it learned from and what a person or a model now meets; tests follow plan-rev3 §5.2 and CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* (the crate's home-store guard, `GuardedProject`, `KillOnFail`, no test reading `~/.openalpaca` or the keychain — `DiscoveryHost`). plan-rev3 §5.5's entries are each *not built*; taking a decision builds nothing. Every borrowing from Claude Code or ZCode is a design written fresh, never a code port. ← plan-rev3 §5.4 *What every entry means*; §5.5 *What every entry means*

**Step 2a — B-22's notices, right after P38**

#### B-22 (notices) — `build(release): THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES generated from both lockfiles, hashed, and bundled` · Required before any beta distribution (B-22 (b)) · scheduled
- **Does.** One script, named in the body, generates `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` from `Cargo.lock` (the packages the daemon, the CLI and the shell link) and `bun.lock` (the packages the frontend bundle ships) — each entry the package, its version and its licence text — and records in the file's header the SHA-256 of each lockfile it read. `scripts/release/package-macos.sh` checks it before `tauri build` and refuses when the recorded hashes are not the tree's lockfiles' (beside its sidecar hash check, `:178-186`); `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`'s `bundle` (`:27`, no `resources` key today) bundles it as a resource; the archive carries it beside the app (all at `476f1f81`). The licences it may name are those P34's `[licenses]` admits. The Rust-side generator is this commit's to choose, pinned like plan-rev3 §5.1 item 13's tools, the choice recorded in the body (plan-rev3 Appendix B #56); for `bun.lock`, `bun pm licenses` is evaluated first and the body records whether it gives every shipped package its licence text or what is used instead. Archive byte-reproducibility is not this commit's (`package-macos.sh:202-210` embeds `built_at_utc`). The Installation Manual says where the notices are. No `.gitignore` line is needed: `git check-ignore THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` exits 1 at `476f1f81` (re-checked if the file is placed elsewhere). Moves no version.
- **Gate.** A `package-macos.sh` run in a worktree passes, the bundle's `Contents/Resources/` and the archive carry the file, and its recorded hashes equal the lockfiles'; the same run after a one-line change to `bun.lock` or `Cargo.lock` without regenerating fails, naming the stale file; every package of the shipped graph appears (counts compared with `cargo metadata` and `bun.lock`, recorded in the body); G-bundle. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P34 (`[licenses]` in `deny.toml`, which it reads); B-22 (b), taken by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17. Does not wait for the LICENSE file, which is the owner's (§5, plan-rev3 §5.1 item 16) — but the beta also waits for the LICENSE.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 2a: right after P38, before the beta is cut (the lead's placement ruling of 2026-10-19 — the beta is cut from `main` after Part I merges, Parts II and III after it); still after P34. Moved here from step 13 (beside Q09).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 B-22 (notices); §5.4 B table row B-22; modernization-plan §6.3 step 2a. The ZCode audit's A-30 with A-08; Z7-02, Z7-12.

**Step 3 — M57, the first Part III source commit**

#### M57 — `fix(llm): a typed reqwest error classification — the retry classifier sees real transport failures` · Required (a latent defect) · scheduled
- **Does.** The providers map `reqwest::Error` through one function — `is_timeout`, `is_connect`, `is_dns` (0.13.5), `is_request`, `is_body` — into typed `LlmError` variants, at `providers/openai/mod.rs:184`, `:232`, `:314`, `providers/anthropic/mod.rs:126`, `:175`, `:254`, `providers/ollama.rs:129`, `:196`, `:315` (today `LlmError::Http(e.to_string())`); `is_transient_http_error` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/error.rs:89-111`) classifies the typed variants; the CLI's REPL check (`apps/openalpaca/src/repl/mod.rs:88-89`, `is_connect() || is_timeout()`) adds `is_dns()`; floor `reqwest = "0.13.5"`. Why: reqwest's `Display` writes only the kind and the URL, never the source chain (`reqwest-0.13.5/src/error.rs:257-305`), so the classifier's substrings never match a real send failure and timeouts and resets are not retried today; the unit tests at `lib.rs:242-277` pass on hand-written strings reqwest never produces.
  - *The type (SF-24).* `LlmError` and `LlmRouterError` derive `Clone` (`error.rs:1`; `routing/router/types.rs:68-69`) and `reqwest::Error` is not `Clone`, so the variant carries a typed kind and a message and no source: `LlmError::Transport { kind, message }`, `kind` one of connect timeout, request timeout, connect refused, DNS, reset or closed, TLS or other; `message` reqwest's text followed by its source chain joined with `: `. `{e}` and `{e:#}` print the same text once.
  - *The retry table* (under M57's question taken (a)):

    | Kind | `complete`'s key layer (`routing/router/retry.rs:203`: `max(pool.len(), max_transient_retries = 3)` attempts, backoff with jitter, then at most 2 cooldown waits of ≤ 30 s) | `complete`'s fallback chain (`completion.rs:189-196`, then the CLI backend) | `complete_streaming` (`completion.rs:18-132`; on its error the loop's backend retries through `complete`, `runner/agentic_loop/backend.rs:31-32`, `:159`) | the agentic loop (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1429-1440`: 3 retries, `2^n` s apart, ≤ 30 s, each a round) |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | connect timeout | retried | taken | returned at once (`:128`) | retried |
    | request timeout (a non-streaming call's `request_timeout` deadline, or the read gap, `providers/mod.rs:29`) | **not retried**, on the same key or another — returned at once, still transient | taken | returned at once | retried |
    | reset, closed, incomplete body | retried | taken | returned at once | retried |
    | connect refused, DNS, TLS, other | not retried (`error.rs:86-88`) | not taken | returned at once | not retried |

  - *How (a) is built.* A key-layer predicate beside `LlmError::is_transient()` (`error.rs:102`) — false for `Transport { kind: RequestTimeout, .. }`, otherwise `is_transient()` — guards `retry.rs:203`'s arm, so the timeout leaves through the final arm (`:221-225`) as `LlmRouterError::Llm`, which the chain (`completion.rs:190`) and the loop (`agentic_loop/mod.rs:1435`) still take because `is_transient()` stays true; the doc comment (`error.rs:101`) is narrowed to say so.
  - *The bound* (a silent listener; C = the CLI backend's run where one is registered, `routing/router/fallback.rs:30-89`, else 0): one `complete` call waits at most `(1 + chain length) × request_timeout + C`; one loop attempt of a streamed turn at most `(2 + chain length) × request_timeout + C`; a turn at most `4 × ((2 + chain length) × request_timeout + C) + 14 s`.
  - *The breaker.* One circuit breaker, owned by `RateLimiterRegistry` (`routing/rate_limiter/mod.rs:456-478`). A failed try on the key ladder reports to it (`retry.rs:147`, `:190`, `:217`, `:224`; a request timeout included; an authentication error does not; an empty completion counts as a success, `:86`); after `circuit_breaker_threshold` consecutive failures across all providers (default 5, `rate_limiter/mod.rs:430-432`) it opens, and every `complete` call fails at once at `retry.rs:18` on every rung (`completion.rs:221`), landing on the unguarded CLI backend (cost C). `complete_streaming` neither consults nor reports to it. The body records the latency change against L7: today a timeout fails once; after M57 it walks the fallback chain and the loop retries it.
- **Gate.** The retry table above, under (a), tested with **real** reqwest errors (a refused port, a silent listener past its timeout, an unresolvable host), on the non-streaming and the streaming path; the wall time against a silent listener measured with a short `request_timeout`, a fallback chain and no CLI backend, and held to (a)'s bound; the breaker case — after `circuit_breaker_threshold` consecutive failures a `complete` call fails at once at `retry.rs:18` on every rung of the chain, while a streamed call is still made; the router's retry tests (`routing/router/retry.rs:203`, `completion.rs:190`); the user-visible error text changes recorded in the body. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** plan-rev3 P04 (reqwest 0.13.5); M57's request-timeout question, taken (a) by the owner's blanket answer of 2026-10-17 (the key layer does not retry a request timeout; (b), not taken, would retry it like a connect timeout).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3: the first Part III source commit after P38 (after B-22's notices, step 2a).
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M57 (rev 2.0; rev 2.2 SF-24; rev 2.5).

**Step 3a — the audit's fixes that touch nothing a later commit rewrites (in this order)**

Early, because each is a defect a person meets. ← modernization-plan §6.3 step 3a; plan-rev3 §5.4 placement table, row *After P38, in the merged order*

#### N-TOOL-3 — `fix(core): serialise non-read-only tool calls in a round` · Recommended (a fix, presented because it changes a round's latency; lands unless the owner declines it) · scheduled
- **Does.** Today every executable call in a round runs under `join_all` (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:1133`, `:1157`, `:1171`); neither `file_ops.rs` nor `pre_edit.rs` takes a lock and `file_edit`'s body has await points (`file_ops.rs:933-1012`), so bodies interleave once each call has passed its gate (approval-cache hit after Always allow, `auto_approve`, a sandbox with no broker, or two answers within a body's run time). Now each round is partitioned in model order: consecutive calls whose tool declares `read_only_hint = true` (for MCP, also `idempotent_hint = true`) share a group; every other call is a group of one; groups run in order, calls within a group concurrently. `spawn_subagents_batch`, `wait_for_subagents` and `check_subagent_status` stay concurrent-safe; plugin tools (no annotations) run alone. Results keep model order; cancellation unchanged (`RegisteredTool.annotations` lives in `tools/registry/mod.rs`). The body states the latency cost. Learned from ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/scheduler.ts:85-104, :154-194.
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: two `file_edit` calls on one file in one round, under a pre-approved (EntireTool-cached) or `auto_approve` policy, both land (a test with cards answered would likely pass today, audit Z1-01); two read-only calls still overlap in time. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::agentic_loop tools::builtins`. If it adds an accessor to `tools/registry/mod.rs`, clippy stays at 0. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38 (and P36's clippy 0).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3a, first. Touches `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs`, which no commit of the plan rewrites.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 Tools N-TOOL-3 (Z1-01).

#### N-TOOL-6 — `fix(core): an empty successful result says so` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** A successful shell command with no output returns `""` (`tools/builtins/shell_execute.rs:47-60`), and so does an MCP result with no content (`tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-107`). In one place in the loop, an empty `Ok` result becomes `(<tool> completed with no output)`; the tools themselves, `bridge.rs` among them, are untouched (as ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/executor/result-serialization.ts:57-68).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a stub tool returning `Ok("")` reaches the model as the placeholder naming the tool. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib runner::agentic_loop`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3a, after N-TOOL-3. Touches only `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs`; doing it in the loop keeps it off `bridge.rs`, a `step-6-rmcp.patch` file.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 Tools N-TOOL-6 (Z1-15).

#### N-SKILL-3 — `fix(skills): create-skill writes only where the daemon reads` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** create-skill's `scope=user` writes to `$HOME/.config/openalpaca/skills` (`config/skills/create-skill/scripts/write_skill_md.sh:84-86`; `scaffold.sh:59`; `check_exists.sh:31`; `list_skills.sh:58`), which the daemon never scans — it reads `<config>/skills` only (`apps/openalpacad/src/services/mod.rs:195-209`) — outside `~/.openalpaca` (D1), and the skill still reports success. Now the `user` scope is dropped, or refused naming the one root; `SkillScope::User`'s comment is fixed (`middleware/skill/types.rs:13`); and since the seeding gate never overwrites an existing skill directory (`apps/openalpacad/src/bootstrap/content.rs:61-71`), the switch-over note in `docs/Installation_Manual.md` says existing installs keep the old scripts. `list_skills.sh`'s frontmatter grep (`:39-42`) is P30's (A-07).
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: `write_skill_md.sh` run with `scope=user` under a scratch `HOME` writes nothing outside the config root and exits non-zero naming it (a scripted run in the crate's tests, or one recorded in the body if no harness runs the scripts). **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38.
