#### N-ROUTE-1 — `fix(core): /pause and /resume say pause is unsupported and change no status` · Required · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Today `/pause` flips a running workflow's row to `paused` (`orchestrator/task_ops.rs:97-104`, through `apply_task_action`; the bare form through `handle_bare_task_control`, `:325`) while its execution and spend continue, and `/resume` flips it back (`:105-112`). Now the two chat commands answer one sentence — pause is not supported, and `/cancel` stops a run — and change no status; no pause token is added. A later design, when a cancelled run can be resumed, maps `/pause` to cancel-as-interrupted plus resume. *Found in the tree:* `apply_task_action` is shared with `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/action` (`task_ops.rs:53-54`; `apps/openalpacad/src/routes/tasks.rs:506`), whose `pause`/`resume` the answer does not name; whether the route answers the same sentence is this commit's to state in its body, and if the route changes, `docs/api` and `API_MAP.md` change with it.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: `/pause` on a lane with one running workflow answers the sentence naming `/cancel`, its row stays `running` and no `TaskUpdated` is published; `/pause <id>` the same; `/resume` the same; `/cancel` unchanged. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- orchestrator`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38. Decided by: Claude Code (no pause — Esc interrupts, `TaskStop` stops background work); ZCode agrees (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/src/engine/types.ts:492,500).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, first.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-ROUTE-1 (`/pause`, the brief's row 7).

#### N-TOOL-1b — `fix(core): one per-tool bound for every loop — 120 s by default, a 600 s ceiling, a model-settable timeout_secs clamped; a template's timeout_seconds no longer the per-tool bound` · Required (B-11's value) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Today: the main loop cuts a tool at `[execution.agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs`, 60 s (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/daemon_config/execution.rs:36`; `config/daemon.toml:4`; read per turn since N-TOOL-1, `orchestrator/query_handler/simple_query_handler.rs:324-329`); the lead and every subagent at their template's `timeout_seconds`, else 60 s (`SandboxPolicy::from_constraints`, `security/sandbox/mod.rs:76`), that field also replacing the daemon default in their `LoopConfig` (`runner/agentic_loop/config.rs:244-250`); both skill tiers at their `LoopConfig`'s 60 s (`orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs:401`; `orchestrator/skill/invoke_executor.rs:466`); `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs` (300; `execution.rs:69`, `config/daemon.toml:13`) reaches no policy; `shell_execute` keeps its own 300 s (`SHELL_TIMEOUT_SECS`, `tools/builtins/shell_execute.rs:18`), capping its stated figure (`state_enforced_timeout`, `:63-64`) and its kill (`:84`). Now:
  - `agent_defaults.max_tool_runtime_secs` moves 60 → **120** and is the per-tool bound every loop reads — main loop, lead, every subagent, both skill tiers.
  - New key `[execution.agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_ceiling_secs = 600`.
  - The model may set `timeout_secs` on `shell_execute` and the `Command`-backend tools (`ToolRegistry::is_command_tool`, `tools/registry/mod.rs:1332`): clamped to the ceiling, never refused; absent, the bound is the default.
  - A template's `timeout_seconds` is the *task* budget and stops feeding the per-tool bound, in both places.
  - `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs` is removed — from `LeadAgentDefaults`, its clamp (`daemon_config/validation.rs:257-260`), the shipped `config/daemon.toml` and the CLI's config schema row (`daemon.execution.lead_max_tool_runtime_secs`, `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/daemon_config_cli/mod.rs:206-208`); a hand-edited `daemon.toml` still carrying it gets the retired-key boot WARN (`warn_on_removed_keys`, `daemon_config/mod.rs:78`).
  - `shell_execute`'s definition states the default and the ceiling, and its own 300 s bound yields to the ceiling (N-TOOL-1's one-number rule).
  - The manuals' figures and CLAUDE.md's B-11 line change in the same commit. Z1-18 (backgrounding a command at its budget) is not taken; R45's kill at the bound stands.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: a lead's and a subagent's policy whose template sets `timeout_seconds: 600` cut a command at `max_tool_runtime_secs` (120 by default), not 600; both skill tiers cut at the same figure; `shell_execute` with a `timeout_secs` above the ceiling runs under the ceiling (clamped, not refused), with a lower one under that one (at test-scale bounds, as N-MCP-2's gate ran); every loop's `shell_execute` definition states the figure its policy enforces and the ceiling; a `daemon.toml` carrying `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_tool_runtime_secs` boots with one WARN naming the key, and nothing reads it. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- security::sandbox tools::builtins runner::agentic_loop orchestrator::skill daemon_config`; `cargo test -p openalpaca` (the schema row). **Back-out.** Revert; the 60 s bound, the template's figure and the dead key return.
- **Needs.** P38; N-TOOL-1 (landed `f35eb584`). Decided by: Claude Code (the Bash tool's `timeout`, "default 120000, max 600000" ms, per call); ZCode agrees (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/bash-timeout-policy.ts:30-35; ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/bash.ts:11,40). It **reopens** B-11's taken deferral of the model-settable timeout; it is B-11's commit (plan-rev3 §5.4 B table: "rev 3.18: N-TOOL-1b, §5.5, after P38").
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-ROUTE-1.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-TOOL-1b (the brief's row 1); §5.4 B-11 row.

#### N-TOOL-8 — `fix(core): a top-level JSON-string call decoded once at the sandbox's entry, before the sanitizer` · Required · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** N-TOOL-5 (`ab7a2e65`) decodes a JSON string holding an object at the two provider sites only, and the registry decodes nothing; a string reaching the sandbox by another path — the plugin proxy (`runner/plugin_agent.rs`), the only producer — stays a string, which a schema asking for an object refuses. Now, at the sandbox's entry (`SandboxManager::execute_tool`, `security/sandbox/mod.rs:228`), before `sanitize_tool_args_with` (`:260`): a call whose arguments are a top-level JSON string that parses to an object is read as that object, once, with one WARN naming the tool and the source; a nested string is never decoded; then the sanitizer, the schema check and the card run on the decoded object. The registry still decodes nothing (N-TOOL-5's rule: nothing after the sanitizer decodes). CLAUDE.md's *Gotchas* bullet ("The registry decodes nothing") says so in the same commit.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: a proxied call whose arguments are the JSON string of an object reaches the tool as that object, with one WARN naming the tool and the source; a string holding a backtick, a line break, `../` and a NUL shows the sanitizer refusing the decoded object; a nested string field passes undecoded; the confirmation card shows the decoded object; N-TOOL-5's tests stay green, the registry decoding nothing. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- security::sandbox runner::plugin_agent tools::registry`. **Back-out.** Revert; a proxied JSON string is a string again.
- **Needs.** P38; N-TOOL-5 (landed `ab7a2e65`). Decided by: ZCode (Claude Code has no design; ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/input-normalization.ts:26-86; ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/executor/call-runner.ts:190-353); overrides the lead's recommendation (b), no decode.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-TOOL-1b.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-TOOL-8 (the brief's row 5).

#### N-LEAD-6 — `fix(core): a run's subagents bounded by a lifetime backstop, refused with its number` · Required · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Since N-LEAD-2 (`7461ab0b`) a spawn past the concurrency cap is queued, and nothing bounds how many subagents one run may queue. New key `[execution.lead_agent_defaults] max_subagents_per_run = 48` — `max_concurrent_subagents` (6; `daemon_config/execution.rs:71`) × the batch cap (8; `runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:1187`, `:1283`), a derived default, re-derived if either moves. A run's 49th spawn, by `spawn_subagent` or inside `spawn_subagents_batch`, is refused to the lead with the figure, and the lead prompt names the bound. The concurrency cap and N-LEAD-2's queueing are unchanged.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: a run that has spawned 48 subagents is refused the 49th, the refusal naming 48, by `spawn_subagent` or within a batch; the 48 accepted queue and run as N-LEAD-2 built; the lead prompt's golden names the bound. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::lead_agent daemon_config compose`. **Back-out.** Revert; a run's queue is unbounded again.
- **Needs.** P38; N-LEAD-2 (landed). Decided by: Claude Code for the form (a lifetime backstop far above normal use); the lead for the figure (ZCode has no bound by design, ZCode:docs/dynamic-workflow.md:237-238).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-TOOL-8; before SA-2 (same prompt and goldens).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-LEAD-6 (the brief's row 2).

#### N-LLM-1 — `fix(llm,core): per-key concurrency 8, a full batch; the unused capacity estimate deleted; the lead told what queues a spawn` · Required · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** `default_per_key_concurrency()` is 5 (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/routing/rate_limiter/mod.rs:418-420`), under the stock subagent bound of 6, so on a one-key install the sixth subagent reads `running` while its model call waits in the router. `LlmRouter::estimated_llm_capacity` (`routing/router/capacity.rs:25`) has no caller outside its tests (`routing/router/tests.rs:929`, `:951`, `:1004`) and its doc comment claims a use that does not exist (`:23-24`). The lead prompt says spawning is managed "based on available LLM capacity" (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/lead_agent/prompt.rs:137-140`), as do its `<tools>` line (`:160-161`) and `spawn_subagent`'s description (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:1852-1853`). Now: the default moves 5 → **8**; `estimated_llm_capacity` is deleted with its tests, and `LlmCapacityInfo` (`routing/router/types.rs:127`, re-exported at `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/lib.rs:55`) with it if nothing else names it; the three sentences say a spawn past the concurrency cap is queued; `docs/agent-loop.md` documents that a subagent waiting inside the router reads `running`; `docs/api` regenerated.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: a rate-limiter config with no `per_key_concurrency` reads 8; the lead prompt's goldens (today's sentences pinned at `compose/tests.rs:2794`, `:2816`) and `spawn_subagent`'s description say a spawn past the concurrency cap is queued and no longer speak of LLM capacity; `rg -n estimated_llm_capacity crates apps` finds nothing. `cargo test -p openalpaca_llm`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::lead_agent compose`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38. Decided by: the lead (neither reference has a design for this mismatch).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-LEAD-6; before SA-2.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-LLM-1 (the brief's row 3).

#### SA-2 — `feat(core): the subagent objective is a self-contained brief — the tool descriptions and the lead prompt say so` · Required · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** `spawn_subagent`'s and `spawn_subagents_batch`'s descriptions and the lead prompt say the `objective` is written as a self-contained **brief** — goal, inputs, constraints, what to return and where. Nothing caps it: the 500- and 50-character cuts (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:514`, `:850`) are the span record and a log line, unchanged. It stays the child's first user turn, never its system prompt, for a file-based (LLM) template; a plugin-backed template's executor is handed its objective appended to the instructions after the system prompt (`runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:909-914`), left as it is. A prompt-and-description commit, the cheapest of the four SA changes.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, failing on the parent: the lead prompt's golden and the two tools' description pins carry the brief's four parts; beside them a characterization holding before and after, for a file-based (LLM) template — a 4,000-character objective reaches the subagent whole as its user turn (`current_user_turn`, `runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:720`; a memory block or the working-folder line may be inserted before it, `:746-760`) and is absent from its system prompt (a plugin-backed template is the exception: `:909-914`, and again in the plugin context, `:915-918`). `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- runner::lead_agent compose`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** N-LEAD-6 and N-LLM-1 (same prompt and goldens). Decided by: Claude Code (the Agent tool's `prompt`); ZCode agrees (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/runtime/methods/subagent.ts:454-461). Does not wait for the subagent brief design.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-LLM-1.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 SA-2.

#### N-SKILL-5 — `fix(skills): the catalog lists every skill's description — a 20,000-character budget, a 250-character cut` · Required (`skills_catalog_budget`) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** `[orchestrator.prompt_budgets] skills_catalog_budget` defaults to 500 characters (`daemon_config/orchestrator.rs:390`; `config/daemon.toml:93-97`; clamped 100..=20000, `daemon_config/validation.rs:119-124`), each description cut at 100 (`AVAILABLE_SKILLS_DESCRIPTION_CHARS`, `orchestrator/skill/catalog/prompt.rs:26`). Now the default is **20,000** (the clamp's maximum); the per-skill cut moves **100 → 250**; the names-only fallback past the budget is kept; `config/daemon.toml`'s comment and value follow. *Found in the tree:* create-skill's description is 199 characters (`config/skills/create-skill/SKILL.md:5-8`), cut at 100 today and whole under 250, so a stock install's catalog grows by that line's remaining 99 characters — the body says so.
- **Gate.** Tests to write, each failing on the parent: a config with no `skills_catalog_budget` reads 20,000 (the pin `skills_catalog_budget_defaults_to_five_hundred`, `daemon_config/tests.rs:739`, moves with it); a twenty-skill catalog whose lines fit 20,000 characters lists every description, none names-only; a 200-character description is listed whole and a 300-character one cut at 250 with its ellipsis; the four stock skills' block differs from today's in create-skill's line alone, now whole. `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --lib -- orchestrator::skill daemon_config`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38; N-SKILL-1 (landed `5c9cdbf8`). Decided by: Claude Code, within ZCode's ceiling (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/context/sections/skills.ts:9-10,39-79: `DEFAULT_SKILL_METADATA_BUDGET = 20_000`, `MAX_DESCRIPTION_CHARS = 250`).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after SA-2.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-SKILL-5 (the brief's row 4).

#### N-EVENT-1 — `refactor(core,daemon): the log-only event variants deleted with their arms` · Required (telemetry's log-only events) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Deletes the `SystemEvent` variants nothing consumes — the bridge's log-only arms in `apps/openalpacad/src/event_bridge.rs:276-389`: `IntentClassified` (`:276`; declared `crates/openalpaca_core/src/events.rs:97`), `DispatchDecision` (`:325`; `events.rs:256`), `OrchestrationStage` (`:338`; `:270`), `SkillDiscovered` (`:350`; `:281`), `SkillSelected` (`:360`; `:291`), `SkillContextInjected` (`:380`; `:310`) — with their publish sites (`orchestrator/handlers.rs:103`, `:148`, `:437`; `orchestrator/handler_helpers.rs:108`; `orchestrator/dispatcher/mod.rs:246`; `orchestrator/skill/catalog/mod.rs:553`; `orchestrator/skill/router/mod.rs:224`; `orchestrator/skill/invocation.rs:174`) and bridge arms. None has a `ServerEvent` twin, so no persistence arm goes. `SkillInvocationStarted`, `SkillCompleted`, `SkillFailed` (forwarded, `eb.skill_*`) stay; the dispatch-decision record (`DispatchDecisionRepository`, `dispatcher/mod.rs:259-260`, served by `apps/openalpacad/src/routes/dispatch_decisions.rs`, read by the GUI's `useDispatchDecisions`) and the latency record `OrchestrationStage`'s publisher persists (`handlers.rs:426-437`) are not events and stay. `docs/api` regenerated. Any variant is re-added with a consumer. *For the commit to settle:* `OrchestrationStage` is read for `mode` and `ack_ms` by the two live-eval harnesses (`crates/openalpaca_core/tests/memory_eval.rs:3532`, `:7424`; `tests/steer_routing_eval.rs:298`), so the commit either re-points those reads to the persisted latency record or keeps that one variant under the re-add clause, and says which; the unit tests observing the deleted variants (`orchestrator/tests.rs:2389`, `:2393`, `:2455`, `:2634`, `:3143`, `:6709`; `orchestrator/memory_commands/tests.rs:497`; `tools/builtins/start_workflow.rs:417`) are re-pointed or go.
- **Gate.** The check that fails first is the count: `rg -n 'SystemEvent::(IntentClassified|DispatchDecision|OrchestrationStage|SkillDiscovered|SkillSelected|SkillContextInjected)' crates apps` finds the publish and match sites on the parent and none after (or `OrchestrationStage`'s alone, if kept); the workspace suite; the live-eval compile (`cargo check -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --tests`); `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` clean after the regeneration. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38. Decided by: the lead.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-SKILL-5.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-EVENT-1 (the brief's row 12).

#### SA-DESIGN — the subagent brief design: `tasks/subagent-brief-design.md` (a document, not a commit) · Required (SA-1, SA-3, SA-4: taken 2026-10-19; not built; needs a design) · design (the file does not exist yet)
- **Does.** Settles, from the answers:
  - **SA-1.** `general_agent` is the default when `agent_id` is omitted; the lead prompt stops matching "the best agent by skills" over nine personas (`runner/lead_agent/prompt.rs:137-138`) and says: general, unless the brief needs fewer powers (`explore_agent`, read-only and cheap) or more (`system_agent`); the specialist templates stay as capability profiles with shorter persona bodies. Because ruling L9's per-directory seeding gate (`apps/openalpacad/src/bootstrap/content.rs`) leaves an existing install with its old templates, SA-1 reaches installed stores only with **bundled defaults as the lowest-precedence scope** for agents and skills (Z3-06, Z2-06; Claude Code's shape) — which changes ruling L9.
  - **SA-3.** Templates stop pinning vendor model ids — all nine pin `claude-sonnet-4-6` or `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` (`config/agents/*.md`, `model:`), unroutable on a local-only install: `model: inherit` (the routed model) is the default, with tier words the router resolves; `spawn_subagent` gains a per-call `model` override, bounded to the router's routable models and still under the template's `max_cost_per_task`.
  - **SA-4.** An optional `result_schema` on `spawn_subagent`, validated with M82's `jsonschema`, a bounded repair, then a failure (Z4-16, A-46); the schema passes `strict_mode_compatible` before it is sent strict.
  - **Not changed:** delegation depth 3 (`MAX_SUBAGENT_DEPTH`, `runner/lead_agent/tools.rs:208`), the per-template capability ALLOW, the cost caps, no script engine, no per-workflow token ceiling (T13).
- **Gate.** None for the document. Each commit it shapes carries a test that fails on its parent, under the conventions above.
- **Needs.** P38. Decided by: SA-1 Claude Code (three built-in agent types, `general-purpose` the default; ZCode agrees, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/tool/handlers/agent.ts:169); SA-3 Claude Code (ZCode removed the per-call override, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/contracts/src/tools/agent.ts:18-33, and is not followed); SA-4 Claude Code (ZCode agrees, ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/dynamic-workflow/docs/execution-engine.md:216-256) — SA-4 **reopens** the batch-work row taken on 2026-10-17 and Z4-16's later-design row.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 3b, after N-EVENT-1 and before any of SA-1, SA-3 and SA-4's commits. `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §4.6 records it as pending work.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 *SA-1, SA-3, SA-4 — a design to write before their commits*.

#### Q12 — `fix(gui-shell): every command async; blocking work on spawn_blocking; the liveness probe on tokio` · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** `get_connection_info` and `read_daemon_log_tail` (a 64 KiB read) run on the UI thread today, and `ensure_daemon_running` blocks a runtime worker with file reads, a 300 ms connect per address and the spawn (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs:25-34`, `:101-106`, `:38-85`, `:254-268`; Tauri: "Commands without the async keyword are executed on the main thread"). Every command becomes `async`, file and process work moves to `tauri::async_runtime::spawn_blocking`, the probe to `tokio::net::TcpStream` + `tokio::time::timeout`. The workspace's tokio features (`Cargo.toml:52-60`) include `time` but not `net`, so the shell's line (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/Cargo.toml:17`) becomes `tokio = { workspace = true, features = ["net"] }` (enables a feature of a crate already in the graph; adds no dependency); the body records the `cargo tree -p openalpaca_gui -e features -i tokio` delta.
- **Gate.** Q11; G-GUI; G-bundle leg B (start) and leg A (stop). If this commit exercises leg A's start, it goes through plan-rev3 §5.2's confined in-app Start. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** Q11 (step 2, after P31a).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 4, first (after M57 and steps 3a–3b); before Q04.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q12 (rev 3.9; rev 3.13 C-15, MF-4).

#### Q13 — `refactor(gui-shell,gui): typed command errors` · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** One `#[derive(Debug, Serialize, thiserror::Error)] #[serde(tag = "kind", rename_all = "snake_case")]` shell error enum (`NotRunning`, `Expired`, `SpawnFailed { log_tail }`, `RevealRefused { message }`, …; Q04 adds `Mismatch { … }`), each carrying today's sentence byte for byte as `message`; `StopOutcome` serialized as a snake_case enum; `lib/connection.ts`, `lib/reveal.ts`, `lib/daemon-control.ts` read `kind` instead of `typeof cause === "string"`; `API_MAP.md` §1.1. The TS types stay hand-mirrored and pinned from both sides by test (tauri-specta is still `2.0.0-rc.25`, stable 1.0.2 — its 2.0 stable is the way out).
- **Gate.** Q11's round trip per variant; the TS suites; the sentences pinned as literals; G-GUI. **Back-out.** Revert (one commit, both sides).
- **Needs.** Q12.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 4, after Q12; before Q14 and Q04.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q13 (rev 3.9).

#### Q14 — `feat(gui-shell): the shell logs to state/logs/gui.log` · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** A `tracing-subscriber` initialised in `run()` writing `state/logs/gui.log` with the store's rotation (`rotate_log` / `read_log_tail`), Tauri's `log` records bridged; the shell's `tracing::info!`/`warn!` calls (`lib.rs:329`, `:340-342`, `:403-408`) reach a file for the first time. Ownership: the CLI's dev launcher (`apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:139-155`) truncates `gui.log` and pipes `bun run tauri dev` into it; it moves to `gui-dev.log`, appended. D1 kept: under `~/.openalpaca/state/logs`, never `~/Library/Logs`. Also (A-25, applied): on the app's `tauri::Builder`, `on_web_content_process_terminate` (tauri-2.12.3 `src/app.rs:1910`; macOS and iOS only) writes a line to `gui.log` and calls `Webview::reload` (`src/webview/mod.rs:1903`), and `std::panic::set_hook` writes a panic to `gui.log`; startup marks — T0 and the page-load marks (`Builder::on_page_load`, `src/app.rs:1848`) — go to `gui.log`; the webview's own marks reach the log through plan-rev3 §7.5's instrumented build or the console, never through a sixth command (a command would amend M74's five-command list in the same commit). If this commit has not taken the crash handler when M76 lands, M76 does.
- **Gate.** A spawn (G-bundle leg B) writes `gui.log` under the isolated store; a dev launch truncates nothing; G-GUI. Additions: G-bundle leg A — the window's `com.apple.WebKit.WebContent` process killed, the window reloads, `gui.log` has the line and the GUI reconnects to the same `instance_id`; a unit test installs the hook, panics inside `catch_unwind` and finds the line in `gui.log` under a scratch root named through `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` (the shell crate has no home-store guard of its own); a launch writes T0 and the page-load marks. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** Q13.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 4, after Q13; before Q21.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q14 (rev 3.9; rev 3.14 A-25, A-24; Z6-05, Z6-04, Z6-02).

#### Q21 — `feat(gui-shell,daemon): the app's daemon gets the login shell's PATH, captured once, a fixed list its fallback` · Required (*App PATH* (a) with (c) as its fallback) · scheduled
- **Does.** At start the shell captures the person's login environment once, asynchronously (the window's first paint never waits on it): the account's login shell run as a non-interactive login command printing a start marker, the environment and an end marker; a deadline stated in the body; the child in its own process group, killed with the group at the deadline; an allowlist of keys taken from between the markers (ZCode's form, ZCode:packages/services/src/runtime-tools/runtimeLoginShellEnvCapture.ts:6-11, :54-101, :173-216, a design, never ported). `spawn_daemon` (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:344-350` at `476f1f81`) waits for the capture at most until its deadline and sets the captured `PATH` on the sidecar's command; on failure or timeout it sets (c)'s fixed list — `/opt/homebrew/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin` and `~/.cargo/bin`. The allowlist is `PATH` alone (the credential-pointer keys are N-DAEMON-2's); API keys are never inherited this way, so `secret_env` stays a terminal-launch feature. **The announcement:** `PATH`'s source (`login-shell` or `fallback-list`) and the capture's duration — never its value — go to `gui.log` (Q14) and, through one variable the shell sets beside `MANAGED_LOG_ENV`, to one boot line in `daemon.log` (the commit's only daemon change), a daemon started from a terminal logging `inherited`; Settings → Connection shows the same for a daemon this app spawned. The carrier to the page is this commit's to name — a field on an existing command's response, or a new command, which amends M74's five-command list in the same commit — never a field of the daemon's API. The body states the deadline, the carrier and the capture time measured on the reference machine (scenario (1)'s daemon-ready figure carries its cost at Q10); P38's beta limitation (A-41) is rewritten to what this commit does, and `tasks/pending-decisions.md`'s B3 line with it.
- **Gate.** Unit tests on an injected login shell and a scratch `HOME`, `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` pointed at a scratch root (the test module sets it itself, behind one lock; no test reads or writes `~/.openalpaca`): a script printing noise around the markers yields the captured `PATH` and nothing else (an `OPENAI_API_KEY` and an `AWS_PROFILE` it exports are absent from the sidecar's environment); one sleeping past the deadline is killed with its process group (its child's pid gone) and the fallback list used; one exiting non-zero gives the fallback; the announcement names the source and the duration and never the value. Natively, in G-bundle leg B's form with a scratch `HOME` whose `.zprofile` prepends `$T/bin` (the app's store under `$T/home`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/store/mod.rs:79-98`): the app started as `env -i HOME=$T/home PATH=/usr/bin:/bin …/Contents/MacOS/openalpaca_gui` (standing in for a Finder launch, plan-rev3 Appendix B #52) spawns a daemon whose `daemon.log` names `login-shell` and whose stdio MCP stub, a bare program found only in `$T/bin`, connects; with the login shell made to hang, the same launch names `fallback-list`. G-GUI; Q11's suite. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** Q14 (`gui.log`); *App PATH* (a) with (c) fallback, taken 2026-10-17.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 4, after Q14 and before Q04 (which rewrites the locator beside `spawn_daemon`).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q21 (rev 3.16; the ZCode audit's A-24; Z6-02; rev 3.18 note on the credential-pointer keys).

#### N-DAEMON-2 — `feat(gui-shell,daemon): the credential-pointer keys reach the app's daemon only through an owner-edited list` · Required (*App PATH*'s credential-pointer keys) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Q21 passes `PATH` alone. Now `AWS_PROFILE`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` and `KUBECONFIG` reach the app's daemon only through a new owner-edited list, `[daemon.env] passthrough_keys = []` — key names only, never values, empty by default. A listed key present in the capture is set on the sidecar's command beside `PATH`, and Q21's announcement (`gui.log`, the boot line in `daemon.log`, Settings → Connection) names the keys passed, by name, never their values. *Found in the tree:* nothing under `apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri` reads `daemon.toml`, so how the list reaches `spawn_daemon` (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:344-350`) is this commit's to name in its body. The manuals' *App PATH* sentence (Q21's) follows.
- **Gate.** Tests on Q21's harness (an injected login shell, a scratch `HOME` and home store): a login shell exporting `AWS_PROFILE` and `KUBECONFIG` with the list empty passes neither (Q21's own case, true on the parent too); with `passthrough_keys = ["AWS_PROFILE"]` the sidecar's environment holds `AWS_PROFILE` and not `KUBECONFIG` (failing on the parent); the announcement names `AWS_PROFILE` and never its value (failing on the parent). G-GUI; Q11's suite. **Back-out.** Revert; `PATH` alone again.
- **Needs.** Q21. Decided by: Claude Code (`settings.json`'s `env` is a map the user sets); ZCode passes the three by name (ZCode:packages/services/src/runtime-tools/runtimeCommandEnv.ts:48-106) and is not followed.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 4, right after Q21, before Q04.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-DAEMON-2 (the brief's row 8); §7.3 Q21's rev 3.18 note.

#### Q04 — `feat(daemon,gui): the daemon compatibility contract — protocol constants, /v1/health.protocol, additive ConnectionInfo fields, decide_reuse` · Recommended (DU-29 a) · scheduled
- **Does.** Rust: `support::protocol` (written in storage here; moved by Q05), `make_discovery` reads it, `/v1/health` gains `protocol`, `ConnectionInfo` gains `version`/`protocol`, the shell's `ensure_daemon_running` goes through `decide_reuse` with the two-step probe (the hand-written GET under (a-i)), the `Mismatch` rejection — `Mismatch { daemon_version, daemon_protocol, shell_protocol, remedy }` a variant of Q13's typed error enum, its `message` the sentence Q04 writes; the health probe on `tokio::net::TcpStream` + `tokio::time::timeout` (Q12). TS: `ConnectionInfo` fields, `ConnectionError.kind = "mismatch"`, the parked ladder, the Connection-panel note. The `PROTOCOL + 1` override is compiled only under `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` or a test-only feature every release build leaves off (C-20).

#### M60 — `refactor: CancellationToken::run_until_cancelled at the two-arm races` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `tokio-util = { version = "0.7.16", features = ["rt"] }` (`Cargo.toml:127`) — written as the version the lock holds when M60 lands, `0.7.20` at D1 (a)'s cut, 0.7.16 named in the body as the API's minimum. The 28 eligible two-arm `tokio::select!` races between a future and `token.cancelled()` → `let Some(x) = token.run_until_cancelled(fut).await else { … };` (or `.is_none()` for a sleep): `apps/openalpacad/src/background.rs:20`, `:45`, `:77`, `:192`, `:302`, `:340`, `:357`, `:377`; `hot_reload.rs:136`, `:676`, `:731`, `:789`; `event_bridge.rs:22`; `main.rs:681`; `notification/mod.rs:69`; `credential_discovery/mod.rs:127`; `memory/task_extraction/mod.rs:303`; `runner/plugin_agent.rs:251`, `:282`; `runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:852`, `:1156`, `:1454`; `prompt_ctx/compaction/graduated.rs:99`; `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:603`; `discord/connector.rs:301`, `:351`; `imessage/connector.rs:148`, `:190`. The bias (`tokio-util-0.7.19/src/sync/cancellation_token.rs:300-313`, `:457-469`): one up-front `is_cancelled()` check, then the **future** polled first — the 26 unbiased sites become deterministically future-first; the two token-first `biased;` sites (`client.rs:603`, `task_extraction/mod.rs:303`) are read one by one and keep `select!` where the token must win. `hot_reload.rs:136`'s arm is refutable (`:137`): through `run_until_cancelled` a closed channel is `Some(None)` and the converted loop **breaks** on it — stated in the body. Excluded and kept: `apps/openalpacad/src/routes/chat.rs:550` (D-E's `sse_frames` contract) and the three-arm `memory/learning/queue.rs:1715`, `routes/events.rs:96`. Also (A-35, Z4-12) the plugin agent's two RPCs, `executor.spawn` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/runner/plugin_agent.rs:142-145`) and `executor.step` (`:198`), today bounded only by the plugin channel's 60 s default (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/process_pool.rs:85-90`): where a token is present (`plugin_agent.rs:139`) both take `run_until_cancelled`, a cancel ending as the loop's existing arms end it (`executor.stop(instance_id)`, `PluginLoopOutcome::Failed { error: "Cancelled" }`, `:259-263`) — thirty sites with these two. Each site's ordering stated in the body. N-LEAD-2 (landed) already acquires its permit in this form. Memory code: read first (§1).

- **Needs.** P38 (Part III's source rule; the entry reads "any time").
- **Place.** Merged-order step 6, last.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M12.

**Step 6b — the plugin fixes, before the plugin traits move**

← modernization-plan §6.3 step 6b; plan-rev3 §5.4 placement rules (c), (d)

#### N-PLUG-1 — `fix(plugins): an isError result is an error` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `PluginToolProxy::execute` joins `content[].text` and returns `Ok` whatever `isError` says (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/tool_bridge.rs:46-66`), while the MCP bridge maps `is_error` to `Err` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:102-104`). Now `isError: true` maps to `Err(joined text)`, and the bridge's comment states that a plugin's `tools/call` result follows MCP's `CallToolResult`.
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a plugin stub answering a single-frame `{content: [{type: "text", text: "boom"}], isError: true}` yields `Err("boom")`. `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 6b, first; before M58 (a) (A-34). `tool_bridge.rs` is in no commit of the plan.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 Plugins N-PLUG-1 (Z2-02).

#### N-PLUG-3 — `docs(plugins): the approve surface says what approving grants` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** The GUI manual says "`Approve` grants exactly those strings" (`docs/GUI_Manual.md:1332`; also `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/ext.rs:602`, `:1144`), while the child is spawned as the owner, with the daemon's environment and its own directory as working directory (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/process_pool.rs:36-48`). Now one sentence sits under the approve preview in the CLI and the GUI, and in both manuals, in substance: approving starts the plugin's program as you, with the daemon's environment (less the master-key variable, Stage 0's F3) and full file and network access; its capabilities name what it contributes to agents, not what it can do. It cross-references `docs/Daemon_Manual.md:185`. B-04 is taken (its minimal form): the environment clause changes in B-04's commit, after P38. ZCode states the same boundary (ZCode:docs/plugin-hooks-compat.md:23-29).
- **Gate.** The CLI's approve-preview test and the GUI's preview test pin the sentence; `cargo test -p openalpaca`; the four bun gates. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P38.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 6b, after N-PLUG-1.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 Plugins N-PLUG-3 (Z2-05).

#### N-PLUG-5 — `fix(plugins): mcp_compatible plugins get the real MCP lifecycle` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** When `mcp_compatible` is true, the manager skips `initialize` and sends `tools/list` first (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:1165-1190`, `:1518-1521`), and an rmcp server refuses any first message other than `initialize` (rmcp-0.16.0 `src/service/server.rs:195-201`); the `tests/fixtures/echo-plugin` fixture is a tolerant shell script. Now such a plugin runs the lifecycle `openalpaca_mcp` runs after P19; the manuals state that an `mcp_compatible` plugin receives no `[config]`, and the validate step warns if one declares config keys (N-PLUG-2's warning path). (Z2-08's option (a); (b), deleting the flag, is the owner's alternative if Z2-07 is declined.)
- **Gate.** A test that fails on the parent: a plugin fixture that refuses any first message but the lifecycle's, as an rmcp server does (scripted, so no rmcp server feature enters the lock), loads and lists its tools; the echo fixture still loads. `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P19 (whose lifecycle it reuses); N-PLUG-2 (landed `eb6b41fc`, the warning path).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 6b, last.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 Plugins N-PLUG-5 (Z2-08).

**Step 7 — the `async-trait` series: M86, M13, M55 (a–d), M58 (a–j), M59**

M86 first, so the series converts nothing a taken decision deletes — M58 (e)'s plugin impl included. Under DU-R1 (b) + enums for the closed set (§2): M55 (a–d), M58 (a–j), M59. ← modernization-plan §6.3 step 7; §6.2 DU-R1

#### M86 — `refactor(llm,plugins): the plugin LLM provider removed, as D-I (revised) rules` · Required (the owner's D-I (revised), taken 2026-09-23) · scheduled
- **Does.** Deletes `PluginLlmProvider` (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/provider_bridge.rs`, 255 lines, with its re-exports `bridge/mod.rs:11` and `lib.rs:13`), `PluginManager::get_plugin_provider` (`manager.rs:2439-2459`), the `provider/info` probe (`manager.rs:1263-1294`) and the state only it fills (`registered_provider`, `registered_models`), `ProviderType::Plugin(String)` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/keys/key_pool/mod.rs:16`, its `Display` arm `:48`) and the two `plugin:` parsers (`config/llm_config/mod.rs:29-31`, `config/settings_service.rs:1284-1286`). Kept: the manifest's `types.provider` key — such a plugin installs and approves, its tools, skills and agents work, and it gets D-I's one warning in the validate dry run, the install preview and the log. M86 rewrites N-PLUG-2's warning string (which says the bridge is not wired) to a sentence true when it lands: the plugin provider type is removed, and LLM providers are the built-in ones, configured in `llm.toml` or in Settings → Models & keys. The extension row's `provider` field (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/extensions/ledger.rs:182`, filled at `manager.rs:1799`) then reads `null` on every row and stays in the wire shape beside `connector` until the plugin connector bridge's deletion (taken (a); needs a plan entry), where whether both fields leave `GET /v1/extensions` is presented. *M86's own question, taken (a):* a `[providers."plugin:<name>"]` section in a hand-written `llm.toml` is skipped with one warning (a recognizer of the prefix kept for the warning alone), every other unknown name keeping its refusal (`LlmError::UnknownProvider`, `config/llm_config/router_builder.rs:61-62`; `enabled = false` at `:57-59`). The body states that this is D-I applied and names every place a `ProviderType` is written (it derives `Serialize`), showing none holds a plugin variant. CLAUDE.md's *Extensibility* sentence on the unwired provider bridge updated in the same commit (its connector half left to Z2-12's commit).
- **Gate.** `rg -n 'PluginLlmProvider|get_plugin_provider|ProviderType::Plugin|registered_provider' crates apps` → 0, and `"plugin:"` left only in the warn-and-skip and its test; a fixture plugin with `types.provider = true` installs, approves and registers its tools, with M86's sentence in the three places and N-PLUG-2's "not wired" in none; an `llm.toml` with an enabled `plugin:` section builds the router with one warning, and one with `[providers.bogus]` still refuses; `cargo test -p openalpaca_plugins -p openalpaca_llm`; `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check` (regenerated if a public item's doc changed). **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** N-PLUG-2 (landed `eb6b41fc`) and P38; D-I (revised) (`tasks/llm-simplify/design.md:18`, untracked; `tasks/pending-decisions.md` §3.7, §4.1); M86's `llm.toml` question taken (a) in modernization-plan rev 2.5.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 7, at its head; before M13 and M58 (e).
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M86 (rev 2.3, the ZCode audit's A-34, Z2-12; rev 2.5).

#### M13 — `refactor(connectors,plugins): native async fn in trait for Connector` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `crates/openalpaca_connectors/src/lib.rs:41-56`: the attribute and its `use` removed, `run` and `shutdown` become `fn …(&self) -> impl Future<Output = Result<(), ConnectorError>> + Send`; `telegram/connector.rs:223`, `discord/connector.rs:722`, `imessage/connector.rs:366`, `crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/bridge/connector_bridge.rs:49`: the attribute and unused `use` lines removed, the `async fn` bodies kept. `async-trait` stays in every manifest (other traits use it). The plugin connector bridge has no production caller; its deletion is taken (a) (Z2-12's connector half) and needs a plan entry — if that commit lands before M13, M13 has no attribute in `connector_bridge.rs` to convert.
- **Gate.** `cargo check -p openalpaca_connectors -p openalpaca_plugins --all-targets --all-features`; `cargo test -p openalpaca_connectors --all-features`; clippy 0 new (no `async_fn_in_trait` warning, since the `impl Future + Send` form is used); G-CI. **Back-out.** Revert (five files).
- **Needs.** M86; after Part I (conflict avoidance); before any connector-bridge wiring.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 7, after M86.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M13 (rev 2.0; rev 2.3 Z2-12, A-34; rev 2.5).

#### M55 (a–d) — `refactor(<crate>): enum dispatch for <Trait>` ×4 · Required (DU-R1) · scheduled
- **Does.** One commit per trait, each removing the trait's `#[async_trait]` and its impls' attributes (`async-trait` stays in every manifest):
  - **(a)** `Transport` (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/mod.rs:23`; `TransportKind` is already an enum, so `build_transport`'s `Box<dyn Transport>` at `client.rs:619` becomes an enum-dispatched `connect`, ≈ 30 lines); the `#[cfg(test)]` `MockTransport` (`transport/mod.rs:88-128`) becomes a test-only enum variant.
  - **(b)** `PersonaHandler` (`core/src/tools/builtins/update_persona/mod.rs:33`; an enum over soul/user/identity replaces `&dyn PersonaHandler` at `:81`, ≈ 20 lines).
  - **(c)** `EventWatcher` (`wake/src/watcher/mod.rs:11`; one impl today, `Vec<Box<dyn EventWatcher>>` at `wake/manager/mod.rs:19`).
  - **(d)** `ExtensionSupervisor` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/extensions/mod.rs:423`): its one `&dyn` site, `apps/openalpacad/src/managers/extensions.rs:114`, becomes an enum in the daemon over `McpSupervisor` (`managers/mcp.rs:2032`) and `PluginManager` (`crates/openalpaca_plugins/src/manager.rs:2473`); the trait stays in core with its methods in M13's form — `fn …(&self, …) -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send` (D-17); ADR-030's two supervisors keep their lifecycles.
- **Gate.** The crate's tests; clippy 0 new; `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp` with the stdio fixture for (a); for (d), the extension routes' and `managers::mcp`'s tests. **Back-out.** Revert the one commit.
- **Needs.** M13; before M58 (so these four never take dynosaur).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 7, after M13.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M55 (rev 1.1; Required since rev 2.0; rev 2.2 D-17).

#### M58 (a–j) — `refactor(<crate>): native async fn in trait + dynosaur for <Trait>` ×10 · Required (DU-R1 (b)) · scheduled
- **Does.** One trait per commit, crate order api → llm → core: **(a)** `PluginToolExecutor` — and `dynosaur = "0.3.1"` into `[workspace.dependencies]` and the crate's manifest (0.3.1, 2026-07-03, rust-version 1.84, the newest eligible at D1 (a)'s cut) — **(b)** `ToolCallbackExecutor`, **(c)** `PluginSkillExecutor`, **(d)** `PluginAgentExecutor` (`crates/openalpaca_api/src/plugin_traits.rs:8`, `:31`, `:39`, `:66`), **(e)** `LlmProvider` (`crates/openalpaca_llm/src/lib.rs:63`), **(f)** `BuiltInTool` (`core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:318`), **(g)** `MessageHandler` (`core/src/gateway/router/mod.rs:194`), **(h)** `ConnectorSendProvider` (`core/src/orchestrator/mod.rs:85`), **(i)** `ContextSource` (`core/src/prompt_ctx/sources/mod.rs:31`), **(j)** `Summarizer` (`core/src/context_budget/compaction.rs:11`). Each: methods declared `fn m(&self, …) -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send`, `Future` spelled `std::future::Future` or the prelude's, never `futures::Future` (dynosaur's macro does not box it; D-12); `#[dynosaur::dynosaur(pub DynX = dyn(box) X)]` on the trait; the 10 default `async fn` bodies (`BuiltInTool` 3; `LlmProvider` 5 — `chat_with_key`, `list_models_with_key`, `discover_models`, `chat_streaming`, `chat_streaming_with_key`, `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/lib.rs:81-133`; `MessageHandler` 1; `ConnectorSendProvider` 1) as `fn … -> impl Future<Output = T> + Send { async move { … } }`; every impl loses the attribute and keeps its `async fn` body; every `dyn X` becomes `DynX<'_>` / `Arc<DynX<'static>>`, built with `DynX::new_arc` / `from_arc` / `from_ref` (`dynosaur_derive` 0.3.1 `lib.rs:508-547`) — every construction site changes, not only the ≈ 89 `dyn` uses; each commit counts its trait's construction sites in its body, and a registration API taking `impl X` and building the `DynX` itself is preferred; the `use async_trait::async_trait;` lines go. A dynamic call boxes as `async-trait` did; a static call no longer boxes. (e) converts no plugin implementation (M86 deleted `PluginLlmProvider`). (a)–(d) land after N-PLUG-6 and N-PLUG-1.
- **Gate, each.** `cargo check --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets --locked`; the crate's tests (the workspace's for (e) and (f)); clippy 0 new; a grep that the trait has no `where Self: Sized` method and no associated const (dynosaur refuses both). (e) and (f) budget for higher-ranked "`Send` is not general enough" errors; (f)'s compile time recorded against its parent. (a)'s `Cargo.lock` entry follows the freshness rule (§1). **Back-out.** Revert the one commit.
- **Needs.** P19 and P38, M13 and M55 (a–d); M86 (before (e)); N-PLUG-6 (landed `1a5512dc`) and N-PLUG-1 (before (a)); DU-R1 (b).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 7, after M55 (d).
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M58 (rev 2.0; rev 2.2 D-12; rev 2.3 A-34; rev 2.4).

#### M14 — `feat(core): the MCP bridge serializes structured_content and prints resource-link URIs` · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** `tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-115`: whenever `structured_content` is `Some`, the JSON pretty-printed is **appended** after the text blocks as one more block — **unless a text block already parses (`serde_json::from_str::<Value>`) to the same value**, in which case nothing is added (an rmcp-built server mirrors the compact `value.to_string()` into `content`); a summary in `content` with the data only in `structured_content` loses nothing; `ContentBlock::ResourceLink(r)` (rmcp 3's name, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/model/content.rs:259-265`) → `[resource link: <name> — <uri>]` (name omitted when absent) instead of `[non-text content: resource_link]`. `is_error` handling unchanged. Under DU-R4 (vi): `Tool.output_schema` (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/model/tool.rs:30`) carried on the registered tool and served by `GET /v1/tools` (the bridge reads only `input_schema` today, `tools/mcp/bridge.rs:42`) — never placed in a provider's tool definition (`strict_mode_compatible` stays the rule); `docs/api` regenerated; validation is M82. Images, audio and embedded blobs keep their placeholders (M85 keeps them). Written over N-MCP-4's mapped names (both edit `bridge.rs:33-42`).
- **Gate.** New unit tests on four shapes: structured only → the JSON; both and equal (compact text, pretty JSON) → the text only, nothing twice; a summary text plus a different structured value → the summary then the JSON; a link → name and URI; the existing bridge tests green; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core tools::mcp`; `output_schema` in `GET /v1/tools`; `gen_api_docs.py --check` clean. **Back-out.** Revert.

#### M85 — `feat(core): MCP images, audio and embedded blobs kept as by-products` · DU-bound (DU-R6 (b)) · scheduled
- **Does.** Today an image becomes a placeholder sentence and audio or an embedded resource its bracketed kind name (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/mcp/bridge.rs:84-104`). Under DU-R6 (b): each image, audio and embedded-blob block (rmcp-3.5.1's `ContentBlock::{Image, Audio, Resource}`, `src/model/content.rs:261-263`; a blob is `ResourceContents::BlobResourceContents`, `src/model/resource.rs:182-188`) is base64-decoded and saved through `put_tool_artifact` (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/builtins/artifact_write.rs:183`) as a by-product of the turn's project or of none — never asked (ST-15) — within `[execution.artifacts] max_artifact_bytes`, its kind from its MIME type by the store's existing rule; never image-decoded, transcoded or compressed. The block's text names the artifact's id, its absolute path, the MIME type and the size; a block over the limit, or not valid base64, says nothing was saved and why; with no `ToolContext`, or no store, the text says nothing was saved (A-33). An embedded text resource stays text. **Plumbing (the widening DU-R6 presents):** the registry holds no database (`ToolRegistry`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:438-449`) and `serialize_call_result` is pure, so M85 gives the registry's MCP arm (`:832-852`) a by-product sink the daemon sets at boot — the database and the daemon config `put_tool_artifact` takes (`artifact_write.rs:183-189`) — and one `pub(crate)` wrapper in `tools/builtins/mod.rs` that takes the `ToolContext` and the sink and calls `scope_from_context` (`artifact_write.rs:61`) and then `put_tool_artifact` (`:183`); both stay `pub(super)` (`artifact_write` is a private module, `tools/builtins/mod.rs:3`).
- **Gate.** A stub server returning an image, an audio clip, an embedded blob, an over-limit image and invalid base64: three by-products whose bytes equal the decoded input byte for byte, two "nothing saved" lines; a call with no context saves nothing and says so; `artifact_read` of a saved id returns it; the Library lists the three and no card was raised; `cargo test -p openalpaca_core tools::mcp`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** M14 (whose serializer it extends); DU-R6 (b), taken 2026-10-17 (under (a) M85 is dropped and the placeholders stay).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 8, after M14.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M85 (rev 2.3; rev 2.5).

#### N-MCP-5 — `feat(mcp): a stdio server's stderr kept as a bounded, redacted tail on its failure` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** `TokioChildProcess::new(cmd)` inherits stderr (`crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/transport/stdio.rs:79`; rmcp-3.5.1 `src/transport/child_process.rs:124-130`), so a server's stderr goes raw into `daemon.log`, unattributed, unbounded and unredacted, and a Failed row's detail is the rmcp error alone (`apps/openalpacad/src/managers/mcp.rs:1132-1136`). Now: spawn with `TokioChildProcess::builder(cmd).stderr(Stdio::piped())`, and a reader task that always drains the pipe, keeping the last 4 KB per connection; redact by the exact secret values this load resolved, plus generic patterns, in OpenAlpaca's own code (not a port of ZCode's table); attach the tail to the Failed detail and the crash warnings, wrapped with `wrap_untrusted_context` where it enters a tool result or a status rendering, and escaped with `escape_hidden` where a person sees it. Builds the extension design's rule for an MCP child's stderr (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md:1855-1859`). ZCode keeps a redacted 4,000-character tail, on failures only (ZCode:apps/zcode-cli/packages/adapters/src/mcp/index.ts:110).
- **Gate.** Tests that fail on the parent: a stub that writes a known secret value and 10 KB to stderr and exits leaves a Failed detail of at most 4 KB that ends with the stub's last line and shows the secret redacted; a stub writing 1 MB to stderr still answers `tools/list` (the pipe is drained); a planted U+202E in stderr is escaped on the row. `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp --features test-utils`; `cargo test -p openalpacad -- managers::mcp routes::extensions`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P19 (rmcp 3.5's builder; P19's prep rewrites `stdio.rs` with the process-group wrapper, which this composes with).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 8, after SA-4.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 The MCP client N-MCP-5 (Z5-09).

#### M15 — `feat(mcp): protocol-level cancellation, the MRTR loop and progress-reset timeouts through send_cancellable_request` · DU-bound (DU-R4 (v) (b); DU-R4 (i)) · scheduled
- **Does.**
  - *The raw path.* `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:558`, `:585-593`: `running.call_tool(params)` under `tokio::time::timeout` → `peer.send_cancellable_request(ClientRequest::CallToolRequest(..), …)`; a `ServerResult::CallToolResult` is the result, any variant but the two below `UnexpectedResponse`; the "later refinement" comment replaced by the contract.
  - *The MRTR loop (DU-R4 (v) (b)).* A ≤ `DEFAULT_MRTR_MAX_ROUNDS` (10) loop that answers each `InputRequiredResult` through `ClientHandler` (elicitation's `Decline` until M84 lands, then M84's form), each round a `send_cancellable_request` the token can cancel; past the 10th round an error naming the cap. rmcp 3.5 has no cancellable `call_tool` that runs the loop (`prepare_input_required_retry` is private, `rmcp-3.5.0/src/service/client.rs:2098-2130`), so M15 copies the loop (≈ 60 lines); way out: an rmcp issue the owner posts, and M15's copy goes once a release carrying it is eligible.
  - *Timeouts (DU-R4 (i)).* The request carries `PeerRequestOptions { timeout: Some(request_timeout), reset_timeout_on_progress: true, max_total_timeout: Some(max_total_timeout) }` (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:772-806`), `max_total_timeout_secs` a new `config/mcp.toml` key (proposed default 300, beside `request_timeout_secs = 30`, `config/mcp.toml:11`); `ClientHandler::on_progress` logged at debug (no new event variant); `docs/Daemon_Manual.md`'s `mcp.toml` table names the key. Progress resets only rmcp's idle clock, inside the bound (DU-R4 (i)'s sub-question taken (a)); the sandbox's contract is unchanged.
  - *(a) The cancel always reaches the server (A-32; Z5-06, Z5-02).* `ToolContext` gets a cancellation token for the call — a child of the loop's (`runner/agentic_loop/mod.rs:434`), cancelled by the sandbox when its deadline passes — which the registry's MCP arm (today passing no token, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/tools/registry/mod.rs:852`) passes to `McpClient::call_tool`. Since `await_response` and `cancel` take the handle by value (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:555`, `:666`) and `RequestHandle` has no `Drop` (`:542-550`), the in-flight request holds a guard — a clone of the handle's public `peer` and `id` — the one sender: the response awaited in a `select!` against the token, and a token that fires, or a drop before `await_response` returns, sends one `notifications/cancelled` naming that id. The guard is disarmed whenever the handle is consumed (a response, rmcp's own timeout — which has sent its own notification, `send_timeout_cancel_notification`, `:570`, `:636`, `:646` — or a closed transport, or `cancel` sent), so a request id sees at most one `notifications/cancelled`. A dropped `await_response` leaves rmcp's progress watcher (`:687-698`; `:901-907`) until a later progress notice (`:1018-1022`) or the connection ends — named beside the rmcp issue. The sandbox's bound itself is unchanged.
  - *(b) One bound, the sandbox's.* `max_tool_runtime_secs` is 60 s for an agent and 300 s for the lead (`config/daemon.toml:4`, `:13`), so the proposed `max_total_timeout_secs = 300` would bind only the lead, and race it there. The sandbox passes the call's own bound on the same context, and each request takes `max_total_timeout = min(max_total_timeout_secs, that bound less a margin the body states)`, so rmcp's own clock fires first and sends its `notifications/cancelled` itself; the manuals' `mcp.toml` table says the smaller wins. N-TOOL-1b (§3.3 step 3b; plan-rev3 §5.5) changes the per-tool bound before M15 lands (120 s by default, a 600 s ceiling, the lead key removed), so the 60 s and 300 s figures here and the gate's 60 s are re-read at M15's commit.
  - *Contract kept from N-MCP-1 and N-MCP-3:* a request that may have reached the server is never sent again — each MRTR round is its own request, sent once — a dead connection is reconnected once before sending, inside the call's one deadline, and the request goes out on a `Peer` cloned out of the service lock.
- **Gate.** P19's real-server round trip; with the stdio fixture: a cancelled call emits `notifications/cancelled` with our reason, a timed-out one emits rmcp's, an `InputRequiredResult` round is answered through the handler (the Decline until M84) and the round past the 10th reported as the cap's error; a stub reporting progress past `request_timeout` completes; one past `max_total_timeout_secs` is cut with rmcp's `notifications/cancelled`; one input-required round answered and the server's final answer returned; a stub holding a call past the sandbox's bound, one ended by a run's `/cancel` and one cut by rmcp's own timeout each record exactly one `notifications/cancelled` naming the request's id, and a call answered records none; an agent-policy call under `max_total_timeout_secs = 300` is cut by rmcp's clock before the sandbox's 60 s; `cargo test -p openalpaca_mcp`; `gen_api_docs.py --check` if a doc comment changed. **Back-out.** Revert (the local bail-out returns).
- **Needs.** P19 (rmcp 3.5.x); N-MCP-1 and N-MCP-3 (Phase G's head, step 2), over whose call path it is written; DU-R4 (v) (b) and (i), taken.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 8, after N-MCP-5.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M15 (rev 2.0; rev 2.2 E-15; rev 2.3 A-32; rev 2.5).

#### M17 — `build: std::fs::File::{lock, try_lock} replace the file-lock crate` · DU-bound (DU-R2 a) · scheduled
- **Does.** `storage/discovery/mod.rs:187-205` (the singleton lock; `block` → `lock()` vs `try_lock()`), `core/config_io.rs:241-256`, `llm/keys/key_encryption/mod.rs:299-315`: a `struct … (File)` guard whose drop closes the file; `TryLockError::WouldBlock` mapped where `file-lock`'s `os error 35/11` was; `file-lock` removed from `Cargo.toml:99` and the three crate manifests. The body records the primitive change (`fcntl` → `flock`), the mixed-build window and that "the process, then the lock, never the port" is unchanged. *Exclusion inside one process (D-06):* a `flock` lock belongs to the open file description, so after M17 two writers of one config file in the same process exclude each other (closing a latent lost-update race) and a path that takes a lock it already holds now **blocks**. So M17 audits every caller for re-acquisition (`atomic_write_with_backup`'s callers hold `<path>.lock` already; `persist_only` holds it through `acquire_config_write_lock`); rewrites the comments stating the semantics — `crates/openalpaca_core/src/config_io.rs:138-141`, `crates/openalpaca_llm/src/config/settings_service.rs:36-37`, `apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:150-156`; adds one test with concurrent in-process writers (every write survives); and states the bound in the body (a blocked writer waits for the holder's one read-modify-write, `lock()` has no timeout, the test fails on its own timeout rather than hanging).
- **Gate.** The CLI's cross-process `LockHolder` test (`apps/openalpaca/src/test_util.rs:160-245`); `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage -p openalpaca_core -p openalpaca_llm`; a manual second-daemon start against an isolated store (sandboxed, plan-rev3 §5.2) refused with the new wording; `cargo tree -i file-lock` empty; the `LockFileEx` leg only if Windows is ever built (DU-33). **Back-out.** Revert and `cargo update -p file-lock --precise 2.1.11`.
- **Needs.** P03 (and P38); DU-R2 (a) (under (b) M17 is dropped).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 8, last.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M17 (rev 2.2 D-06).

**Step 9 — the `thiserror` series M19–M38**

← modernization-plan §6.3 step 9 (rev 2.7: the rewritten Q06 moves neither file M19/M20 touch; M21–M23 after their `{:#}` golden tests)

#### M19–M38 — `refactor(<crate>): thiserror derive for <Type>` ×20 · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** One type per commit, squashable by crate at review; storage then core: **M19** `ProjectError` (`store/projects.rs:342-375`), **M20** `RevealRefusal` (`store/reveal.rs:50-76`), **M21** `SnapshotError` (`memory_files/snapshot.rs`, `:206`), **M22** `MemoryFilesError` (`memory_files/mod.rs:268-315`), **M23** `IndexError` (`memory_index/mod.rs`, `:143`), **M24** `NoteParseError` (`memory_files/note.rs`, `:253`), **M25** `RunsInFlight` (`project_life/delete.rs`, `:98`), **M26** `RepointInterrupted` (`project_life.rs`, `:58`), **M27** `UploadError` (`uploads/mod.rs`, `:138`), **M28** `ArtifactError` (`artifacts/mod.rs:298-333`); **M29** `MemoryError` (`memory/service/error.rs:98-152`), **M30** `SecurityViolation` (`security/capabilities/mod.rs:35-69`), **M31** `WorkingFolderRefusal` (`tools/working_folder.rs:112-143`), **M32** `ConstraintConflict` (`skill/constraints.rs:37-58`), **M33** `AgentParseError` (`agent/template/mod.rs:107-122`), **M34** `UserParseError`, **M35** `SoulParseError`, **M36** `SkillParseError`, **M37** `BootstrapParseError`, **M38** `IdentityParseError` (`middleware/{user,soul,skill,bootstrap,identity}/mod.rs`).
  - *Each:* first a golden test enumerating every variant and asserting its `to_string()` against the manual `impl`'s output (literal strings); then the derive (`#[derive(thiserror::Error)]`, `#[error("…")]` per variant, `#[error(fmt = path)]` where an arm computes its text), the manual `impl fmt::Display` and `impl std::error::Error` deleted. **No `#[source]`** (SF-25): every variant's `source()` stays `None`, so every text stays byte for byte — the type's own and every wrapper's `{:#}` (`MemoryError::Internal` prints `{e:#}`, `crates/openalpaca_core/src/memory/service/error.rs:147`, over an `IndexError` kept whole, `:184-191`). thiserror's two implicit sources are refused: `#[from]` (the hand-written `From` impls stay — `memory_files/mod.rs:317`, `:323`; `memory_index/mod.rs:163`, `:169`; `memory/service/error.rs:154`, `:184`, `:205`) and a field named `source` (none among the twenty). Each golden test also asserts `source().is_none()` for every variant. The golden test stays.
  - **Before M21–M23**, golden tests of the `{:#}` text through every wrapper of the memory types — at least `MemoryError::Internal` over `IndexError` (`:184-191`) and over a `MemoryFilesError` (`:179`, `anyhow!("{other}")`) — written against the unchanged code and kept.
  - *Memory code* (M21–M24 and M29): the read-first rule — 03 §7a/§7c and the 09 revision-log entry of the slice that wrote each type, read before the derive and named in the body. M23's derive changes no query, ranking or chunking code, so the index's `SCHEMA_VERSION` rule is not triggered; the body says so.
  - *M36 and N-SKILL-2 (A-38, Z3-03).* N-SKILL-2 (landed `8c63804f`) serves `SkillParseError`'s `Display` strings as skill diagnostics, so M36's golden test pins the strings N-SKILL-2 already serves, and N-SKILL-2's own test may be the one M36 keeps. `InvalidYaml(String)` carries the YAML crate's own message (`crates/openalpaca_core/src/middleware/skill/mod.rs:54`, `:63`), which P30 changes to serde-saphyr's (D4 (a)); M36's golden test builds each variant with a literal payload, pinning the frame `Invalid YAML: {msg}`, not the crate's text; a kept N-SKILL-2 test that parses a real malformed file pins that text, recorded on the parser P30 took.
- **Gate, each.** The golden test green before and after; the crate's suite; the manuals' quoted refusal sentences unchanged (`git grep -F` of each variant's text under `docs/`); G-lint unchanged. M21–M24 add `cargo test -p openalpaca_storage memory_files memory_index` (the fixtures `memory_files::fixtures` and `memory_index::fixture_tests` among them); M29 adds `cargo test -p openalpaca_core memory::service`. **Back-out.** Revert the one commit.
- **Needs.** P38; Q06 no longer constrains M19/M20 (the rewritten Q06 moves neither file — they meet `store/projects.rs` and `store/reveal.rs` where they are); N-SKILL-2 (landed) before M36; P30 before M36.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 9, in order M19 → M38.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M19–M38 (rev 2.2 SF-25, D-13; rev 2.3 A-38; rev 2.4; rev 2.7).

**Step 10 — M39, M40, M41**

← modernization-plan §6.3 step 10

#### M39 — `style: #[expect] for every allow` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** **Every** `#[allow(lint)]` becomes `#[expect(lint, reason = "…")]` — 135 attributes (`too_many_arguments` 61, `dead_code` 48, `result_large_err` 14, `type_complexity` 3, `unused_variables` 2 (one the multi-lint allow at `llm/config/llm_config/router_builder.rs:39`), `unused_imports` 2, `unreachable_patterns` 2, `large_enum_variant` 2, `should_implement_trait` 1); a lint that fires only under some selection takes `#[cfg_attr(<where it fires>, expect(lint, reason = "…"))]` (the connectors' conditions name `target_os = "macos"` beside the features), an item read only by tests `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, reason = "…"))]`; the 23 deserialized-but-unread fields in `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/tail.rs` are removed or read; R79's `created_by: _` (`event_bridge.rs:37`) under `#[expect(clippy::unneeded_wildcard_pattern, reason = "R79")]`. The selections: the bare `cargo build` (after M02, every member but the shell), CI's `cargo build|test --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui` and `cargo clippy --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`, `cargo test -p <crate> --no-run` for each of the eight library crates, the daemon and the CLI, `cargo test -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --test memory_eval --no-run`, `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` (its own target dir, `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/gui CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=target/gui`), and (SF-28) CI's rust job on ubuntu-24.04 (`cargo clippy --locked --workspace --exclude openalpaca_gui --all-targets`), read from its log on the draft PR **before any allow is converted** — over 250 platform `cfg` sites (254 in 54 files) have never been compiled on Linux, so a lint firing on one platform only takes `cfg_attr(target_os = …)` too. A `-p` build that warns is not accepted. The audit's fixes of steps 1a, 2, 3a, 6b and 8 land before M39, so an allow any of them adds is converted with the rest.
- **Gate.** 0 `unfulfilled_lint_expectations` under each selection named above, the list in `Verified:`; `rg -n '#!?\[allow\(' crates apps` → 0; G-CI. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** M38 (the derives change which allows still hold); CI's ubuntu clippy log read (SF-28; plan-rev3 §5.1 item 9's draft PR).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 10, first.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M39 (rev 2.0; rev 2.2 SF-28, MF-5; rev 2.3).

- **Does.** `lib/host/{types,index,tauri,dev-server,fixture}.ts`, the Vite serve-only plugin, `connection.ts`/`reveal.ts`/`TrafficLights.tsx`/`AddExtensionForm.tsx` through the host, `vitest.setup.ts` installing `FixtureHost`, the 23 `vi.mock` blocks removed. `lib/host/index.ts` chooses with `isTauri()` from `@tauri-apps/api/core` (`lib/reveal.ts:20-26`'s comment and the 14 `vi.stubGlobal("isTauri", …)` in 7 test files go); `TauriHost`'s ≈120 lines tested through the real invoke path with `mockIPC` / `mockWindows` / `clearMocks` from `@tauri-apps/api/mocks` (the tests set the `isTauri` marker themselves); `FixtureHost` serves everything above the host; an argument-dispatching `mockImplementation` that remains becomes `vi.when(spy).calledWith(…)` (Vitest 5). `DevServerHost`'s plugin (SF-21, DU-28 (a) as corrected): registered only when `VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST=dev-server`; sets `server.cors: false` and requires an `Origin` exactly `http://localhost:1420` or `http://127.0.0.1:1420`; refuses unless `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` is set and absolute, no fallback to `home_root()`; the log-tail endpoint follows the same rules. The *DevServerHost proxy* is answered (a): not built.
- **Gate.** The four bun gates (121+ files, every test green on the fixture host); `bun run dev` with `VITE_OPENALPACA_HOST=fixture` renders Chat/Settings/Memory with a known connection; with `dev-server` against an isolated development daemon (under `loopback-only.sb`) the page connects and `dist/` of `bun run build` contains neither `__openalpaca` nor any token (grep); the Tauri path unchanged in E5; the TauriHost suite green on `mockIPC`; a `fetch` of `/__openalpaca/connection` from a page served on another localhost port gets a refusal and no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and so does a request while `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` is unset or relative; **G-bundle leg A** (SF-23, MF-4): connect, the log tail, stop and start through plan-rev3 §5.2's confined in-app Start (confinement proved after the Start), Reveal, the folder picker, no duplicate window chrome, and — once M74 has landed (after this commit) — a refused ungranted invoke. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P32 (Vitest 5's `vi.when`); Part III's Rust series (steps 6–10); DU-28 (a).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 11, first.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q07 (rev 3.9; rev 3.13 SF-21, SF-23, MF-4; rev 3.14 A-21; rev 3.16).

#### N-GUI-1 — `fix(gui): the oa-* storage keys are namespaced by the served store` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** The project selection and the pane widths live in WKWebView's localStorage (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src/stores/project.ts:13`, `:100-108`; `stores/pane-widths.ts:82-93`), under the bundle identifier in `~/Library`, outside `~/.openalpaca`, untouched by `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` or the factory reset; Tauri's `data_store_identifier` needs macOS 14, and `data_directory` is unsupported on macOS (tauri-2.12.3 `src/webview/webview_window.rs:1194-1205`). Now the `oa-*` keys are namespaced by the served store's install id or root from `GET /v1/status`, so a different store starts empty (as ZCode:packages/desktop/src/main/desktopRuntimeEnv.ts:60-80). The D1 record of the webview's own storage is B-27 (taken; written by P38).

- **Needs.** Q07 (which rewires the GUI's host calls).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 11, right after Q07.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.4 The daemon, the store and the GUI N-GUI-1 (Z6-10's apply half).

#### N-GUI-2 — `feat(gui): one draft per conversation — the text and the mode, kept per store and session` · Required (drafts) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)
- **Does.** Today the composer's draft text is one window-level value (`apps/openalpaca-gui/src/views/chat/useChatSession.ts:493`), kept across a conversation switch by decision, while the attachment chips are cleared on the switch (F6). Now each conversation has one draft — the text and the mode — keyed by the daemon's store (N-GUI-1's namespace) and the session id, with a root scope for a conversation not yet created; attachments are not persisted, and F6 still clears them. DESIGN_SPEC §5.6 and `docs/GUI_Manual.md` change in the same commit.
- **Gate.** Vitests to write, each failing on the parent: text typed in conversation A, then a switch to B, shows B's own draft (empty), and a switch back restores A's text and mode; a draft typed before a conversation exists is kept in the root scope; two stores' drafts never meet; the attachment chips still clear on a switch. The four bun gates. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** N-GUI-1 (its store-namespaced keys). Decided by: ZCode (Claude Code has none; ZCode:packages/ui/src/v4/composer/composerDraftStore.ts:16-38).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 11, after N-GUI-1.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §5.5 N-GUI-2 (the brief's row 9).

#### N-GUI-3 — `fix(gui): an approval card's selection and focus reset per request; ↵ answers only the focused card; no timer` · Required (arming) · scheduled (taken 2026-10-19; not built)

- **Gate.** Vitests to write, each failing on the parent: with a card blocking and focus elsewhere, ↵ answers nothing; with two cards, ↵ on the focused one answers it alone; a card that replaces another under a new request id comes up with its selection and focus reset, so a ↵ meant for the first does not answer the second; `approval-card-returns.test.tsx` stays green. #### M76 — `feat(gui-shell): the main window built in code; downloads land in Downloads` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** **First, E5's one Export click** (its own step, taken here rather than at step 19): Library Export clicked in the packaged app — expected: nothing saved while the toast says "exported" (WKWebView cancels a download navigation when no handler is set: `wry-0.57.0/src/wkwebview/navigation.rs:57-74`; `tauri-2.12.3/src/webview/mod.rs:362`, `:442`). Then the window's `"create": false` in `tauri.conf.json`, and `setup` builds it with `WebviewWindowBuilder::from_config(app, &config.app.windows[0])` and `.on_download(…)`: `Requested` → `app.path().download_dir()` plus a collision-free name computed in the handler (answered synchronously on the WebKit delegate's main thread, so no dialog there; a WKDownload onto an existing file fails); `Finished` → an event to the webview, so `views/library/LibraryDetail.tsx:105-118`'s toast states the real outcome (the capability gains the event-listen permission it needs). WKDownload needs macOS 11.3. The window keeps today's decorations ((c)-5 (a)). If Q14 has not taken A-25's crash handler, M76 does: `on_web_content_process_terminate` (tauri-2.12.3 `src/app.rs:1910`) writes a line to `gui.log` and reloads the window, and a `std::panic::set_hook` writes to `gui.log`.
- **Gate.** G-bundle: Export lands the file in Downloads; a second export gets a distinct name; the toast reports it; G-GUI. If it takes A-25: G-bundle kills the WebContent process and sees the window reload and reconnect to the same `instance_id`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** M74; Q13 (its `Finished` event); E5's one Export click first; (c)-5 (a).

#### M77 — `feat(gui-shell): external links open in the browser; navigation stays in the app` · Required · scheduled
- **Does.** On M76's builder: `.on_new_window(…)` handing an http(s) URL to `opener::open_browser` and answering `Deny` — the Extensions row's "Authorize at <url>" link (`views/settings/ExtensionsSection.tsx:419-427`, `target="_blank"`) opens the default browser instead of nothing (`wry-0.57.0/src/wkwebview/class/wry_web_view_ui_delegate.rs:173-204`); `.on_navigation(…)` admitting **exactly the entry document** (the devUrl's root in dev), refusing every other same-origin path (Tauri answers an unknown asset path with `index.html`, `tauri-2.12.3/src/manager/mod.rs:388-416`), handing an http(s) URL to `opener::open_browser`, and refusing every other scheme — `file:` is never handed to the operating system, and ST-18's Open by id stays the only way to open a local file; `on_new_window` takes the same rule. DocumentPreview (`components/work/preview/DocumentPreview.tsx:20-21`, `:139`) handles a click on an anchor itself: an http(s) URL goes the browser's way; a fragment scrolls within the preview and navigates nothing; any other href (an absolute or relative path, `file:`, `mailto:`, any other scheme) is prevented. Offering Open or Reveal for such a link is not part of M77 (it would go through the daemon's routes only; Reveal's argument is a file id since D8 (a)). `opener` is the shell's own dependency from M79 on; no plugin and no capability.
- **Gate.** G-bundle: the Authorize link (a fixture MCP server answering 401 with a hint) and a markdown link both open the default browser; the app stays on its page — both links point at loopback URLs (a local fixture page), so the browser reaches nothing beyond the machine (MF-4); G-bundle clicks an absolute path, a relative path, a fragment, a `mailto:` and an http link in a previewed markdown file and finds the app on its page after each, the http one opened in the browser; the four bun gates, with a DocumentPreview test per shape; G-GUI. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** M76 and M79 (D-18).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 12, after M76.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M77 (rev 2.2 D-18, MF-4; rev 2.3 A-39, Z6-03; rev 2.7).

#### Q18a — `build(gui): LSMinimumSystemVersion — the declared macOS floor` · Required ((c)-2 (a), 13.3) · scheduled
- **Does.** `bundle.macOS.minimumSystemVersion = "13.3"` (`tauri.conf.json`'s `bundle`, `:27` at `476f1f81`, has no `macOS` table today); the README and `docs/Installation_Manual.md`'s *Scope* and *Build Machine Requirements* (`:17-38`) name the floor once, with C-18's architecture fact — each package is built for its build host's architecture only (`scripts/release/package-macos.sh:103-107`; `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts:39-40` refuses `universal-apple-darwin`), so a package built on Apple silicon excludes Intel Macs whatever the floor; no release-notes file exists. **The minos gate rides here** (*Minos gate* (b)): `apps/openalpaca-gui/scripts/prepare-sidecar.ts` (every sidecar build, by hand or as Tauri's hook) and `package-macos.sh`'s CLI build (`:159` at `476f1f81`) set `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` to the floor, read from `tauri.conf.json` so the number is written once; `package-macos.sh` runs the minos check on every package. Whether `tauri build` passes the floor to the shell's own compile is read on the bundle [NE]; the first run answers plan-rev3 Appendix B #55. The staged sidecar's hash changes once with the variable; G-ident compares bundle and stage as before. Does not depend on P32 (Tailwind 4.3.3, already in the lock, requires Safari 16.4).
- **Gate.** E5 reads `LSMinimumSystemVersion` `13.3` from `Info.plist`; G-bundle; the `LC_BUILD_VERSION` minos (`otool -l`) of every Mach-O in the `.app` and in `libexec/` at or below 13.3, the check passing on the bundle and failing on a scratch sidecar built with `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` above the floor. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** (c)-2 (a), 13.3, and the *Minos gate* (b), taken 2026-10-17.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 13, after Q09.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q18 (rev 3.13 C-18, G-06, C-19; rev 3.14 A-29; rev 3.16).
