- **Does.** A short design under `tasks/` mapping an MCP task (SEP-2663; `crates/openalpaca_mcp/src/client.rs:549` sets `task: None`) onto the daemon's run model — a Work-pane row, status polling through `tasks/get`, cancel — and which calls set `task` (those a server marks task-capable); `Peer::call_tool` answers a task with `UnexpectedResponse`, so the build uses `call_tool_once` and polls (`rmcp-3.5.1/src/service/client.rs:1996-1999`). The build's commits are sized by the design. Reference shape: ZCode's task registry, a completion notification delivered once, and one read tool (ZCode:docs/task-output-contract.md).
- **Gate.** The owner reads the design. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** P19, N-MCP-2 (landed `48f2b9a6`) and M15; DU-R4 (iii) as (c)-4 (a), taken 2026-10-17.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 14, first.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M83 (rev 2.3 A-40; rev 2.5).

#### M84 — `feat(mcp): MCP elicitation answered by a person, as a form, through the ConfirmationBroker` · DU-bound (DU-R4 (ii) as (c)-3 (a), a form, with T35 (iv)) · scheduled
- **Does.** `ClientHandler::create_elicitation` (default `Decline`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/handler/client.rs:179-190`) answered through the `ConfirmationBroker`; M15's MRTR loop answers input-required rounds through it; an unattended turn refused at once (= `Decline`). In the rev 2.3 shape (A-31; Z1-12, Z5-07):
  - *Form mode only.* The client advertises `elicitation: { form: { schemaValidation: true } }` and never `url` (`ElicitationCapability`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/model/capabilities.rs:80-133`) — a wire change; a request in any other mode (`ElicitRequestParams::UrlElicitationParams`, `src/model.rs:3870`) is declined with a warning in the log. An answer is validated against the requested schema before it is sent.
  - *One input per primitive field* — string, number, integer, boolean, enum — the server's message, field titles, descriptions and enum labels escaped under the approval rule (`utils::text::escape_hidden_line` / `escape_hidden`, `lib/hidden-chars.ts` in the GUI, the hidden-character notice beneath).
  - *Attributed to one call.* An MRTR round is attributed exactly, through M15's loop. A request sent during a call (`elicitation/create`) carries no related-request id (`RequestContext`, `rmcp-3.5.1/src/service.rs:1206-1215`), so it is attributed only when exactly one call to that server is in flight, and declined otherwise (ZCode's server-name-keyed map, which two concurrent calls overwrite, is not copied); with no call in flight, declined.
  - *The clocks held while a person answers.* A request sent mid-call arrives while the call's MCP and sandbox clocks run (30 s and 60 s or 300 s today); the broker extends both by at most its own confirmation timeout — a change to the sandbox's per-call bound, taken with (c)-3, never by a server's notifications — so the call stays bounded by the sum; where a clock cannot be held, the request is declined. An MRTR round runs only the sandbox's clock. Both holds change the bound the extension design rests on — its §3.2 T3 statement (`tasks/extension-enable-design.md:608-623`) and the disable latency of its §3.2 T4 and §8's disable route (`:662-663`, `:2195-2197`) — so M84 amends those texts, as N-MCP-3 leaves them, and that design's revision log, in the same commit. N-TOOL-1b (§3.3 step 3b) sets the per-tool bound to 120 s with a 600 s ceiling before M84 lands, so "today"'s figures are re-read at M84's commit.
  - *Declined at once* on an unattended turn and on a connector lane, as `Decline`.
  - *Nothing kept.* The answer is never persisted: the confirmation's persistence arm keeps writing ids alone (`apps/openalpacad/src/events/persistence.rs:301`).
  - *Not added:* rmcp's `elicitation` feature (at 3.5.1 it gates the server side and one client-builder helper); M84 builds the form capability directly, `enable_elicitation_with(ElicitationCapability::new().with_form(FormElicitationCapability::new().with_schema_validation(true)))` (`src/model/capabilities.rs:437-454`, `:93-102`, `:132-146`).
  - *The surfaces it touches:* the confirmation request and its answer carry the form's schema and values (the `ToolConfirmationRequested` family; `POST /v1/chat/confirmations/{request_id}`, `apps/openalpacad/src/router.rs:401`; `docs/api` regenerated); the GUI card draws the inputs; the CLI's attended prompt asks one line per field or declines [NE: which; modernization-plan Appendix B #27]; the connectors decline. Size [NE].
  - *Not M84's:* T35 (iv), B-01 (a) and (b), B-03 (1) and (2) — the tool-approval rule changes taken by the same answer — are carried by plan-rev3 §5.4's owner-decision table (B-02, which is T35, after P19; B-01 and B-03 after P38); M84's form does not wait for them.
- **Gate.** An elicitation stub per case: a field-less request accepted and declined; a typed form with each primitive, an invalid value refused before sending; a `url`-mode request declined with its warning; an MRTR round attributed through M15's loop; a mid-call request with one call in flight attributed, with two declined; an unattended turn and a connector lane declined at once; the clocks held within the broker's bound; the server's text escaped with a U+202E and a U+200B planted and the hidden-chars suites; no form value in `event_log`. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** M15 (and M83 before it in the step); DU-R4 (ii) as (c)-3 (a) with T35 (iv), taken 2026-10-17.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 14, after M83.
- **Record.** ← modernization-plan §6.1 M84 (rev 2.0; rev 2.3 A-31; rev 2.5).

#### M65 — `build: [workspace.lints], a warnings gate and a format gate` · Required (DU-R3 (b) + (d); DU-17's gate half) · scheduled

Whether `allow_attributes_without_reason` also reads `#[expect]` at the pinned toolchain is checked here and recorded in the body (modernization-plan Appendix B #26). **The Linux build (SF-28):** `apps/openalpacad/src/notification/imessage.rs:4-5` import `ConfigRepository`, `PreferenceRepository` and `warn` unconditionally while only the `cfg(target_os = "macos")` functions use them (`:12`, `:65`; stubs at `:61`, `:130`) — `unused_imports` on Linux; M65's first hunk puts those imports under the same `cfg` (a source hunk in a `build:` commit, kept there on purpose). CI's clippy log on the draft PR is read for Linux-only warnings again on M65's own run. CLAUDE.md's "clippy 78, the baseline" becomes "0, a gate" (M54).

**Step 18 — Q10 → Q19 → G4–G6 as Q10's profiles justify**

← modernization-plan §6.3 step 18; plan-rev3 §7.3, §7.4–§7.6

**The measurement protocol (plan-rev3 §7.5, G7), summarised once** — it governs Q10 and G4–G6, and Q00's owed rows:
- *Fixture store:* a fresh isolated `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE` under `$T` (never the owner's); `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=<store>/config` with `llm.toml` from `scripts/release/templates/config/llm.toml` plus credential discovery off; the daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its first launch; onboarding finished first (one attended `update_persona` turn); then seeded: one synthetic registered project (a scratch git repository), one 100-message conversation of long text/code/tables, one 20-note memory partition, five artifacts. The conversations are seeded deterministically by **(s-1)** a canned OpenAI-compatible stub on loopback (a test key value, never a real key) or **(s-2)** a test-only storage seeder writing through `openalpaca_storage`'s repositories before the daemon starts; Q10's record and its baseline name the form. Built once, archived beside the baseline (the repository keeps counts and hashes only), copied for each trial.
- *Processes counted:* the shell (`openalpaca_gui`) and its WebKit XPC helpers (`com.apple.WebKit.WebContent`, `.Networking`, `.GPU`), `openalpacad` and its children; per process **and** combined with the OS's non-double-counting measure (macOS `footprint`/`vmmap -summary`, phys_footprint), never a sum of RSS; local-model memory (Ollama) reported separately and excluded. Windows and Linux rows deferred (DU-33).
- *Scenarios* (5 cold trials each; median and p95; the driver's OS clock): (1) cold launch, no daemon → first shell paint, first useful content, daemon-ready (`/v1/health` 200); (2) launch with the daemon running; (3) idle foreground 60 s and hidden/minimized 60 s → memory and CPU %; (4) one streamed 2,000-token answer → peak memory, dropped frames; (5) the 100-message conversation opened and scrolled; (6) three previews opened; (7) ten quit/relaunch cycles with the daemon surviving (same `instance_id`) — Q00 measured it (10/10), no row owed; (8) build-side: clean full build, warm full build, shell-only incremental (`touch src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), support-only incremental, daemon-only incremental, React HMR turnaround — each with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, profile, toolchain, hardware and cache state recorded.
- *Launchers:* (1) uses the bundle copy whose `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` is plan-rev3 §5.2's wrapper, confinement proved after the spawn, the wrapper's own cost measured apart; (2)–(6) use rev 3.8's launcher (the daemon under launcher (i), the app unsandboxed with the same store variables); (8) launches nothing. Profiler traces and Web Inspector figures (G4, G6) come from **the instrumented build** — a release bundle with Tauri's `devtools` feature and react-dom's profiling build aliased in — used identically at baseline and after, its provenance recorded in Q10, never compared with a release package's figures.
- *Rules:* baseline on the same application commit before each architecture step; compare like with like; **set budgets from the baseline** — the plan writes no MB/ms target. Baseline and candidate alternate trial by trial, each on its own fresh copy of the one archived fixture (A-26); scenario (1)'s first paint and first useful content are read from Q14's startup marks in `gui.log` (A-25). Q00's owed rows (D13 (b), taken): a measurement build of `a91f4b23` carrying Q14's marks, in the instrumented build's class, after Q14 lands; the instrumented row's own baseline goes with them.
- *The native CI matrix (plan-rev3 §7.6, G8):* `shell-check` (Q08), `native-smoke` (Q09) and `rust-macos` (Q20), each its `macos-latest` / pinned-label row alone under DU-33 (a); costs [NE, reasoned] in plan-rev3 §7.6 — macOS minutes bill 10× on a private repository, free on a public one.
← plan-rev3 §7.5 (G7), §7.6 (G8)

#### Q10 — recapture and report (a measurement, not a commit) · — · scheduled

Q10's record names the instrumented build's provenance (commit, features, the react-dom alias) and the seeder form used, and compares each with the baseline taken the same way (C-16, SF-22). Trials alternate with the baseline's, each on a copy of one fixture, and scenario (1)'s first paint and first useful content come from Q14's marks (A-26, A-25). Q10's figures set Q19's budgets; Q21's capture time is carried in scenario (1)'s daemon-ready figure; Q17's build-time effect is reported against Q00 (scenario 8); M75's bundle bytes before and after are recorded here.
- **Gate.** The protocol's own rules (above); the record under `tasks/deps-upgrade/baselines/<date>/`, compared with Q00.
- **Needs.** Part III's M64, M45–M53 and M65 (steps 15–17); Q00 (taken, `baselines/2026-10-12/README.md`).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 18, first.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q10 (rev 3.13 C-16, SF-22; rev 3.14 A-26, A-25, A-28).

#### Q19 — `build(release): package-macos.sh checks the bundle's size against Q10's budgets` · Recommended · scheduled
- **Does.** `scripts/release/package-macos.sh` compares the `.app` and the daemon (the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` and `libexec/openalpacad`) with budgets set from Q10's recapture plus a stated margin, and on failure lists the largest items in the bundle, so a regression names its cause. The budgets are Q10's numbers, never the plan's, re-set at each recapture (plan-rev3 §9). A build guard, not a product enforcement point (as ZCode:docs/desktop-bundle-size-architecture.md:120-129).
- **Gate.** One `package-macos.sh` run in a worktree passes under the budgets; the same run with a temporary padding file in the bundle fails and names it. **Back-out.** Revert.
- **Needs.** Q10's figures.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 18, right after Q10; before G4–G6.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.3 Q19 (rev 3.14, the ZCode audit's A-28; Z6-14).

#### G4 — streaming render · measure-first · measure-first (no commit scheduled)
- **Does.** Path: each SSE `delta`/`reasoning` frame dispatches (`lib/chat-stream.ts:433-444`); the reducer appends (`:292-298`, `:281-287`); `useChatStream` dispatches into React state (`hooks/useChat.ts:195-214`, `:311-317`); `buildTranscript` is memoized on the whole `stream.state` among eleven inputs (`views/chat/useChatSession.ts:1098-1125`), so every delta rebuilds every item; `Transcript` maps all items (`Transcript.tsx:36-43`); `MessageBody` calls `parseProse(text)` on every render (`components/chat/MessageBody.tsx:117-124`); `UserMessage`/`AssistantMessage` are plain functions (`MessageRow.tsx:84`, `:144`). Change, when justified (measured on the compiled build, M69): split the live row out of `buildTranscript`'s inputs; manual memoization only where the compiled build's profile shows work the compiler leaves; only then coalesce deltas to one flush per animation frame with a **mandatory flush** before `done`/`error`/`confirmation_requested` and on unmount, never delaying approval or control events.
- **Gate.** **First:** a vitest harness replaying a recorded 2,000-delta stream into `useChatSession`, counting `parseProse` calls per history row (expect 1 after; ≈ deltas today) and `MessageRow` renders per row; a React Profiler trace of a 100-message conversation while streaming (commit count, render ms), taken on plan-rev3 §7.5's instrumented build at baseline and after, on plan-rev3 §7.5's seeded fixture. Worth it = history-row renders per delta ≈ 0 **and** `done.content` and the event order byte-identical in the harness; a lost delta rejects the change.
- **Needs.** Q10; M69 (the compiled build).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 18, after Q19, as Q10's profiles justify.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.4 G4.

#### G5 — the latest history page · measure-first · measure-first (no commit scheduled)
- **Does.** Path: `useChatSession.ts:379-406` asks `GET /v1/chat/history?limit=100`, reads `total`, then asks `offset = total − 100`; the route (`routes/chat.rs:657-706`) → `ChatService::get_history` (`chat/service.rs:303-313`) → `list_by_session(limit, offset)` + `count_by_session` (`repository/conversation/mod.rs:197-202`). The repository already has the tail: `list_recent_by_session` (`:276-299`) and `list_recent_by_lane` (`:246-271`), used by the context builder (`orchestrator/context_builder.rs:82-83`, `:248`). So G5 = `tail=true` (or `offset=-1`) on `GET /v1/chat/history` answered by `list_recent_by_session(limit)` with the same `total`, one request for the GUI and for the CLI's `chat --resume` (`apps/openalpaca/src/commands/chat.rs:270-324`); `(created_at, id)` is the sort key in both queries.
- **Gate.** **First:** the probe→tail latency on a 500-message fixture session seeded by plan-rev3 §7.5's deterministic seeder; worth it if plan-rev3 §7.5's "first useful content" on a long session improves measurably or the CLI shares the simplification. Tests: route tests for short (< limit), exact (= limit) and long sessions; a concurrent-append test (no missing or duplicate ids across the tail + one older page); `gen_api_docs.py --check`.
- **Needs.** Q10.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 18, after Q19, as Q10's profiles justify.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.4 G5.

#### G6 — lazy previews, then maybe virtualization · measure-first · measure-first (no commit scheduled)
- **Does.** Path: `App.tsx:51-55` lazy-loads the five views; `ChatView.tsx:57` imports `FilePanelSlot` statically, `:64` `WorkPaneSlot`; `FilePanelSlot.tsx:17-35` imports `ArtifactPreview`, `ArtifactDiffTab`, `HistoryTab`, the library preview helpers; `WorkPaneSlot.tsx:19` imports `WorkPane`. The build (`gui-baseline-dist.json`): `ChatView-*.js` 78,600 B importing `DiffView-*.js` 88,997 B (≈29 KB gzip) and `preview-*.js` 4,831 B; entry 395,009 B. Change: `lazy(() => import("./FilePanelSlot"))` behind the aside's open state with loading/error boundaries, same for `WorkPaneSlot`. Virtualization only after a React Profiler + DOM-node count on a 100-message fixture of long code/tables shows layout cost dominating; acceptance list: scroll anchoring while streaming, selection/copy, find, keyboard navigation, approval focus.
- **Gate.** **First:** in the packaged webview, the bytes evaluated before first paint of the chat route and the time-to-interactive of the empty chat (from plan-rev3 §7.5's instrumented build, at baseline and after); worth it if the deferred chunk is not needed for the default screen (the aside is closed by default) **and** first open of a preview stays under a budget the baseline sets (e.g. ≤ 150 ms on the reference machine).
- **Needs.** Q10.
- **Place.** Merged-order step 18, after Q19, as Q10's profiles justify.
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §7.4 G6.

## 4. Verification

This section holds what proves the programme: when verification runs (§4.1), what CI does and cannot do (§4.2), the G-base driver and its run of record (§4.3), the cut-day compile probe (§4.4), the wave's end-to-end gates E1–E8 with the E6 comparators and E8's probe (§4.5), load and runner images (§4.6), and Part III's phase gates V1–V8 (§4.7). The named per-commit gates — G-check, G-CI, G-GUI, G-lint, G-ident, G-base, G-bundle — are defined with their commands in §1; this section uses them by name. Entries with an id use the entry template, with *Does* read as "what it checks" and *Gate* as "passes when". ← plan-rev3 §6, §5.1 items 3 and 6–6b, §5.2; modernization-plan §7

### 4.1 When verification runs

- **Per commit:** the gate in the commit's §3 entry, run before its `Verified:` line is written. ← modernization-plan §7
- **G-base's run of record:** at the cut instant (at or after 2026-10-21T19:53Z), on the exact branch point, before P42 and P03 (§4.3). ← plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a, 6b; §5.2 G-base
- **E6:** at §5 item 3's consent point — before P04, P12, P29 and P30 — not in the end-of-programme list (A2-16). ← plan-rev3 §6 E6; §5.1 item 3
- **E5's one Library Export click before M76:** taken on the tip before M76 lands, in merged-order step 12 (§3.3) (SF-16). ← plan-rev3 §6; modernization-plan §6.3 step 12
- **At the end of each Part III phase and at the end of the programme:** V1–V8 (§4.7). ← modernization-plan §7
- **E1–E8 otherwise:** at the very end — merged-order step 19 (E6 where §5 item 3 places it; E5 there but for its Export click), after the before-the-merge freshness sweep of §5 item 5; only M54 (step 20, docs only, so it reopens no E-gate) follows. E1–E8 run after P38, so after P03b: their pinned toolchain is 1.99.1. ← plan-rev3 §6 preamble; modernization-plan §6.3 steps 19–20

**Conditions on every run.** Every manual run under §1's rules (plan-rev3 §5.2); every live daemon under `scripts/eval/memory/loopback-only.sb` from its first launch, on an isolated store, with §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) credential pre-seed and its log check (SF-13); any Start from inside the app is §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) confined in-app Start, its confinement proved after it (MF-4); every export or worktree build that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside it under the same scratch root (harmless before M63, required after it — MF-5); no run against `~/.openalpaca`. ← plan-rev3 §6 preamble; modernization-plan §7

**Expectations, not gates of record.** The cut-day compatibility report (`tasks/deps-upgrade/reports/2026-10-17-cut-compatibility.md`, rev 1, and its rev 2's §8) proved the set eligible at 2026-10-21T19:53Z compatible in two trees: a Rust tree, the prep tip with the whole-lock refresh at the instant (5,126 passed / 0 failed / 17 ignored, clippy 0 triples, G-GUI clean), and a JS tree with P31–P33 and P31a's JS half (121 files / 1,551 tests, the four bun gates, `bun audit` 0), whose fifteen Tauri-family lock entries are identical; neither carried both halves. A combined tree (the report's rev 2, §8) passed every gate run on it, a debug app bundle with G-ident's debug variant among them, but the workspace suite, check and clippy did not run on its exact lock and nothing was launched (its §8.4). None of these is a gate of record: G-base's run at the instant and E1–E8 on the wave's own tip are, and the report's figures are their expectations. E4 and E5 deny outbound networking, so aws-lc-sys 0.46.0 — AWS-LC 5.11.0, which rejects non-canonical Ed25519 public keys (C9) — is exercised over a real TLS connection by P28's and P41's live gates (D10 (a): they wait for the owner's test bots; A-09 (2)'s stub gates beside them make no real TLS connection), not by E4 or E5. ← plan-rev3 §6 (rev 3.15–3.16 paragraph)

### 4.2 What CI covers, and what it cannot

**What CI covers** (first time for some of it): the Linux build and tests (the first Linux compile of zbus/keyring 4, sysinfo 0.39's Linux zombie path, the `managers::mcp` direct-child kill with process-wrap 10); clippy and `--locked` (DU-22); the four bun gates under the pinned Bun; Pester on Windows; the advisories jobs (P34). **On this branch CI covers nothing until §5 item 9's draft PR exists** — `ci.yml:3-6` runs on `pull_request` and on pushes to `main` only, and PR #34 is merged; the Linux compile of keyring-core's zbus store (P12), the libdbus removal (P13) and sysinfo's Linux path (P08) are read at item 9's second CI read, before P15. The Windows `cargo check` (P01a) and Q08's Windows and Linux legs are deferred under DU-33 (a) (*DU-33 (a) reopened* answered (a)); the existing windows-latest Pester job runs on every PR and every push to `main`. Dependabot and the weekly `schedule:` run read the default branch, so P35's gate and the advisories' weekly run are judged after the merge. Once their commits land, CI also runs B-23's seconds-long `tooling` job (after P34) and Q20's `rust-macos` check — the CI set's build, test and clippy on a pinned macOS label — so the macOS-only code is compiled by CI for the first time; P01b's `ubuntu-24.04` pin stays through the merge and after it, P39 being dropped. ← plan-rev3 §6 *What CI covers*

**What CI cannot cover, and how it is covered.**

| Gap | Why CI cannot | How |
|---|---|---|
| macOS at all | CI is ubuntu and Windows only | Everything was measured on macOS aarch64 (Darwin 27.0.0); re-run G-CI locally on the final commit (E3). From Q20 on, CI's `rust-macos` check builds, tests and lints the CI set on a pinned macOS label on every pull request — (c)'s `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch` triggers instead if the minutes matter (plan-rev3 Appendix B #34) — and E3's local run on the final commit stays. |
| The Tauri crate | CI compiles nothing under `src-tauri` — until Q08 (DU-31) | G-GUI by hand after P04, P08, P31a and at the end; also after P10 (for P09 and P10 — A1-10) and the Part II and Part III shell commits the G-GUI row names (§1). |
| A packaged app, which daemon it bundles, CSS in WKWebView | no bundle job | E5 with G-ident. The cut-day set was built and tested by the compatibility report and launched by nobody (its §7): G-bundle at P31a is its first runtime check, E5 its last. |
| CSS in WebView2 and webkit2gtk; WebView2 under windows 0.62 | no Windows/Linux desktop here | DU-14 (w-a) accepts, (w-b) runs E5b; DU-31's native-smoke covers it nightly with labelled gaps. Deferred under DU-33. |
| Any Windows Rust build; the Windows keychain | no Windows Rust job | P01a (temporary) or Q08 (permanent); the keychain stays unverified. Deferred under DU-33: neither P01a nor Q08's Windows leg runs. |
| The Linux Secret Service read-back | no session bus on the runner | DU-10: a container with gnome-keyring unlocked, if Linux `use_keychain` users exist. |
| The macOS keychain read-back | never the real keychain | DU-10 (a): a disposable macOS user or VM. |
| Real MCP servers; descendant processes on disable; the offered protocol version | network, third-party servers | P19's round trip, `ps` check and forking fixture. P19b is Required (D5 (a)); its gate of record is its in-process stubs with A1-12's refresh and the three conditions; the real-server check (a real `2026-07-28` server's list change) stays E5's, run if such a server exists. |
| The REPL at a terminal | no TTY | P27's checklist. |
| A live Telegram bot | network, a token | P28's gate (Required); D10 (a): P28 waits for the owner's test bot (§5 item 12), and A-09 (2)'s loopback stub of the Bot API joins its gate as a supplement, never a replacement. |
| A live Discord connect | network, a token | P41's gate: a live Discord connect and one message under §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) rules, after P28 (§5 item 12, D10 (a)); A-09 (2): twilight's HTTP client and gateway against a loopback stub beside the live connect. |
| The owner's real config and skill files | `~/.openalpaca` is off limits to the research | E6, with rebuilt tools — run by the owner at §5 item 3's consent point, not in the end list (A2-16). |
| The owner's real partitions and database under the new build | same | E8, on a copy, read-only, no daemon. |
| `docs/api` staleness | CI does not run `gen_api_docs.py --check` | G-CI's local step; E3. B-23 (b) taken: a seconds-long `tooling` job on ubuntu running `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, `scripts/tests/test_gen_api_docs.py` and lockdates' gate-mode tests on a recorded registry fixture, with no network, and optionally `gbase.sh --self-test` — B-23's entry, after P34; from it on every push leaves `docs/api` current, which P19 already does (DU-25 (s1), P21 folded in), and modernization-plan's DU-D3 premise flips to its pin (b). |

← plan-rev3 §6 *What CI cannot cover*

### 4.3 The G-base driver and its run of record

**G-base — `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/gbase.sh` · named gate (the wave's first) · driver landed; run of record not yet taken** (its entry in the sequence is §3.2's *G-base*; the driver's landings are §3.1's *G-base driver*)

- **Does.** From one canonical scratch root `T=$(realpath "$(mktemp -d)")` it exports the base (`git archive <base> | tar -x -C "$T/src"`) and runs the toolchain step, **step 1b** — the Tauri pair's Rust half (P31a: `tauri`/`tauri-build` into `[workspace.dependencies]`, `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=<the dialog pin>"`, `cargo update -p tauri -p tauri-build -p tauri-plugin-dialog` then `--precise <the tauri pin>`) — and the re-cut's steps 2–8: each step its anchored `perl` manifest edits (each anchor asserted to match exactly once), its source hunks (the three split patches), its cargo commands — a plain `cargo update` at step 2 (no Tauri hold), `cargo update -p rmcp` with no `--precise` at step 6 — a `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui`, and a lock snapshot compared with the shipped un-held one for that step (`recut-lock-02-update.lock` at step 2, `recut-lock-99-final.lock` at the end; step 1b against `locks/d1-lock-01b-pair.lock`); a DU-26 pair check after steps 1b and 2 and against `package.json` in the final phase; then the full suite, `python3 -I scripts/gen_api_docs.py --check`, the four bun gates on the P31a+P31+P32+P33 JS set, and G-GUI. It exits non-zero at the first failed command (`set -euo pipefail`, every cargo exit code tested, nothing recorded-and-continued), writes every log under `$T/logs/`, and prints at the end the lock differences it found with the dates of the releases behind them. Every cargo call is `nice -n 10 env CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=<the caller's, else 8> CARGO_TARGET_DIR=$T/target CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR=$T/target cargo +<toolchain> …`, one at a time; nothing runs inside the repository. G-base still applies the measured `recut-source-only.diff` — save step 8's serde-saphyr line and `yaml_options()`, which take P30's 2.0 since `96de1139` (D4 (a)); the source changes of P10's helper, P12's keyring-core, P16's zip 9 and P19's `Auto` lifecycle and wrapper are measured by their own commits' gates. ← plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a, 6b; `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md` (`tools/gbase/` row); `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/README.md`
- **Gate.** The regenerated lock equals `recut-lock-99-final.lock`, or every difference is listed: `rmcp`/`rmcp-macros` 3.5.0 → 3.5.1 (DU-25 (v4); `lockdiff.py`'s exact `name:OLD->NEW` spec, so a later 3.5.x stays unexpected), `serde-saphyr`/`granit-parser` 1.3.0 → 2.0.0 at step 8 (`[D4(a)]`, `SAPHYR_EXPECT`; `96de1139`) and each release dated after 2026-10-10 that is **eligible at the cut instant** — a younger one fails the freshness gate. The run counts as the gate only with item 6b's five requirements (MF-1, MF-2, MF-6):
  1. step 1b checks every Tauri crate exactly — tauri, -build, -codegen, -macros, -runtime, -runtime-wry, -utils, -plugin, -plugin-fs, -plugin-dialog — and that each one's `created_at` is at or before the cut instant minus 7 days;
  2. the Tauri pair and the JS pins are parameters checked against registry dates, not literals, and the swift-rs comment goes;
  3. step 2 and every later lock run the freshness gate (`lockdates.py --gate`), so a release dated after 2026-10-10 is expected only when eligible at the cut instant;
  4. the driver refuses to start before the instant (`--not-before <UTC>`);
  5. the suite verdict fails a crashed test binary: the set of binaries named in `error: test failed, to rerun pass <spec>` must equal the set holding the expected pins, an empty pin set requires `LAST_RC == 0`, any `(signal:` line fails, the passed count is held to a floor (`--min-passed`) from a measured reference, and `--self-test` has a SIGABRT case beside the pins.

  Each requirement counts as built when the gbase README, as committed, records it so; the run of record waits for all five. The gbase README's status line and its revision-log rows of 2026-10-17 (and, for step 8's D4 (a) amendment, `96de1139`, its 2026-10-20 row) record the amendment (`--not-before`, every Tauri crate checked exactly and by date, `lockdates.py --gate` at every step from 1b to 8 with `--apply-step-backs` and `--waivers`, the pins checked against crates.io and npm at the pre-flight, `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` in every cargo call, the suite verdict failing a crashed binary with a `--min-passed` floor). At the branch point all seven of P14's pins fail by design; on a tip carrying all of P15–P18, 0 failed with no pin, and a tree carrying some of them is refused (`expected_pins`). The suite figure measured on the cut-day lock is 5,126 passed; P30's pin test adds one at P30's own commit (expected, not measured) — not in the run of record, whose step-8 patch carries no test. ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6b; §5.2 G-base; `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md` (`tools/gbase/` row)
- **Needs.** `rustup` with 1.99.0 installed (`cargo +1.99.0 --version` answers; the driver installs nothing; `--toolchain` defaults to 1.99.0 — G-base runs before P03, so it keeps 1.99.0 under D3 (a)); Bun **1.4.3**, used ahead of P42's commit (the root `package.json` still says `bun@1.4.2` when it runs); network access for `cargo update`, `bun install` and the registry reads (crates.io's API and npm, User-Agent `openalpaca-deps-check`); Python 3.9+, perl, git, tar; `TMPDIR` on a scratch volume with room for two target directories (the workspace minus the shell ≈ 2.7 GiB cold, the suite's test binaries more). ← `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/README.md` *Prerequisites*
- **Place.** At the cut instant, on the exact branch point, before P42 and P03 (the order is the driver, then P42, P03, P31a …); again whenever the base moves. N-MCP-1, N-MCP-3 and N-MCP-4 land after this run and before P19 (§3.2). ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6a; gbase README
- **Record.** Driver landed as `507f9e8a` (rev 3.5, with Phase 0); rewritten without the hold as `95c2aea1` (rev 3.10: the pair is step 1b compared with a derived reference; step 2 a plain `cargo update`; steps 3–7 compared with the un-held snapshots; `--with-stepbacks` refused; `hold-footprint.txt` and the two held locks deleted, in git history at `d7f21b65`); fixes `74b86219` (the Tauri minor as a tilde requirement), `f7207917` (the suite run in a git checkout, P14's seven pins expected) and `e4cfd37c` (P14's pins expected to fail on the branch point only). Rehearsed in scratch on 2026-10-12 (plan-rev3 rev 3.11; found the suite's two git-checkout tests and P14's seven pins). Amended on 2026-10-17 for the plan review and re-anchored the same day for D1 (a), landing with plan-rev3 rev 3.15 at `gbase.sh:46` (`NOT_BEFORE`), `:54-57` (`TAURI_PRECISE`, `DIALOG`, `JS_API`, `JS_CLI`), `:391` (`TAURI_REF`, `locks/d1-lock-01b-pair.lock`), `:433-448` (`JS_PINS`), the bun gates' scratch `HOME` and `CI=true` (`:1022-1029`), and `lockdates.py:10` (`--bun-lock`). Self-test 42 of 42 on 1.99.0 and on 1.93.0 after the re-anchoring. Amended on 2026-10-20 for D4 (a) as `96de1139`, after the owner's ruling of 2026-10-19 (PDT; 2026-10-20T06:53Z): step 8 writes `serde-saphyr = "2.0"` (`gbase.sh:814` at `96de1139`), `SAPHYR_EXPECT` (`:688`) beside rmcp's class, `step-8-config.patch` cut at `7fc775bc` with P30's 2.0 options and no pin test; self-test 42 of 42 on 1.99.0; and its cut-day finding, annotate-snippets 0.12.17 stepped back to 0.12.16 at step 8 (§3.2 *G-base*). **Not yet run for real** — that run is the cut's. cargo-nextest (a process per test, serial groups, leak detection, JUnit output) is recorded as the later structural fix (the ZCode audit's A-45, a later design), not scheduled; requirement (5) stands for the cut-day run. ← plan-rev3 §5.1 items 6a, 6b; §8 (gbase row); `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md`

**The defaults at the instant** (D1 (a); the compatibility report §6.3; C2): `NOT_BEFORE="2026-10-21T19:53Z"` (every freshness gate's max date 2026-10-14T19:53:00Z), `TAURI_PRECISE="2.12.4"`, `DIALOG="2.8.3"`, `JS_API="2.12.4"`, `JS_CLI="2.12.3"`, step 1b's reference re-derived for the 2026-10-14 family as `locks/d1-lock-01b-pair.lock`, and seven `JS_PINS` rows at P31's and P32's values — react and react-dom 19.3.1, @tanstack/react-query 5.104.2, dompurify 3.4.17, marked 18.1.1, @testing-library/user-event 14.6.8, vitest 5.0.4. The run of record passes no `--tauri`, `--dialog`, `--js-api`, `--js-cli`, `--tauri-crate` or `--js-pin`. Before the re-anchoring the same run was given as flags — `--not-before 2026-10-21T19:53Z --tauri 2.12.4 --dialog 2.8.3 --js-api 2.12.4 --js-cli 2.12.3`, eight `--tauri-crate` (tauri-build, tauri-codegen and tauri-macros 2.7.3; tauri-runtime and tauri-runtime-wry 2.12.2; tauri-utils 2.10.2; tauri-plugin 2.7.2; tauri-plugin-fs 2.7.1) and the seven `--js-pin`; the `--edits-only` proof of 2026-10-17 shows the defaults change exactly what those arguments did. A rehearsal from the prep tip takes the `TAURI_PRECISE` path with those eight `--tauri-crate` values (C2). ← plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6b (rev 3.15); gbase README *The cut instant*, *The other modes*

**The run** (from the repository root):

```sh
G=tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase
CUT=2026-10-21T19:53Z    # the owner's D1 (a): the run starts at or after it (12:53 PDT); the driver's default
TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --self-test --toolchain 1.99.0        # the failure modes still caught
TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --base <branch point> --bun <bun 1.4.3> --min-passed <floor> \
    --not-before "$CUT" --dry-run                                               # pre-flight + the plan, exit 0
TMPDIR=/path/to/scratch bash $G/gbase.sh --base <branch point> --bun <bun 1.4.3> --min-passed <floor> \
    --not-before "$CUT" --apply-step-backs --dates --archive-to tasks/deps-upgrade   # the run (≈ 30-60 min cold)
```

Start it at the instant or soon after: the max date is the instant's however late the run starts, while `bunfig.toml` counts from the install. Measured on 2026-10-17 from npm's records of the cut-day lock's 203 packages: a JS install before **2026-10-22T15:05:34Z**, about 19 hours after the instant, is expected to write the report's lock again (barring a yank), and a later one to be refused, naming undici 8.12.2. `--waivers <file>` names recorded DU-23 (c) waivers (none is recorded; D2 (a)'s rustls waiver never triggers — 0.23.46, published 2026-10-14T09:54:15Z, is eligible). Record the result — the exit code, the RESULT line, the step-backs (`logs/step-backs.txt`), the lock differences with their dates, the counts and the evidence archive — in `tasks/todo.md` Chunk 25 (§5 item 7); it also answers plan-rev3 Appendix B #46 (whether the pair commit followed by a plain `cargo update` resolves to `recut-lock-02-update.lock`). ← gbase README (the run, `:43`, `:45`); plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6b

**Modes.** `--dry-run`: the pre-flight (export, anchors, patches, toolchain, Bun, `--min-passed`; `--not-before`'s form, not its instant; no registry read), then the plan — every edit, every patch's files, every command with its log name, every freshness gate and Tauri-set check, every pair check, every comparison, the suite's verdict, the new `bun.lock`'s freshness check — and exit 0. The pre-flight checks every anchor exactly once — the 23 manifest lines always (20 of the re-cut's steps, 3 of step 1b), and the 20 JS anchors (19 `package.json` lines, the `vite.config.ts` import) when the run reaches the final phase, 43 in all — and each of the three patches with `git apply --check`. `--edits-only`: the export with the steps' edits and patches applied (with `--steps all`, the JS edits too), no cargo and no registry read; proved against a control that shares no code with the driver by `diff -r` (2026-10-11 on `c30e41df` and `e182f2ab`; 2026-10-12 on `05541f1b` and `d7f21b65`; 2026-10-17 on `3598a6fb`, 43 edits each; 2026-10-17 for the re-anchoring on `6d3e47fc`, 43 edits and 3 patches each, the tree differing from a plain export in 31 files). `--self-test`: toy workspaces under `$T` with no registry and no network, 42 cases (i)–(xv) plus the controls. ← gbase README *The other modes*

**Exit status.** **0** — every step and gate passed (lock differences printed; under `--strict` there were none). **1** — the first failure (a cargo command, an anchor matching 0 or 2+ times, a patch that does not apply, a lock `--locked` changed, step 6's version assertion, the freshness gate, the Tauri set, a pre-flight pin published after the max date, a broken pair, a lock comparison `lockdiff.py` could not make, the suite's verdict, the docs check's set, the new `bun.lock`'s freshness, a bun gate, a write under the real HOME's Bun or npm caches, G-GUI, or an unexpected lock difference under `--strict`); the message names the step and the log. **2** — a usage error or missing prerequisite, a run started before `--not-before` (nothing made), and `--with-stepbacks`, refused in every mode. ← gbase README *Exit status*

**Evidence.** A real run (not `--self-test`, `--dry-run` or `--edits-only`) writes `$T/evidence-<UTC date>-gbase.tar.gz` when it exits, whatever the outcome — `logs/` and `locks/` only (every log with its command line and exit code, `lock-differences.txt`, `lock-dates.txt` under `--dates`, `step-backs.txt`, `registry-cache.json`, the pre-flight's pins checks, every per-step lock), never the export or a target dir; `--archive-to tasks/deps-upgrade` puts it beside the other archives (git-ignored by `tasks/deps-upgrade/*.tar.gz`), to be indexed in `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md` with the run's result (A1-17). ← gbase README `:131`; plan-rev3 §5.1 item 6a

### 4.4 The cut-day compile probe

Run at `38649f7e` with the nineteen pre-cut fixes landed: the driver's `--edits-only` of all eight steps on `38649f7e` with `locks/recut-lock-99-final.lock`, then `cargo +1.99.0 check --offline --workspace --all-targets --exclude openalpaca_gui` — **0 errors**, so the run of record will not stop compiling this code at steps 6 or 8. Since `96de1139` step 8 writes serde-saphyr 2.0: on an export of `7fc775bc` with step 8's edits, `cargo check -p openalpaca_core --all-targets` gave 0 errors and 0 warnings under 1.93.0, and over `recut-lock-99-final.lock` moved by `cargo update -p serde-saphyr`, `check --locked -p openalpaca_core --all-targets` 0 errors under 1.99.0, its one rustc warning P03's known `fetch_update` (the gbase README's 2026-10-20 row). Beside it, at the same tip: G-base `--dry-run` green (23 anchors, its three patches apply); `cargo check -p openalpaca_gui` and the live-eval compile (`cargo check -p openalpaca_core --features live-eval --tests`) clean, run after the records commit on the same code. Evidence: `tasks/deps-upgrade/evidence-2026-10-18-precut.tar.gz` (the per-commit gate summary, every lane's red and green logs, the reviews' logs, the facts and the shared rules; git-ignored). ← plan-rev3 revision log 3.17; CLAUDE.md *Current Work* (*Gate at `38649f7e`*)

### 4.5 The wave's end-to-end gates, E1–E8

After P38, on the branch, before the merge (§4.1 for the order and the conditions every run carries).

#### E1 — CI green on the PR · end-to-end gate · verification

- **Does.** CI's existing jobs green on the draft PR (§5 item 9) — the rust job, the gui job and the windows-latest Pester job — plus the macOS shell-check leg (Q08's macOS job once it lands, G-GUI by hand until then), plus B-23's `tooling` job and Q20's `rust-macos` check, each once its commit has landed.
- **Gate.** All of those green. The Windows check the gate first named (P01a's last green run recorded before P40, or Q08's three `shell-check` jobs) is deferred under DU-33 and cannot be met; Q08's Windows and Linux legs stay deferred (*DU-33 (a) reopened* answered (a)).
- **Needs.** §5 item 9 (the branch pushed, a draft PR open); B-23; Q20; Q08 (macOS job).
- **Place.** Merged-order step 19 (§3.3).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §6 E1 (SF-17; rev 3.16: B-23 (b), the macOS CI job (b))

#### E2 — advisories · end-to-end gate · verification

- **Does.** cargo-deny and cargo-audit on the wave's lock, the advisory DB fetched fresh at the gate; `bun audit`; P34's licence policy.

No rustls id is ignored: D2 (a)'s waiver never triggers, P04 takes 0.23.46, -0346…-0349 are cleared, and -0347 never enters the lock. The list of cleared ids is rewritten from the cut-day run on a freshly fetched database; by the review's match against advisory-db `64f92cd` it reads: lopdf -0187 (P15), quick-xml -0194/-0195 (P16), rmcp -0189 (P19), rustls -0285 and -0346, -0348, -0349 (P04), quinn-proto -0338, -0340, -0343 (P04's refresh), zip 2.4.2's -0367…-0369 for our own zip (P16), RUSTSEC-2024-0449 for unsafe-libyaml (P30), and the two unsound `lru` warnings (P07). Licences: `cargo deny --locked --all-features --workspace check licenses` (and `sources` / `bans` where P34 wrote them) passing on the wave's lock, the three zip ignores it reads being the ones P34 mirrors into §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) waiver record, so cargo-deny, cargo-audit and the record name the same three.

#### E5 — a packaged build of the wave tip, which cannot pick up a stale daemon, with G-ident · end-to-end gate · verification

- **Does.** The sequence (Q02/Q03 landed) is `package-macos.sh`'s, replicated by hand: `bun run prepare:sidecar:release` (cargo's own artifact, the `.sha256` and `.provenance.json` recorded), then `bunx tauri build --bundles app --config '{"identifier":"com.tangyunpei.openalpaca-gui.wavecheck"}'` with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` **not** exported (`src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml` already sends the shell to `target/gui`; exported, the `beforeBuildCommand` hook rebuilds and re-hashes the daemon into that target dir and G-ident can end up comparing a build with itself — A2-11), then compare the bundled `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad*` and the staged copy to the recorded `.sha256` — or `package-macos.sh` itself once it passes a `--config` through. Every `bunx tauri build` here carries G-bundle's G-09 cache confinement: `BUN_INSTALL_CACHE_DIR`, `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH`, `npm_config_cache` and `npm_config_logs_dir` under the scratch root, and a closing `find -newermt` check over those directories only. (plan-rev3 §6 E5's "Without them" hand sequence is dead since Q02/Q03 landed and is kept there as rev 3.1's record only.)
  - **G-ident markers**, derived from the wave tip's lock, not literals: `rmcp-<the 3.5.x the lock holds>` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, the bundled SQLite version of the libsqlite3-sys the lock holds ≥ 1 — from the cut-day lock `rmcp-3.5.1` ≥ 1, `rmcp-0.16.0` = 0, `3.53.2` ≥ 1 (libsqlite3-sys 0.38.2, the newest release, D11).
  - **Launch** (step 4): the daemon first under launcher (i), then the app unsandboxed with the same store variables (launcher (iii) leaves the webview unable to reach its daemon — Q00), the seeded `llm.toml` with discovery off; §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) two-request proof (localhost succeeds, a public host fails, from the sandboxed shell and from a `setsid`-detached child of it) **before the first chat turn**; before any write confirm the store root in Settings → Connection and `GET /v1/status` equals `$T/store` with `$T` canonical, the `Config base dir:` line in `daemon.log`, and that no project is selected.
  - **4a. Onboarding**: one attended turn asking for `update_persona` on both documents, then confirm `BOOTSTRAP.md` is gone — before 5; a refusal during onboarding is not an attempt.
  - **5. The checks**: the first-run card; Models & keys add a key → restart → it loads; Persona ETag / memory If-Match; Stop daemon then Start daemon — the restart and the Start go through §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) confined in-app Start: G-ident first on the untouched bundle, then, only in a copy used for these legs, `Contents/MacOS/openalpacad` replaced by the wrapper beside `openalpacad.real` (whose hash is recorded), or the Start done by hand under launcher (i); after each Start, Q00's confinement proof — `sandbox_check(network-outbound)` on the new daemon's pid, a refused public request in its log, and a `ps` check that the running pid is the confined one — before any further check (a bare in-app Start spawns the daemon with full outbound network, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs:275-305`, and every model turn after it would run unconfined) (MF-4); Chat, Memory and Settings in WKWebView against a pre-wave bundle built the same way with a second throwaway identifier and G-ident's pre-wave markers; **plus the current contracts, on synthetic registered projects** (R28): project registration, re-point (`Re-point…`) and `Delete data…`; approval re-entry (`approval-card-returns.test.tsx` is the unit twin); the working state card; an upload and an artifact's Open and Reveal; the memory flows; **Stage 2** — a project `file_write`/`file_edit` with its confirmation card and the pre-edit snapshot under `sessions/<id>/snapshots/` (`pre_edit.rs:47` `check_pre_edit`, `:86` `keep_pre_edit_copy`), a projectless scratch write (`scratch/<conversation id>/`), a pointer row beside a by-product at the same address (`Results in the project` filter, no History/Diff), a missing/re-pointed project's `410 ARTIFACT_PROJECT_MISSING` on Preview/Open/Export and Reveal's refusal, and a project delete that keeps the real project file.
  - **6. Cleanup**: stop the recorded temporary daemon, wait for exit and lock release (the shell detached it, `lib.rs:362`), then `rm -rf "$T"` and the `~/Library` entries named after the throwaway identifier.
- **Gate.** The bundled daemon's hash equals the recorded `.sha256`; the markers hold; every check in 5 passes with the confinement proved after each Start.

#### E6 — the owner's real data, read-only, printing only equal or different, with rebuilt tools · end-to-end gate (run at the consent point) · verification

- **Does.** Before any tool touches a real file, each old/new pair is built (`tomlcmp` 0.9.12 ↔ 1.1.x; `tedit` 0.25.13 ↔ the version P04's lock takes; `yamlcmp` serde_yaml ↔ serde-saphyr with the P30 options) **from one sanitized source differing only in the dependency** — the archived `tedit-0.25.15` is not that (its `:114` `DIFF` diagnostic) and the archived `yamlcmp` **writes** `render-*.yaml` beside its corpus and walks every `.md` — with the serializer branch removed, the real `SKILL.md` inputs enumerated explicitly, only an index and an equality bit printed (never a value, key or path fragment), and proof on a synthetic trigger path that the inputs and their neighbours are byte-unchanged after a run (R13). It includes **typed** probes built from current source (`load_daemon_config` with `carry_over_turns`, without `episode_max_per_day`/`compaction_model`; `render_config`; the M3 `llm.toml` edit; `McpConfig`; `AgentConfigFile`; the plugin manifest). The files: `llm.toml`, `daemon.toml`, `mcp.toml` in `~/.openalpaca/config/` and in the checkout's `config/`; any other `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR`; `plugins/.permissions.toml`, `plugins/.config/*.toml`, each `plugins/*/plugin.toml`; `config/tools/*.toml`, legacy `config/agents/*.toml`; the skill directories, plugin-contributed ones included. The DU-10 count (SF-14): `secret_ref` keys at any depth, read with a TOML parser, over `llm.toml` (both copies), `plugins/.config/*.toml` and `plugins/.permissions.toml`, counts only. Its builds take the toolchain from `rust-toolchain.toml` (A2-16: typedprobe's `cargo +1.93.0` fails on a tree after P03); typedprobe is built twice, from the branch point and from the prep tree, and reports an index and ok/err per build, so the real files are loaded under toml 1.1 before any live start (F-08); the proof that a run wrote nothing hashes `plugins/` as well as `config/`, and each tool runs under a silent panic hook with `catch_unwind`, a panic reported as a `panic-*` word and never its default message (F-13); from M63 on its builds set `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (MF-5).
- **The comparators** (`tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/`, with the build recipe and the owner-side run in its `README.md`; read-only — each takes an explicit file list, walks nothing, writes nothing but `tedit record --out`, and prints only an index and a word: `equal`, `different`, `parse-error-*`; `ok`/`err`):

  | Tool | Compares | New side at the cut |
  |---|---|---|
  | `tomlcmp` | toml 0.9.12 against 1.1 | `toml11` at `version = "=1.1.8"` (`tomlcmp/Cargo.toml:17` at `2c913f99`), the toml P29 takes (1.1.8+spec-1.1.0) — landed `2c913f99` (2026-10-20; `=1.1.6` before), its lock moving toml 1.1.6 → 1.1.8 and nothing else |
  | `tedit` (`old/`, `new/`) | toml_edit 0.25.13 against 0.25.17 through the M3 edit path | `toml_edit = "=0.25.17"` (`tedit/new/Cargo.toml:17`) is already the toml_edit P04 takes (0.25.17+spec-1.1.0) |
  | `yamlcmp` | serde_yaml against serde-saphyr with P30's options | landed `2c913f99` (2026-10-20), D4 (a): `serde-saphyr = "=2.0.0"` (`yamlcmp/Cargo.toml:19` at `2c913f99`) with `non_finite_float_policy: serde_saphyr::NonFiniteFloatPolicy::AsString` in place of the deprecated `reject_non_finite_typeless_float`, and `emit_comments: false` in `yaml_options()` (`yamlcmp/src/main.rs:52-62`; C1) — with which its typed parse errors on the report's probes go from 4 to 0; its `Cargo.lock` moving serde-saphyr and granit-parser 1.3.0 → 2.0.0 and nothing else, its module doc and the README's rows rewritten |
  | `typedprobe` | the current loaders, by path | built from the branch point and from the prep tree (F-08) |
  | `keycount` | the DU-10 count of `secret_ref` at any depth and of `use_keychain = true`, by index, with toml 0.9.12 (the parser the daemon reads these files with until P29) | — (new 2026-10-17, SF-14) |

- **Gate.** Each tool prints only an index and a word; the run reports, separately, parse failures, cross-version differences, and the four shared `reparse_equal=false` round-trip defects [A08], with the typed probes' ok/err per build; the inputs and their neighbours are byte-unchanged after the run (`config/` and `plugins/` hashed); the DU-10 counts are recorded — until the count covers every keychain reference, P12's waiver of its keychain round trip needs a 0 on both the `llm.toml` count and a plugin-config count. The run still shows whether C10 matters in practice: a non-finite float in a `serde_json::Value` position of a real skill reads `different` (serde_yaml read it as null, 2.0.0 reads its canonical spelling as a string). It no longer answers C1's question — whether any real skill has 33 or more consecutive comment lines between a key and its block value: with `emit_comments: false` such a run reads `equal` (`parse-error-new` without the option, and on 1.3.0), so the run shows that such a skill reads as serde_yaml read it, not whether one exists; P30 carries the same option under D4 (a), so for the wave the question is moot, and answering it would take a build without the option (the compatibility report's §7; plan-rev3 Appendix B #3; the E6 README's *What to expect*, measured on synthetic files on 2026-10-20). None of the four repository skills has either; all four read `equal` on both facets on 2.0.0. *A note for P30's author* (the same measurement): a top-level key written twice reads `parse-error-old` on the typed facet — serde_yaml hands both entries to `SkillFrontmatter`, which refuses a duplicate field, while P30's `DuplicateKeyPolicy::LastWins` keeps the last — a file the tree refuses today and P30 would load (P30's option, not D4's; P30's *Gate*).
- **Needs.** The owner, who runs it (no agent reads `~/.openalpaca`); `yamlcmp`'s D4 (a) rebuild and `emit_comments: false`, and `tomlcmp`'s `=1.1.8` re-pin, the tool amendments before the consent point — landed as `2c913f99` on 2026-10-20 (at `5aa0f260`, when rev 1.0 was read, `yamlcmp/Cargo.toml:17` still held `serde-saphyr = "=1.3.0"`, `yamlcmp/src/main.rs:46` `reject_non_finite_typeless_float`, no `emit_comments`, and `tomlcmp/Cargo.toml:17` `=1.1.6`).
- **Place.** §5 item 3's consent point — before P04 (toml_edit 0.25.x arrives there), P12, P29 and P30; not in the end list (A2-16).
- **Record.** Comparators written 2026-10-11 (`df477c67`), built and run that day on a synthetic corpus and on the checkout's shipped files (every input `equal`, every probe `ok`). The 2026-10-17 amendments — A2-16, F-08, F-13 (a hook in every tool, `yamlcmp`'s included, each printing one fixed line), SF-14 (`keycount`), MF-5 — built in the tree and landed with plan-rev3 rev 3.14's commit; the D4 (a) and C1/C10 amendments to `yamlcmp` and the `tomlcmp` re-pin landed as `2c913f99` on 2026-10-20 (the E6 README's 2026-10-20 revision row and *What to expect*). ← plan-rev3 §6 E6; §5.1 item 3; `tasks/deps-upgrade/README.md` (`tools/e6/` row); `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/e6/README.md`

#### E8 — a rehearsal on a copy of the owner's real store, read-only, no daemon · end-to-end gate · verification

- **Does.** The only check of the owner's existing partitions, snapshots and database under SQLite 3.53.2 and sha2 0.11 on schema 4 / cache 6.

The copy holds `.master_key` and encrypted keys: keep it under `$T`, delete it afterwards.
  2. In the isolated shell: `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy`, `OPENALPACA_CONFIG_DIR=$T/realcopy/config`, `HOME=$T/home`, a scratch working directory outside any repository. **Then delete `$T/realcopy/state/discovery.json` and `$T/realcopy/state/openalpacad.lock` from the copy, or assert both are absent**: `openalpaca memory doctor` branches on `crate::manager::is_daemon_running()` → `daemon_lifecycle::running_daemon_pid()` → `read_discovery()` → `pid_is_daemon(pid)` [C: `apps/openalpaca/src/commands/memory.rs:693`, `apps/openalpaca/src/manager.rs:120-122`, `crates/openalpaca_storage/src/daemon_lifecycle.rs:122-125`], and under `OPENALPACA_HOME_STORE=$T/realcopy` that reads the copy's `discovery.json`; a graceful stop removes the file (`apps/openalpacad/src/main.rs:908`) but a forced exit does not, so a copy carrying a pid that is alive again would make steps 5–6 call `Api::connect(&at)?.get("/v1/memories/doctor")` on the **real** daemon. **Never start a daemon on the copy**: the learner's boot catch-up would call the model with the copy's real keys and write into each turn's real project root, and the copy's `in_project = 1` rows point at **real project files** which no copy isolated.
  3. **A raw read-only connection first, before any application opens the copy** (R08): `sqlite3 "file:$T/realcopy/state/openalpaca.db?mode=ro"` — in double quotes, so the shell expands `$T` — or the rebuilt probe in `mode=ro`; never with `immutable=1`, which skips the copied WAL and would undercount rows. Record `schema_version` (expect 4), every `sqlite_master` entry (tables, index definitions — `idx_file_assets_addr` on `(COALESCE(project_root, ''), in_project, rel_path)`), per-table row counts, the count of `file_assets` rows with `in_project = 1` and the count of their distinct `project_root` values (**inventory only — never stat or read those paths**; counted, not printed, F-04), and the retryable-`Error` extraction rows P15 will re-extract. Both CLIs' `memory doctor` open the **migrating** database (`memory.rs:697` → `:1737`), so this capture is the baseline, not the doctor's.
  4. **An independent inventory** (R10): `projects/*/project.json` (id, root, version), every owner directory under `memory/owners/` and `projects/*/memory/owners/`, every `state/memory-control/*.json` (partition key, bound root or project id, note count, tombstones) — the expected partition set, against which both doctors' reports are checked; a doctor that omits a snapshot whose id no folder records (`doctor.rs:168-172`; pinned by `memory_files/tests.rs:4194`) or continues after a listing error (`:154-160`) is **explained** by this inventory or the rehearsal fails.
  5. **The pre-wave CLI** (E7's binaries): `openalpaca memory doctor --global`, no `--repair`; save the report.
  6. **The bumped CLI**, same command: the same report, partition for partition.
  7. **The rebuilt current-source probe** (E8-PROBE; old/new variants from `<pre-wave>`, differing only in rusqlite/sha2): in read-only mode `PRAGMA integrity_check` and `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` (FTS5's `integrity-check` is an `INSERT` and fails on a `mode=ro` connection with "attempt to write a readonly database", while `PRAGMA integrity_check` already runs FTS5's integrity check read-only since SQLite 3.44 — so the FTS5 `INSERT` form runs only on a second scratch copy of the cache opened read-write, on **all three** tables); the same row counts as step 3 (equal); the same search over the index copy by both variants (same ids); and, standing in for the home partitions' healthy read, for every control snapshot re-derive each note file's revision hash and the partition key with both variants' `sha256_hex` and compare with the snapshot's recorded revisions, printing mismatch **counts** only, writing nothing (no `MemoryIndex::open` that would recreate a cache; no snapshot write; never `doctor --repair` on unknown state).
  8. Reopen the copy with the pre-wave variant: equal integrity results and counts.
  9. `rm -rf "$T"`; only now restart the real daemon, if wanted. Instead of deleting the copy the owner may keep it as §5 item 4's backup — moved to a 0700 directory outside any repository, its checksum list recorded — provided no daemon has started on the real store since step 1; otherwise item 4's backup is taken immediately before the first bumped start (SF-1).
- **Gate.** Steps 5 and 6: the saved output's first line is `No daemon is running; scanning locally.` (`memory.rs:696`) — a report without it came over HTTP from a daemon, and the rehearsal is void; the two reports equal partition for partition. **Home partitions will read as quarantined in both builds**: a home snapshot binds its absolute root (`snapshot.rs:283`) and the copy is at `$T/realcopy` — equal reports are relocation, not healthy reads (R09); project partitions bind by id and are readable in place in the copy. Steps 7–8: equal integrity results, counts and search ids; zero mismatch counts. **Output rule (F-04):** E8 prints what E6 prints — counts, health words and partition keys only; the doctor's full report (partition roots, note file names) and step 3's per-table output are saved under `$T`, outside the repository, and never pasted into a transcript.
- **Needs.** E7's pre-wave binaries; E8-PROBE (below); the owner (no agent reads `~/.openalpaca`).
- **Place.** Before the first start of a bumped build on the real store (§5 item 4 follows it).
- **Record.** ← plan-rev3 §6 E8 (rev 3.1: R08–R10, R12; rev 3.13: A2-17, SF-15, F-04, SF-1, MF-5)

The archived `sqlite-probe` is **not** this probe (R12: no `src/database/`, registries 43/44/45, cache 3, two FTS tables, failures printed not returned).
- **Gate.** E8 step 7's behaviour as written: read-only pragmas, the FTS5 `INSERT` only on a read-write scratch copy of the cache, counts only.
- **Needs.** —
- **Place.** Before E8 can run; a Chunk 25 item of its own.
- **Record.** Not built: the probe does not exist yet — `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/` holds only `e6/` and `gbase/` (SF-15; still so at `5aa0f260`). ← plan-rev3 §6 E8 (SF-15)

- **Load and timing.** A full suite is ≈ 5.5 min here [M-today]; measure on a quiet machine or rerun a flaky module before calling a failure a regression. ← plan-rev3 §6
- **Runner images.** The PR runs in the 2026-10-19 … 2026-11-19 window. P39 is dropped (A-15 (b)), so `ubuntu-24.04` (P01b) stays pinned through the merge and after it, and the wave never runs on 26.04's image; a move to 26.04, if one is wanted, is a hand-made commit of its own that changes the label and reads that image's Node (24.21.0, above jsdom's floor) in its own run. jsdom 30.1.2 (P31) declares `engines.node` `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26`, met by the 24.04 image's default 22.23.3, by 26.04's 24.21.0 and, locally, exactly by 24.15.0; Bun does not enforce `engines` at install, so a runner below the floor would show only at test time. ← plan-rev3 §6 *Runner images*

## 6. Files and docs that change

plan-rev3 §8 and modernization-plan §8, merged. **Method:** a file both plans cite has one row carrying both; a source row that cites several files for one change stays one row, filed under its first file — except where one of its files already has a row of its own, in which case that file is listed there too and the pointer repeats (p#8, p#15, p#19, p#85, p#98, p#100, p#101, p#106, m#1, m#17, m#18, m#25, m#34, m#35, m#36, m#37); rows are grouped by area. *Commit* gives every commit id the sources name for the file, with its status where a source records one (a SHA means landed). The last column points at the source row: `p#n` is the n-th row of plan-rev3 §8's table, `m#n` the n-th row of modernization-plan §8's (rows counted from the top of each table, header excluded). ← plan-rev3 §8; modernization-plan §8

**Line anchors.** plan-rev3 §8 cites lines at `7295f91beb6832204092f98ddc688578321eb0a0` unless a row says otherwise; several have moved by `3598a6fb` — the root `Cargo.toml`'s lines are +5 up to `tower-http` and +7 from `tokio-tungstenite` on (`865a2376` added them); rows added later are cited at `ff0e7740` (whose code is `3598a6fb`'s), `6d3e47fc` (code again `3598a6fb`'s), `476f1f81` or `2e02f684`, as each row says. modernization-plan cites code at `7295f91b` ([C]); its status line gives HEAD `3598a6fb` for rev 2.2, `ff0e7740` for rev 2.3, `6d3e47fc` for rev 2.4, `476f1f81` for rev 2.5, `38649f7e` for rev 2.6 and `2e02f684` for rev 2.7, and it names no anchor commit for its rev 2.0 rows (m#30–m#37). **Every citation is re-checked at the cut's tip, where the version-moving commits are authored.** A version in a row is the target at the cut instant. ← plan-rev3 §8 preamble; modernization-plan *Evidence tags* and status line

## 7. Keeping current afterwards

The standing rules of rev 2.1 §8 hold — monthly Dependabot with a 7-day cooldown; the weekly advisories jobs; semver-major bumps hand-planned one commit per crate with the format pin first; the toolchain moved deliberately every second or third stable, an exact x.y.z, `rust-version` with it; Bun bumped with `packageManager` and the local install together — with these changes. ← plan-rev3 §9

- **Dependabot's bun updater** is at Bun 1.4.2 and accepts `bun.lock` versions ≤ 3 (source level, since 2026-10-06) [Docs]. Under DU-13 (a) nothing watches the lock version any more; the first hosted run on the v2 lock is the one check (P35's gate). That run happens only after the merge (Dependabot reads the default branch), and it can fail a second way — the updater's bundled Bun 1.4.2 against `packageManager` 1.4.3 (P42); a fallback to (b) needs a fresh Bun 1.3.x in its own `BUN_INSTALL` root (D12 (a): recorded, and installed only when the fallback triggers). Under (b) the per-PR checklist keeps watching `lockfileVersion`. ← plan-rev3 §9 (SF-2, SF-29)
- **The Tauri pairing rule, three guards:** (1) the requirement strings in `[workspace.dependencies]` — `tauri = "~2.12"` and `tauri-build = "~2.7"` to the minor (tilde; a caret admits the next minor), `tauri-plugin-dialog = "=…"` exactly (P31a: the newest version both registries carry that is eligible — `"=2.8.3"` at the cut) — stop a lockfile-only cargo PR or a plain `cargo update` from moving a Tauri minor or a plugin patch alone; (2) Dependabot's `ignore` for `tauri*`/`@tauri-apps/*` minors and for **every** update type of `tauri-plugin-*`/`@tauri-apps/plugin-*` (DU-15 (p-i), widened; (p-ii) is withdrawn); (3) G-GUI and G-bundle on every pair commit. A Tauri minor or a plugin patch is always a hand-made commit that moves both locks (`tauri` ↔ `@tauri-apps/api` same minor; each `tauri-plugin-*` = its `@tauri-apps/plugin-*` exactly; a crate-only patch, as 2.0.3–2.0.5 were, a Rust-only commit that says so), followed by an E5-style packaged check. The Tauri CLI's own check compares nothing under Bun [M-today] — an upstream tauri-cli issue is the way out (the owner's to file). ← plan-rev3 §9
- **The per-PR checklist** (every dependency PR, Dependabot's included): (1) the data-path watch-list (P04) — read the changelog of every listed crate the PR moves; the list carries the parsers the wave added (`cff-parser`, `typed-path`, `atoi_simd`, `fast-float2`; P04, G-07) and aws-lc-sys (C9: 0.46.0 bundles AWS-LC 5.11.0, which rejects non-canonical Ed25519 public keys); (2) G-GUI by hand (or Q08's `shell-check`) when the PR moves anything the shell links — `tauri*`, `wry`, `tao`, `windows*`, `sysinfo`, and until Q06 `openalpaca_storage`'s dependencies; (3) the format-pinning tests ran and passed: P05's AES fixture, the `sha256_hex("abc")` and the UUID partition-key literal, the owner-key and folder-suffix pins, the newer-than-build refusal (`database/tests.rs:68`), the serde-saphyr pin (P30), P14's characterisations, Q01's discovery byte pin — a failing pin is a format change, never a test to update in passing; (4) the Tauri pairing (above); (5) a packaged build gets G-ident — bundled == staged `.sha256` == `libexec/` (Q02/Q03), markers from the lock; (6) a `bun.lock` PR keeps the committed `lockfileVersion` (DU-13); (7) a hand-made lock move passes the freshness gate (§1) and its body names the new transitive majors it pulls in — for a JS lock too: C5 (a), the cut instant governing every `bun.lock` entry, transitive and platform packages included, `bunfig.toml`'s `minimumReleaseAge` counting from the install, so a `bun.lock` written for an instant other than its install's runs §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) `bun.lock` check against that instant's max date; (8) the licence check: P34's `[licenses]` (B-22 (b)) refuses a crate whose licence it does not name, which the PR allows by name with its reason or answers by refusing the crate, and B-22 (notices) regenerates the notices at the next package. ← plan-rev3 §9
- **Freshness.** A hand-planned bump takes the newest release ≥ 7 days old in both ecosystems. Until the pin reaches Cargo 1.100, `tasks/deps-upgrade/tools/gbase/lockdates.py --gate --max-date <UTC>` is the cargo half of the rule (§1). **Cargo 1.100 (2026-11-12) stabilises `registry.global-min-publish-age`** — the resolver form of this rule; adopt it in `.cargo/config.toml` once the pin is ≥ 1.100 (then P04-style `--precise` step-backs are no longer hand work); Cargo 1.100 also changes the intermediate build-directory layout (Q02 reads cargo's JSON messages, so it is unaffected; anything else reading `target/<profile>` is) and makes `cargo install --locked` a no-op [Docs: the Cargo Book changelog]; the Cargo 1.100 claims were not re-read by the 2026-10-17 review (the Cargo Book changelog has moved) [NE]. A step-back is taken only for a release younger than 7 days, never to keep a measured older version (DU-25 (v4) took rmcp 3.5.1 on this rule), and the rule binds Tauri's crates too — no hold except DU-23's freshness step-back (MF-1). `build.build-dir` (stable since 1.91) is adopted by modernization-plan M63, with the shell's build-dir equal to its target-dir because tauri-build resolves its sidecar copy from `OUT_DIR`; from M63 on every build that sets `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` sets `CARGO_BUILD_BUILD_DIR` beside it (§1), or the build-dir's tree in `~/.cargo` grows where deleting a scratch root never reaches (MF-5); B-23's guard checks that pairing in the workflows from M63 on, the rule written by B-23 or by M63 (B-23's entry). ← plan-rev3 §9 (MF-1, MF-2, MF-5)
- **The weekly advisories run** (P34): a red run on an unchanged lock is a new advisory — a lock refresh, a bump, or a reasoned `ignore` (`deny.toml`) / `--ignore=<CVE>` (`bun audit`). The only ignores are zip2's three `extract()`-path advisories against calamine's zip 8.6.0 — RUSTSEC-2026-0367, -0368, -0369 in `deny.toml` and cargo-audit's configuration (P34), live since RustSec's 2026-10-16 import — lifted when calamine requires `zip ^9`; they are mirrored into §1's (plan-rev3 §5.2) waiver record, and B-23's guard fails when the lock holds no zip 8 while they remain, so their lift is checked rather than remembered (no rustls ignore: D2 (a), C6). A licence finding belongs to the PR that moved the lock (the checklist's (8)), the weekly run's lock being unchanged. Once a year, read the transitive unmaintained list (4 warnings after the wave). ← plan-rev3 §9 (SF-7; B-22 (b), B-23 (b))
- **The Rust toolchain:** the procedure is P03's gate (clippy and fmt counts by the stated method against the previous toolchain, the full suite, G-GUI, the future-incompat list; the lock refresh after it). DU-23 governs a toolchain as it governs a crate (D3 (a)): a Rust release is eligible 7 days after the later of its stable channel manifest (`static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-<x.y.z>.toml`) and its GitHub release — for 1.99.1, 2026-10-15T17:39:23Z and 2026-10-16T04:47:44Z, so from 2026-10-23T04:47:44Z, which is P03b; DU-2's "take an x.y.1" is read inside that rule, never ahead of it. The freshness gate reads crates.io only, so the check is made by hand and recorded in the commit's `Verified:` line (§1). 1.100.0 is eligible from 2026-11-19 — that date assumes both of its publication times fall on 2026-11-12; the same rule re-derives it from the two times once they exist. ← plan-rev3 §9 (SF-8; D3 (a))
- **Bun and Node:** `packageManager` bumped with the local Bun (exactly that version); 1.4.3 is taken at the cut (P42, its own one-line commit with the four gates re-run). Node stays with the runner image until a floor is threatened (22.23.3 default on 24.04, 24.21.0 on 26.04); the floor nearest to being threatened is jsdom 30.1.2's `engines.node`, `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26` (§4.6). The runner labels stay pinned — P39 is dropped (A-15 (b)) — so the image's Node changes only with a hand-made label commit, which reads it then. ← plan-rev3 §9
- **Part II's own upkeep:** after any architecture step the shell's dependency baseline is recaptured before the next dependency comparison; `openalpaca_app_support` joins the guard audit; the `shell-check` jobs are the standing G-GUI; `native-smoke`'s labelled gaps are re-read when a platform tool (WebDriver on macOS) improves; from Q06 on Q08's G1 guard script runs on every macOS job and by hand, and Q19's budgets are re-set from each recapture, never loosened in passing (A-22, A-28); Q20's `rust-macos` check is the standing macOS compile of the CI set; B-25's edge table changes with an edge, in the same commit; Q18a's minos check runs on every package; and when an MCP server or a CLI backend fails to resolve in the app, Q21's `daemon.log` line says where its `PATH` came from. ← plan-rev3 §9
- **Owner settings, once:** Dependabot alerts on; local Bun at the pinned version. ← plan-rev3 §9
